Parameter dependent rough SDEs with applications to rough PDEs
Fabio Bugini, Peter K. Friz, Wilhelm Stannat

Abstract
Rough stochastic differential equations (rough SDEs), recently introduced by Friz, Hocquet and Lê in [ FHL21 ] have emerged as a versatile tool to study “doubly” SDEs under partial conditioning (with motivation from pathwise filtering and control, volatility modelling in finance and mean-field stochastic dynamics with common noise …). While the full dynamics may be highly non-Markovian, the conditional dynamics often are. In natural (and even linear) situations, the resulting stochastic PDEs can be beyond existing technology. The present work then tackles a key problem in this context, which is the well-posedness of regular solution to the rough Kolmogorov backward equation. To this end, we study parameter dependent RSDE in sense of ℒ \mathscr{L} - differentiability (as in Krylov, 2008). In companion works ( [ BFLZ25 ] , [ BFS25 ] ), we will show how this removes dimension-dependent regularity assumptions for well-posedness of the Zakai, Kushner–Stratonovich and nonlinear Fokker–Planck stochastic equations.



Keywords.
Rough paths, rough stochastic differential equations, rough partial differential equations, Feynman–Kac formula.

MSC (2020).
60H10, 60H15, 60L20, 60L50.

1
Introduction

Consider the following parameter-dependent stochastic equation in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

dX^{\zeta}_{t}=b_{t}(\zeta,X^{\zeta}_{t})dt+\sigma_{t}(\zeta,X_{t}^{\zeta})\ dB_{t}+\beta_{t}(\zeta,X_{t}^{\zeta})\ d\mathbf{W}_{t}\qquad t\in[0,T],

(1)

where
\zeta
denotes a parameter in a certain parameter space
U
, typically endowed with a Banach space structure.
Here
B=(B_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
is an
m
-dimensional
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t}
-Brownian motion, given on a complete filtered probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
, and
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W})
is a deterministic
\alpha
-Hölder rough path in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2}]
.
The coefficients of the equation are progressively measurable mappings

\displaystyle b:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad\sigma:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times m},\quad\beta:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}.

At this stage (
1
) is completely formal, and it is called a rough stochastic differential equation (rough SDE or RSDE).
In the first part of this work we introduce and develop a concept of continuity and differentiability of the solution
X
with respect to the parameter
\zeta
in the spirit Krylov’s treatise of parameter-dependent SDEs (cf.
[
Kry08
, Section 1.2.8]
) and allows us to deduce some continuity and differentiability properties of the function

U\ni\zeta\longmapsto J(\zeta):=\mathbb{E}(\phi(X^{\zeta}))\in\mathbb{R}.

(2)

Here
\phi(\cdot)
denotes some observable of
X^{\zeta}
; typical examples are a final time evaluation
\phi(X^{\zeta})=g(X^{\zeta}_{T})
for some
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, or some integral functional of the form
\phi(X^{\zeta})=\int_{0}^{T}\mu(X^{\zeta}_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{T}\nu(X_{r}^{\zeta})d\mathbf{W}_{r}

for some
\mu:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\nu:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{1\times n}
.

The second main goal of this paper is to provide an existence-and-uniqueness theory for a certain class of linear rough PDEs, by proving a rough path version of the well-known Feynman–Kac formula.
We consider the following purely deterministic terminal value problem

\begin{cases}-\partial_{t}u(t,x)&=L_{t}u_{t}(x)dt+\Gamma_{t}u_{t}(x)d\mathbf{W}_{t}\qquad t\in[0,T],\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\\
u(T,\cdot)&=g\end{cases},

(3)

where
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W})
is an
\alpha
-Hölder geometric rough path in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2}]
, and
\ g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
.
The solution is a map
u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, and
L_{t},\Gamma_{t}
are the time-dependent (second and first order, respectively) differential operators defined by

\displaystyle L_{t}u:=\frac{1}{2}\text{tr}\left(\sigma(t,\cdot)\sigma(t,\cdot)^{\top}D_{xx}^{2}u\right)+b(t,\cdot)\cdot D_{x}u+c(t,\cdot)u\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Gamma_{t}u:=\beta(t,\cdot)\cdot D_{x}u+\gamma(t,\cdot)u.

Here
\sigma=(\sigma^{1},\dots,\sigma^{m}),\ \beta_{t}=(\beta^{1},\dots,\beta^{n})
and
b
are time-dependent (deterministic) vector fields over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, while
c
and
\gamma=(\gamma^{1},\dots,\gamma^{n})
are time-dependent scalar functions.
In the smooth case (i.e. when
d\mathbf{W}_{t}
is replaced by
\dot{W}_{t}dt
), a standard Itô calculus result states that any bounded
C^{1,2}
solution to (
3
) is given by the Feynman–Kac formula

u(t,x):=\mathbb{E}\Big(g(X_{T}^{t,x})\exp\Big\{\int_{t}^{T}c_{r}(X_{r}^{t,x})dr+\int_{t}^{T}\gamma_{r}(X_{r}^{t,x})\dot{W}_{r}dr\Big\}\Big),

(4)

being
X^{t,x}
the solution to the following SDE:

dX^{t,x}_{s}=b(s,X^{t,x}_{s})ds+\sigma(s,X^{t,x}_{s})dB_{s}+\beta(s,X^{t,x}_{s})\dot{W}_{s}ds\quad s\in[t,T],\quad X^{t,x}_{t}=x,

(5)

for any given Brownian motion
B
.
Notice how the functional defined in (
4
) is an example of the one we introduced in (
2
), where initial time and initial state play the role of parameters.
For a proof of the Feynman–Kac formula in the smooth case see
[
Kry08
, Theorem 10]
, which also shows how parameter-dependence results for SDEs are applied.
One of the key ingredients is indeed the understanding of the continuity of

(t,x)\longmapsto\mathbb{E}\Big(g(X_{T}^{t,x})\exp\Big\{\int_{t}^{T}c_{r}(X_{r}^{t,x})dr+\int_{t}^{T}\gamma_{r}(X_{r}^{t,x})\dot{W}_{r}dr\Big\}\Big),

and of the differentiability of the previous map with respect to
x
, for fixed
t
.
In our work, we generalize this classical result to the rough case (that is, when
W
is no longer time-differentiable and we lift it to a rough path).
Equation (
5
) becomes a rough SDE and the integral with respect to
W
in the exponential term in (
4
) becomes a rough stochastic integral. In this sense, one can appreciate how well-posedness of rough PDEs via a rough Feynman–Kac formula constitutes an application of parameter-dependent rough SDEs theory.
Understanding integrability such as

\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\int_{t}^{T}\gamma(X_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}}\right)<+\infty

when
\mathbf{W}
is a genuine rough path is a non-trivial task. In previous approaches (cf.
[
DOR15
,
DFS17
]
) this was achieved with Gaussian isoperimetry methods, not applicable in the present generality. Instead we employ John-Nirenberg type inequalities
[
Lê22
]
, perfectly adapted to our hybrid rough martingale approach, see
Section

4.1
.

What are rough SDEs?

Rough SDEs are simultaneous generalizations of Itô’s SDEs and Lyons’ rough differential equations (RDEs).
First introduced in
[
FHL21
]
, they unify classical stochastic calculus and rough path theory within a single framework. This gives intrinsic meaning to continuous adapted solutions
X=X^{\mathbf{W}}(\omega)
to equations simultaneously driven by a Brownian motion
B
and a deterministic rough path
\mathbf{W}
, of the form

dX_{t}=b_{t}(X_{t})\,dt+\sigma_{t}(X_{t})\,dB_{t}+(\beta_{t},\beta^{\prime}_{t})(X_{t})\,d\mathbf{W}_{t}.

Coefficients can be random in the sense of progressive measurability.
As is typical for well-posedness in rough path theory,
\beta
needs to have
C^{2+}
space regularity and satisfy a Taylor-like expansion of the form
\beta_{t}(\cdot)-\beta_{s}(\cdot)\approx\beta^{\prime}_{s}(\cdot)(W_{t}-W_{s})
.
In contrast,
b
and
\sigma
are only required to be progressively measurable in
(t,\omega)
and Lipschitz continuous in space, as is standard in Itô theory to have well-posedness.
In
[
FHL21
]
, all the coefficients are assumed to be globally bounded, as is common in rough path theory. The linear (unbounded) case is treated in
[
BCN24
, Section 3]
, also using stochastic sewing tools
[
Lê20
]
.
A brief yet rich and self-contained introduction to rough SDEs can be found below in
Section

2
. Examples of rough SDEs can be found in the discussion below; for a more general overview of the theory and its motivating applications we refer to
[
FHL21
]
.

Why rough PDEs?
Rough PDEs of the form (
3
) arise in a variety of situations.
A selection of these is presented below to highlight how our work can serve as a key ingredient, as well as to illustrate the versatility and applicability of the theory of (parameter-dependent) rough SDEs.
In all the examples below
B
and
B^{\perp}
are independent Brownian motions on a given filtered probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
.

The class of regular backward equations we are dealing with serves as a key tool for establishing uniqueness results for both linear and nonlinear measure-valued Fokker–Planck equations by duality methods.
Building on the approaches in
[
CP24
]
and
[
CG19
]
, our work prepares a systematic way to get rid of dimension-dependent regularity assumptions used in those papers, due to their use of backward stochastic PDEs (BSPDEs) and Sobolev embeddings. More precisely:

1. In stochastic filtering (see, for instance,
[
CP24
]
for a general overview) one is interested in estimating
\varsigma_{t}(\varphi):=\mathbb{E}(\varphi(X_{t})\mid Y_{r},\ 0\leq r\leq t)
for a sufficiently large class of test functions
\varphi
, where

\displaystyle dX_{t}

\displaystyle=b_{t}(X_{t})\,dt+\sigma_{t}(X_{t})\,dB_{t}+\beta_{t}(X_{t})\,dY_{t}\quad t\in[0,T]

Observation

\displaystyle dY_{t}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{t}(X_{t})\,dt+dB^{\perp}_{t}.

Signal

By Girsanov’s theorem, the process
Y
is a Brownian motion under a new measure
d\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\mid_{\mathcal{F}_{t}}=e^{I_{t}}\,d\mathbb{P}\mid_{\mathcal{F}_{t}}
with
I_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\gamma_{t}(X_{t})^{T}\,dY_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}|\gamma_{t}(X_{t})|^{2}\,dt
. In particular, it admits an (Itô) rough path lift
\mathbf{Y}(\omega):=(Y(\omega),(\int\int dYdY)(\omega))
.
From the Kallianpur–Striebel formula (see e.g.
[
CP24
, Proposition 3.5]
),

\varsigma_{t}(\varphi)(\omega)=\frac{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\varphi(X_{t})e^{I_{t}}\mid Y_{r},\ 0\leq r\leq T)(\omega)}{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(e^{I_{t}}\mid Y_{r},\ 0\leq r\leq T)(\omega)}=:\frac{\pi_{t}(\varphi)(\omega)}{\pi_{t}(1)(\omega)}\qquad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}(\mathbb{P})$-a.s.}

and one can show that
\pi_{t}
is a measure-valued solution to the so-called Zakai stochastic PDE. One of the focuses of
[
CP24
]
is in showing uniqueness for that equation, via duality arguments involving a certain class of BSPDEs, seemingly unavoidable if observation coefficients depend more generally on
(X_{t},Y_{t})
.
The adoption of a rough path perspective on the stochastic filtering problem is the focus of
[
BFLZ25
]
; we briefly outline the main ideas here. For any given rough path
\mathbf{W}
, one can define

\pi^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}(\varphi):=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\varphi(X^{\mathbf{W}}_{t})e^{I^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}})\qquad t\in[0,T],

where
dX^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}=b_{t}(X^{\mathbf{W}}_{t})\,dt+\sigma_{t}(X^{\mathbf{W}}_{t})\,dB_{t}+\beta_{t}(X^{\mathbf{W}}_{t})\,d\mathbf{W}_{t}
is a rough SDE and
I^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\gamma_{t}(X^{\mathbf{W}}_{t})^{T}\,d\mathbf{W}_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}|\gamma_{t}(X^{\mathbf{W}}_{t})|^{2}\,dt
. From
[
DFS17
, Lemma 3.1]
, one has that
\pi^{\mathbf{W}}
solves a measure-valued rough PDE of the form

d\pi^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}(\varphi)=\pi^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}(L_{t}\varphi)\,dt+\pi^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}(\Gamma_{t}\varphi)\,d\mathbf{W}_{t}\quad t\in[0,T],\quad\pi^{\mathbf{W}}_{0}=\text{Law}(X^{\mathbf{W}}_{0})

(6)

where
L_{t}\varphi=\frac{1}{2}tr(\sigma_{t}\sigma^{T}_{t}D^{2}_{xx}\varphi)+b_{t}D_{x}\varphi-\frac{1}{2}|\gamma_{t}|^{2}\varphi
and
\Gamma_{t}\varphi=\beta_{t}D_{x}\varphi+\gamma_{t}\varphi
.
By a duality argument (see
[
DFS17
, Lemma 4.15]
for a rough PDEs version), uniqueness for the forward equation (
6
) follows from the existence of a solution
u^{\mathbf{W}}
to the backward equation (
3
).
Within the framework of our paper, one appreciates how uniqueness for (
6
) therefore holds under assumptions that are not dimension-dependent. (The rough path approach works immediately with observation coefficients depending on
(X_{t},Y_{t})
.)
Going back to the original filtering problem, one can perform a randomisation argument as explained in
[
FLZ25
]
to conclude that

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\varphi(X_{t})e^{I_{t}}\mid Y_{r},\ 0\leq r\leq T)(\omega)=\pi^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}(\varphi)\big|_{\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{Y}(\omega)}\qquad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}(\mathbb{P})$-a.s.}

2. Consider a McKean–Vlasov SDE with common noise of the form

\begin{cases}dX_{t}=b_{t}(X_{t},\mu_{t})\,dt+\sigma_{t}(X_{t},\mu_{t})\,dB_{t}+\beta_{t}(X_{t},\mu_{t})\,dB^{\perp}_{t}\quad t\in[0,T]\\
\mu_{t}(\omega)=\mathbb{P}(X_{t}\in\cdot\mid B^{\perp}_{r},\ 0\leq r\leq t)(\omega)\end{cases},

where
\mu
solves the following nonlinear measure-valued Fokker–Planck stochastic PDE (see, for instance,
[
CD18
, p. 110]
):

d\mu_{t}(\varphi)(\omega)=\mu_{t}(L_{t}[\mu_{t}]\varphi)(\omega)\,dt+\mu_{t}(\Gamma_{t}[\mu_{t}]\varphi)(\omega)\,dB^{\perp}_{t}(\omega)\qquad t\in[0,T],

(7)

with
L_{t}[\mu]\varphi=\frac{1}{2}tr((\sigma_{t}\sigma^{T}_{t})(\cdot,\mu)D^{2}_{xx}\varphi)+b_{t}(\cdot,\mu)D_{x}\varphi
and
\Gamma_{t}[\mu]\varphi=\beta_{t}(\cdot,\mu)D_{x}\varphi
.
In
[
CG19
]
the authors prove uniqueness of (
7
) via a duality argument and BSPDEs, under dimension-dependent regularity assumptions, which are due to some Sobolev embeddings. The adoption of a rough path point of view towards nonlinear Fokker–Planck equations is the object of
[
BFS25
]
; we briefly outline here the main ideas. Given any rough path
\mathbf{W}
, one can define

\mu_{t}^{\mathbf{W}}(\varphi):=\mathbb{E}(\varphi(X_{t}^{\mathbf{W}}))\qquad t\in[0,T],

where

dX^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}=b_{t}(X^{\mathbf{W}}_{t},\mu^{\mathbf{W}}_{t})\,dt+\sigma_{t}(X^{\mathbf{W}}_{t},\mu^{\mathbf{W}}_{t})\,dB_{t}+f_{t}(X^{\mathbf{W}}_{t},\mu^{\mathbf{W}}_{t})\,d\mathbf{W}_{t},\ \mu^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}=\mathbb{P}(X^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}\in\cdot\ )

is a McKean–Vlasov SDE with rough common noise. A solution theory for the previous class of rough SDEs can be found in
[
FHL25
]
; while
[
BFHL25
]
contains some propagation of chaos results.
In
[
CN21
]
the authors have shown that, if
\beta_{t}(x,\cdot)
is linear on the space of probability measures, then
\mu^{\mathbf{W}}
is the (unique) solution to the following nonlinear Fokker–Planck rough PDE:

d\mu^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}(\varphi)=\mu^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}(L_{t}[\mu_{t}^{\mathbf{W}}]\varphi)\,dt+\mu^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}(\Gamma_{t}[\mu_{t}^{\mathbf{W}}]\varphi)\,d\mathbf{W}_{t}\quad t\in[0,T].

Their argument relies on the well-posedness of the linearized (i.e. without measure-dependent coefficients) equation.
Within the framework of our paper and in a similar fashion to what we discussed in 1., it is possible to obtain a similar well-posedness result under more general assumptions on
\beta
, provided that existence for equation (
3
) is known. Upon randomisation (see
[
FLZ25
]
), one also notes that

\mathbb{P}(X_{t}\in\cdot\ )(\omega)=\mu_{t}^{\mathbf{W}}\big|_{\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{B}^{\perp}(\omega)}\qquad\text{$\mathbb{P}$-a.s.}

being
\mathbf{B}^{\perp}(\omega)
the (Itô) rough path lift of the common noise
B^{\perp}
.

3. Interestingly, there is a third use of rough SDEs/PDEs in the context of conditional option pricing, offering an intrinsic description of the rough PDEs of

[
BBFP25
]
(treated therein via joint lift of Brownian motion and rough path). Specifically, it is seen that PDEs of the form
(
3
) can be useful in pricing options for local stochastic volatility models of the form

dY_{t}(\omega)=l(t,Y_{t}(\omega))v_{t}(\omega)\left(\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\,dB_{t}(\omega)+\rho\,d{B}^{\perp}_{t}(\omega)\right)\in\mathbb{R}\quad t\in[0,T]

for some “leverage” function
l
, stochastic volatility process
v
, measurable only with respect to
B^{\perp}
, and correlation parameter
\rho\in(-1,1)
. No Markovian structure of
(Y,v)
is assumed, thereby also covering popular “rough” (local) volatility models.

The interest is then in computing the time-
T
payoff
\psi
of
Y
conditional on
B^{\perp}
, that is

u(t,y,\omega):=\mathbb{E}^{t,y}\big(\psi(Y_{T})\mid{B}^{\perp}_{r},\ 0\leq r\leq T\big)(\omega)

with
\mathbb{P}^{t,y}(Y_{t}=y)=1
.
Rough path meaning
\mathbf{M}
is given to
M_{t}(\omega):=(\int_{0}^{t}v_{r}\,dB^{\perp}_{r})(\omega)
, so that

u(t,y,\omega)=\mathbb{E}^{t,y}(\psi(Y^{\mathbf{W}}_{T}))\big|_{\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{M}_{\cdot}(\omega)}=:u^{\mathbf{W}}(t,y)\big|_{\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{M}_{\cdot}(\omega)}\qquad\text{$\mathbb{P}$-a.s.}

where
dY^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}(\omega)=l(t,Y^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}(\omega))(v^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\,dB_{t}(\omega)+\rho\,d\mathbf{W}_{t})
can be seen as an instance of a rough SDE.
Here
\mathbf{v}^{\mathbf{W}}_{t}
is the square-root of the derivative of
[\mathbf{M}]
, which coincides with
v_{t}(\omega)
upon randomization
\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{M}_{\cdot}(\omega)
.
Within the framework of our paper, one can characterize

u^{\mathbf{W}}
as the unique solution to (
3
) with
L_{t}=\frac{1}{2}(l(t,\cdot)\mathbf{v}^{\mathbf{W}}_{t})^{2}(1-\rho^{2})D^{2}_{xx}
and
\Gamma_{t}=l(t,\cdot)\rho D_{x}
. Remark that the corresponding stochastic PDE terminal value problem, obtained from (
3
) by (local martingale) randomization, is to the best of the authors’ knowledge, impossible to treat with classical methods (since
M
run backwards, i.e.
t\mapsto M_{T-t}
, may not even be a semimartingale). The situation is actually quite similar in the filtering framework of
[
CP24
]
, discussed above, forcing the authors to resort to BSPDE methods.

Previously on rough PDEs.

Deriving a rough Feynman–Kac formula to give a functional integral representation of solutions to rough PDEs was one of the main objectives of
[
DFS17
]
.

Section

4
of our paper offers
some important improvements concerning regular solutions for backward rough PDEs. Building on the
framework of

[
FHL21
]
,

•

we decrease the needed space-regularity of the coefficients in the equation. Specifically,
[
DFS17
, Theorem 9]
, required
\sigma,\beta,\gamma\in C^{6}_{b}
and
b,c,g\in C^{4}_{b}
to prove regular well-posedness of (
3
). In
4.9
below, those assumptions are significantly reduced (especially on the diffusion coefficient
\sigma
) to
\beta,\gamma\in C^{5}_{b}
and
b,\sigma,c,g\in C^{3}_{b}
;

•

we allow (rough, controlled) time-dependence for the coefficients of (
3
). Time continuity is a sufficient assumption for our well-posedness result, improving
[
BBFP25
]
where the time variable is essentially treated as the space variable;

•

we give an intrinsic meaning to the representation for the solution (in contrast to previous works which approximate the rough driver of the equation via a sequence of smooth (rough) paths); we note that such intrinsic understanding is key to analyze numerical solution methods (cf.
[
BBFP25
]
).

•

we give a direct and self-contained (‘‘rough Itô calculus’’) proof for uniqueness
1
1

1

We could have used a duality argument, which however does not lead to different assumption of
4.12
below.

of (
3
), advertizing the convenience of “rough stochastic calculus”.

Content of the paper.

Section

2
recalls the main definitions and results on rough SDEs, including the general theory from
[
FHL21
]
and the linear case from
[
BCN24
, Section 3]
.

Section

3
focuses on parameter-dependent rough SDEs:
Section

3.1
establishes a continuity result with respect to parameters;
Section

3.2
and
Section

3.3
address linear rough SDEs, proving continuity and differentiability results;
Section

3.4
applies these to prove differentiability of solutions to rough SDEs with respect to a parameter.

Section

3.5
collects key technical results on continuity and differentiability under composition and integration, essential for the analysis of (linear and nonlinear) rough SDEs.

Section

4
turns to rough PDEs, proving existence and uniqueness via a rough Feynman–Kac formula, with supporting auxiliary results on exponential estimates for rough SDEs and functional representations of solutions to rough SDEs in
Section

4.1
and
Section

4.2
, respectively.

Frequently used notation.
We write
a\lesssim b
to express that there exists a non-negative constant
C\geq 0
such that
a\leq Cb
. If such a constant depends on some parameters
\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{N}
we write
a\lesssim_{\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{N}}b
.
We denote by
\mathcal{L}(V;\bar{V})
the space of linear and continuous functions from
V
to
\bar{V}
Banach spaces.
The latter is a Banach space, if endowed with the norm
|T|_{\mathcal{L}}:=\sup_{x\in V,|x|_{V}\leq 1}|Tx|_{\bar{V}}
.
We identify
\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{m};\mathbb{R}^{n})
and the tensor product
\mathbb{R}^{n}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{m}
with the matrix space
\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
.
The following general identifications also hold:
\mathcal{L}(V\otimes\bar{V};\tilde{V})\equiv\mathcal{L}(V;\mathscr{L}(\bar{V};\tilde{V}))\equiv\mathcal{L}^{2}(V\times\bar{V};\tilde{V}),

where
\mathcal{L}^{2}(V\times\bar{V};\tilde{V})
denotes the space of bilinear and continuous maps from
V\times\bar{V}
to
\tilde{V}
.
We denote by
\mathcal{B}(V;\bar{V})
the space of bounded functions from
V
to
\bar{V}
and for any
F\in\mathcal{B}(V;\bar{V})
,
|F|_{\infty}:=\sup_{x\in V}|F(x)|_{\bar{V}}
.
We denote by
DF:V\to\mathcal{L}(V,\bar{V})
the first Fréchet derivative of
F
. If not otherwise specified, in this paper we always refer to differentiability in Fréchet sense simply as differentiability.
Given
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, we denote by
{C}^{n}_{b}(V;\bar{V})
the space of bounded and continuous functions from
V
to
\bar{V}
which are
n
-times continuously differentiable with bounded derivatives.
The space
{C}^{n}_{b}(V;\bar{V})
is a Banach space if endowed with the norm
|F|_{{C}_{b}^{n}}:=|F|_{\infty}+|DF|_{\infty}+\dots+|D^{n}F|_{\infty}
,
where
D^{n}F
denotes the
n
-th derivative of
F
.
Any function
F\in{C}^{n}_{b}(V,\bar{V})
satisfies the following - often referred as mean value theorem - for any
x,h\in V
:

F(x+h)-F(x)-\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\frac{1}{k!}D^{k}F(x)h^{\otimes k}=\frac{1}{(n-1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\theta)^{n-1}D^{n}F(x+\theta h)d\theta\ h^{\otimes n}.

Given
\alpha\in(0,1]
, we define the
\alpha
-Hölder seminorm of
F
as

[F]_{\alpha}:=\sup_{x,y\in V,x\neq y}\frac{|F(x)-F(y)|_{\bar{V}}}{|x-y|_{V}^{\alpha}}.

If
[F]_{\alpha}<+\infty
, we say that
F
belongs to
C^{\alpha}(V;\bar{V})
. In case
\alpha=1
, we say that
F
belongs to
Lip(V;\bar{V})
.
The space
C^{n+\alpha}_{b}(V;\bar{V})
is the space of
C^{n}_{b}
-functions such that
D^{n}F\in C^{\alpha}
, and we write
|F|_{C^{n}_{b}}:=|F|_{C^{n}_{b}}+[D^{n}F]_{\alpha}
.

Given a probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
and an integrability exponent
p\in[1,\infty)
, we denote by
\|\cdot\|_{p;V}
- or simply by
\|\cdot\|_{p}
, if clear from the context - the usual norm on the Lebesgue space
L^{p}(\Omega;V)
of
V
-valued random variables. The norm
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
denotes the essential supremum norm.
A
V
-valued stochastic process
X=(X_{t})_{t}
is said to be
L^{p}
-integrable if any
X_{t}
belongs to
L^{p}(\Omega;V)
. If
V=\mathbb{R}
, we sometimes write
L^{p}(\Omega)
instead of
L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R})
. For a given sub-
\sigma
-field
\mathcal{G}\subseteq\mathcal{F}
, the space
L^{p}(\Omega,\mathcal{G};V)
is the space of
\mathcal{G}
-measurable random variables in
L^{p}(\Omega;V)
.

For any
T>0
we define
\Delta_{[0,T]}:=\{(s,t)\in[0,T]^{2}\ |\ s\leq t\}
.
For any Banach space valued path
[0,T]\ni t\mapsto X_{t}\in E
, we define its increment as the two parameter function

\delta X_{s,t}:=X_{t}-X_{s}
for
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
.
Similarly, given a two-parameter function
A:\Delta_{[0,T]}\to E
,
(s,t)\mapsto A_{s,t}
, we define
\delta A_{s,u,t}:=A_{s,t}-A_{s,u}-A_{u,t}
, for
s\leq u\leq t
.

2
A compendium on rough SDEs

In this section we recall the main definitions and results on rough stochastic analysis and rough SDEs contained in
[
FHL21
]
and
[
BCN24
, Section 3]
.
Even if the reader is familiar with the topic, we recommend giving a look to
2.1
(for the progressive measurability and integrability assumptions we add on stochastic controlled rough paths), to
2.3
(for a slightly generalized definition of stochastic controlled vector field), and to
2.17
(since interpolation inequalities in Hölder spaces are frequently used in the paper).

Let
T>0
and let
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
be a complete filtered probability space.
Let
p\in[2,\infty)
and
\ q\in[p,\infty]
, and let
V,\bar{V},\tilde{V}
be finite-dimensional Banach spaces.
Let
\mathcal{G}\subseteq\mathcal{F}
be a sub-
\sigma
-field.
Given a
V
-valued random variable
\xi
, we define - if it exists - its conditional
L^{p}
-norm with respect to
\mathcal{G}
as the (unique)
\mathcal{G}
-measurable
\mathbb{R}
-valued random variable given by

\|\xi|\mathcal{G}\|_{p;V}:=\mathbb{E}\left(|\xi|_{V}^{p}|\mathcal{G}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

We often abbreviate the notation as
\|\xi|\mathcal{G}\|_{p}
.
In particular, the mixed
L_{p,q}
-norm
\|\|\xi|\mathcal{G}\|_{p}\|_{q}
denotes the
L^{q}(\Omega)
-norm of
\|\xi|\mathcal{G}\|_{p}
.
We denote by
\mathbb{E}_{t}(\cdot)=\mathbb{E}(\,\cdot\mid\mathcal{F}_{t})
for any
t\ in[0,T]
.
Let
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in[0,1]
and let
W\in{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
be an
\alpha
-Hölder continuous deterministic path, for some
\alpha\in(0,1]
.

Definition 2.1
.

(see
[
FHL21
, Definition 3.1]
)
A pair
(X,X^{\prime})
is said to be a
stochastic controlled rough path
of
(p,q)
-integrability and
(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})
-Hölder regularity with values in
V
if

a)

X=(X_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
is a progressively measurable
V
-valued stochastic processes such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{t}\|_{p}<+\infty\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\delta X\|_{\gamma;p,q}:=\sup_{0\leq s<t\leq T}\frac{\|\|\delta X_{s,t}|\mathcal{F}_{s}\|_{p}\|_{q}}{|t-s|^{\gamma}}<+\infty;

b)

X^{\prime}=(X^{\prime}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
is a progressively measurable
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n},V)
-valued stochastic processes such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X^{\prime}_{t}\|_{q}<+\infty\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\delta X^{\prime}\|_{\gamma^{\prime};p,q}:=\sup_{0\leq s<t\leq T}\frac{\|\|\delta X^{\prime}_{s,t}|\mathcal{F}_{s}\|_{p}\|_{q}}{|t-s|^{\gamma^{\prime}}}<+\infty;

c)

denoting by
R^{X}_{s,t}=\delta X_{s,t}-X^{\prime}_{s}\delta W_{s,t}
for
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
, it holds that

\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}R^{X}\|_{\gamma+\gamma^{\prime};q}:=\sup_{0\leq s<t\leq T}\frac{\|\mathbb{E}_{s}(R^{X}_{s,t})\|_{q}}{|t-s|^{\gamma+\gamma^{\prime}}}<+\infty.

We write
(X,X^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,q}(V)
or simply
(X,X^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,q}
, if clear from the context.
The process
X^{\prime}
is called the (stochastic) Gubinelli derivative of
X
(with respect to
W
). In the case
p=q
, we simply write
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\gamma;p}
instead of
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,p}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\gamma;p,p}
, respectively.
Whereas, if
\gamma=\gamma^{\prime}
, we write
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\gamma}L_{p,q}
instead of
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma}L_{p,q}
.

For a process
(X,X^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,q}
its seminorm is defined by
3
3

3

This definition is consistent with
[
FHL21
]
, hence does not include the
\sup_{t}\|X_{t}\|_{p}
that we included in our previous definition.

\|(X,X^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,q}}:=\|\delta X\|_{\gamma;p,q}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X^{\prime}_{t}\|_{q}+\|\delta X^{\prime}\|_{\gamma^{\prime};p,q}+\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}R^{X}\|_{\gamma+\gamma^{\prime};q}.

Given another stochastic controlled rough path
(\bar{X},\bar{X}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{\bar{W}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,q}
for some
\bar{W}\in C^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we define their distance as

\|X,X^{\prime};\bar{X},\bar{X}^{\prime}\|_{W,\bar{W};\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p}:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{t}-\bar{X}_{t}\|_{p}+\|\delta(X-\bar{X})\|_{\gamma;p}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{t}^{\prime}-\bar{X}_{t}^{\prime}\|_{p}+\\
+\|\delta(X^{\prime}-\bar{X}^{\prime})\|_{\gamma^{\prime};p}+\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}R^{X}-\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\bar{R}^{\bar{X}}\|_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p},

where
\bar{R}^{\bar{X}}_{s,t}=\delta\bar{X}_{s,t}-\bar{X}^{\prime}_{s}\delta\bar{W}_{s,t}
.
If
\bar{W}=W
, we often write
\|\ \cdot\ ;\ \cdot\ \|_{W;\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p}
instead of
\|\ \cdot\ ;\ \cdot\|_{W,W;\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p}
.
The space
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,q}
endowed with the distance
d((X,X^{\prime}),(\bar{X},\bar{X}^{\prime})):=\|X,X^{\prime};\bar{X},\bar{X}^{\prime}\|_{W;\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p}
is a Banach space.

Definition 2.2
.

(see
[
FHL21
, Definition 4.1]
)
We say that
g:\Omega\times[0,T]\times V\to\bar{V}
is a
random bounded vector field
from
V
to
\bar{V}
if
(\omega,t)\mapsto g_{t}(\omega,\cdot)
is a progressively strongly measurable stochastic process taking values in
C^{0}_{b}(V;\bar{V})
and there exists a deterministic constant, denoted by
\|g\|_{\infty}
, such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{\omega}\sup_{x\in V}|g_{t}(\omega,x)|\leq\|g\|_{\infty}.

We say that
g
is a
random bounded Lipschitz vector field
from
V
to
\bar{V}
if it is a random bounded continuous vector field, strongly measurable from
\Omega\times[0,T]\to Lip(V;\bar{V})
, and there exists a deterministic constant, denoted by
\|g\|_{Lip}
, such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{\omega}\sup_{x,\bar{x}\in V,x\neq\bar{x}}\frac{|g(\omega,t,x)-g(\omega,t,\bar{x})|}{|x-\bar{x}|}\leq\|g\|_{Lip}.

Definition 2.3
.

(generalization of
[
FHL21
, Definition 3.8]
)
Let
\kappa>1
and denote by
\lceil\kappa\rceil\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
its ceiling part.
A
stochastic controlled vector field
in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,q}C_{b}^{\kappa}(V;\bar{V})
- or simply in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,q}C_{b}^{\kappa}
- is a pairing
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})
satisfying the following:

a)

\beta:\Omega\times[0,T]\times V\to\bar{V}
is progressively measurable such that, for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega
and for any
t\in[0,T]
,
\beta_{t}(\cdot):=\beta(\omega,t,\cdot)\in C^{\kappa}_{b}(V;\bar{V})
and

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\||\beta_{t}(\cdot)|_{C^{\kappa}_{b}}\|_{q}:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\||\beta_{t}(\cdot)|_{C_{b}^{\lfloor\kappa\rfloor}}+[D^{\kappa}_{x\dots x}\beta_{t}(\cdot)]_{\kappa-\lfloor\kappa\rfloor}\|_{q}<+\infty;

b)

\beta^{\prime}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times V\to\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\bar{V})
is progressively measurable such that, for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega
and for any
t\in[0,T]
,
\beta_{t}^{\prime}(\cdot):=\beta^{\prime}(\omega,t,\cdot)\in C^{\kappa-1}_{b}(V;\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\bar{V}))
and

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\||\beta^{\prime}_{t}(\cdot)|_{C^{\kappa-1}_{b}}\|_{q}<+\infty;

c)

denoting by
R^{\beta}_{s,t}(\cdot):=\beta_{t}(\cdot)-\beta_{s}(\cdot)-\beta_{s}^{\prime}(\cdot)\delta W_{s,t}
and letting
\llbracket g\rrbracket_{\delta;p,q}:=\sup_{0\leq s<t\leq T}\frac{\|\||g(\cdot)|_{\infty}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s}\|_{p}\|_{q}}{|t-s|^{\delta}}
,

\displaystyle\llbracket(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\rrbracket_{W;2\delta;p,q;\kappa}

\displaystyle:=\llbracket\delta\beta\rrbracket_{\delta;p,q}+\llbracket\delta D_{x}\beta\rrbracket_{\delta;p,q}+\dots+\llbracket\delta D_{x\dots x}^{\lceil\kappa\rceil-1}\beta\rrbracket_{\delta;p,q}+\llbracket\delta\beta^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\delta;p,q}+\dots+

\displaystyle\quad+\llbracket\delta D_{x\dots x}^{\lceil\kappa\rceil-2}\beta^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\delta;p,q}+\llbracket\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}R^{\beta}\rrbracket_{2\delta;q}+\dots+\llbracket\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}R^{D^{\lceil\kappa\rceil-2}_{x\dots x}\beta}\rrbracket_{2\delta;q}<+\infty.

We denote by

\llbracket(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\kappa;q}:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\||\beta_{t}(\cdot)|_{C^{\kappa}_{b}}\|_{q}+\||\beta^{\prime}_{t}(\cdot)|_{C^{\kappa-1}_{b}}\|_{q}\right).

Given another
\bar{W}\in C^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
and given
(\bar{\beta},\bar{\beta}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{\bar{W}}^{2\delta}L_{p,q}C_{b}^{\kappa}(V;\bar{V})
, we denote by

\llbracket\beta,\beta^{\prime};\bar{\beta},\bar{\beta}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{W,\bar{W};2\delta;p;\kappa}:=\llbracket\delta(\beta-\bar{\beta})\rrbracket_{\delta;p}+\dots+\llbracket(\delta D_{x\dots x}^{\lceil\kappa\rceil-1}\beta-D_{x\dots x}^{\lceil\kappa\rceil-1}\bar{\beta})\rrbracket_{\delta;p}+\\
+\llbracket\delta\beta^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\delta;p,q}+\dots+\llbracket\delta(D_{x\dots x}^{\lceil\kappa\rceil-2}\beta^{\prime}-D_{x\dots x}^{\lceil\kappa\rceil-2}\bar{\beta}^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\delta;p,q}+\llbracket\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}R^{\beta}\rrbracket_{2\delta;q}+\dots+\llbracket(\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}R^{D^{\lceil\kappa\rceil-2}_{x\dots x}\beta}-\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\bar{R}^{D^{\lceil\kappa\rceil-2}_{x\dots x}\bar{\beta}})\rrbracket_{2\delta;q}.

If
p=q
, we simply write
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p}C_{b}^{\kappa}
instead of
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,p}C_{b}^{\kappa}
.
If
\beta
and
\beta^{\prime}
are deterministic functions satisfying all the requirements above, we write
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\in\mathscr{D}_{W}^{2\delta}C_{b}^{\kappa}
, we say it is a deterministic controlled vector field and we drop the integrability exponents
p,q
when denoting the corresponding norms.

Remark 2.4
.

The previous definition is formulated such that by taking the derivative of a stochastic controlled vector we still obtain a stochastic controlled vector field. More precisely, given
\kappa\geq 2
and
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,q}C_{b}^{\kappa}(V;\bar{V})
, it holds that

(D_{x}\beta,D_{x}\beta^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,q}C_{b}^{\kappa-1}(V;\mathcal{L}(V;\bar{V})).

This is useful when one needs to prove recursive properties, but the prize we pay is that we need to assume the same Hölder regularity
\delta
on every object. (To wit,
[
FHL21
]
would allows to replace the above exponent
2\delta
by a suitable sum
\delta+\delta^{\prime}
.)
Let us also notice that, for any
\kappa>1
and for any
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,q}C_{b}^{\kappa}(V\times\tilde{V};\bar{V})
,

(\beta(\cdot,\tilde{x}),\beta^{\prime}(\cdot,\tilde{x}))\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,q}C_{b}^{\kappa-1}(V;\mathcal{L}(V;\bar{V}))\quad\text{for any $\tilde{x}\in\tilde{V}$},

and
(D_{\tilde{x}}\beta,D_{\tilde{x}}\beta^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,q}C_{b}^{\kappa-1}(V\times\tilde{V};\mathcal{L}(\tilde{V};\bar{V}))
.

Proposition 2.5
.

(see
[
FHL21
, Lemma 3.11]
and
[
FHL21
, Proposition 3.13]
)

•

Let
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}C_{b}^{\kappa}(V;\bar{V})
for some
\kappa\in(1,2]
and let
(X,X^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}(V)
. Define
(Z,Z^{\prime}):=(\beta(X),D_{x}\beta(X)X^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}(X))
. Then
(Z,Z^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}(\bar{V})
with

\|(Z,Z^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}}\lesssim_{T}(\llbracket(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\bar{\kappa};\infty}+\llbracket(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\rrbracket_{W;2\delta^{\prime};p,\infty;\bar{\kappa}})(1+\|(X,X^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}}^{\kappa}),

where
\eta=\min\{\delta,\gamma\}
and
\eta^{\prime}=\min\{(\kappa-1)\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\delta\}
.

•

Let
(X,X^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}(V)
and
(\bar{X},\bar{X}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{\bar{W}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}(V)
, and let
M>0
be any constant such that
\|(X,X^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}}+\|\bar{X},\bar{X}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\bar{W}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}}\leq M
.
Let
\kappa\in[2,3]
. Let
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}C_{b}^{\kappa}(V;\bar{V})
and
(\bar{\beta},\bar{\beta}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{\bar{W}}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}C_{b}^{\kappa}(V;\bar{V})
, and define
(Z,Z^{\prime})
and
(\bar{Z},\bar{Z}^{\prime})
as before. Then

\|Z,Z^{\prime};\bar{Z},\bar{Z}^{\prime}\|_{W,\bar{W};\lambda,\lambda^{\prime};p}\lesssim_{M}\|X,X^{\prime};\bar{X},\bar{X}^{\prime}\|_{W,\bar{W};\lambda,\lambda^{\prime};p}+\llbracket(\beta-\bar{\beta},\beta^{\prime}-\bar{\beta}^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\kappa-2;p}+\llbracket\beta,\beta^{\prime};\bar{\beta},\bar{\beta}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{W,\bar{W};\lambda,\lambda^{\prime};p;\kappa}

where
\lambda=\min\{\gamma,\delta\}
and
\lambda^{\prime}=\min\{(\kappa-2)\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\delta\}
.

Definition 2.6
.

(see
[
BCN24
, Definition 3.1]
)
We say that
Q:\Omega\times[0,T]\times V\to\bar{V}
is a
stochastic linear vector field
from
V
to
\bar{V}
if the mapping
(\omega,t)\mapsto Q_{t}(\omega,t,\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}(V;\bar{V})
is progressively strongly measurable and there is a deterministic constant
\|Q\|_{\infty}>0
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{\omega}|Q_{t}(\omega,\cdot)|_{\mathcal{L}(V,\bar{V})}\leq\|Q\|_{\infty}<+\infty.

We denote by
\|Q\|_{\mathcal{L};p}:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\||Q_{t}|_{\mathcal{L}(V,\bar{V})}\|_{p}
, where
|\cdot|_{\mathcal{L}(V,\bar{V})}
denotes the standard linear operator norm. If clear from the context we simply denote such a norm by
|\cdot|_{\mathcal{L}}
.

Definition 2.7
.

(see
[
BCN24
, Definition 3.2]
)
A pair
(f,f^{\prime})
is said to be a
stochastic controlled linear vector field
from
V
to
\bar{V}
of
(p,q)
-integrability and
(\delta,\delta^{\prime})
-Hölder regularity - and we write
(f,f^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}_{W}L_{p,q}\mathcal{L}(V,\bar{V})
or simply
(f,f^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}_{W}L_{p,q}\mathcal{L}
- if

a)

f:\Omega\times[0,T]\times V\to\bar{V}
is a stochastic linear vector field with

\|\delta f\|_{\delta;p,q}:=\sup_{0\leq s<t\leq T}\frac{\|\||f_{t}-f_{s}|_{\mathcal{L}(V,\bar{V})}|\mathcal{F}_{s}\|_{p}\|_{q}}{|t-s|^{\delta}}<+\infty;

b)

f^{\prime}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times V\to\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\bar{V})
is a stochastic linear vector field with

\|\delta f^{\prime}\|_{\delta^{\prime};p,q}:=\sup_{0\leq s<t\leq T}\frac{\|\||f^{\prime}_{t}-f^{\prime}_{s}|_{\mathcal{L}(V,\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\bar{V}))}|\mathcal{F}_{s}\|_{p}\|_{q}}{|t-s|^{\delta^{\prime}}}<+\infty;

c)

denoting by
R_{s,t}^{f}:=f_{t}-f_{s}-f^{\prime}_{s}\delta Z_{s,t}
for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
, it holds

\|\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}R^{f}\|_{\delta+\delta^{\prime};q}:=\sup_{0\leq s<t\leq T}\frac{\||\mathbb{E}_{s}(R_{s,t}^{f})|_{\mathcal{L}(V,\bar{V})}\|_{q}}{|t-s|^{\delta+\delta^{\prime}}}<+\infty.

Given
(f,f^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}_{W}L_{p,q}\mathcal{L}
we define

\displaystyle\llbracket(f,f^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};q}

\displaystyle:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\||f_{t}|_{\mathcal{L}}\|_{q}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\||f^{\prime}_{t}|_{\mathcal{L}}\|_{q}

\displaystyle\llbracket(f,f^{\prime})\rrbracket_{W;\delta,\delta^{\prime};p,q}

\displaystyle:=\|\delta f\|_{\delta;p,q}+\|\delta f^{\prime}\|_{\delta^{\prime};p,q}+\|E_{\cdot}R^{f}\|_{\delta+\delta^{\prime};q}.

Given another
\bar{W}\in C^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
and given
(\bar{f},\bar{f}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}_{\bar{W}}L_{p,q}\mathcal{L}
, we denote by

\llbracket f,f^{\prime};\bar{f},\bar{f}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{W,\bar{W};\delta,\delta^{\prime};p;\mathcal{L}}:=\|\delta(f-\bar{f})\|_{\delta;p}+\|\delta(f^{\prime}-\bar{f}^{\prime})\|_{\delta^{\prime};p}+\|E_{\cdot}R^{f}-\mathbb{E}_{\cdot}\bar{R}^{\bar{f}}\|_{\delta+\delta^{\prime};p},

where
\bar{R}^{\bar{f}}_{s,t}=\delta\bar{f}_{s,t}-\bar{f}_{s}^{\prime}\delta\bar{W}_{s,t}
.

Proposition 2.8
.

1.

Let
(Y,Y^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p}(V)
and let
(f,f^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}_{W}L_{p,\infty}\mathcal{L}(V,\bar{V})
.
Define
(f,f^{\prime})Y:=(fY,f^{\prime}Y+fY^{\prime})
. Then
(f,f^{\prime})Y\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}L_{p}(\bar{V})
and

\|(f,f^{\prime})Y\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}L_{p}}\leq\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|Y_{t}\|_{p}+\|(Y,Y^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}L_{p}}\Big)\left(\llbracket(f,f^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};\infty}+\llbracket(f,f^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}\mathcal{L}}\right),

where
\eta=\min\{\gamma,\delta\}
and
\eta^{\prime}=\min\{\gamma,\delta,\gamma^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}\}
.

2.

Let
(Y,Y^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{2p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and let
(f,f^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}L_{2p}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
.
Let
(\bar{Y},\bar{Y}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{\bar{W}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{2p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and let
(\bar{f},\bar{f}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{\bar{W}}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}L_{2p}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
.
Let
M>0
be any constant such that

\llbracket(f,f^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};2p}+\llbracket(f,f^{\prime})\rrbracket_{W;\delta,\delta^{\prime};2p;\mathcal{L}}+\llbracket(\bar{f},\bar{f}^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};2p}+\llbracket(\bar{f},\bar{f}^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\bar{W};\delta,\delta^{\prime};2p;\mathcal{L}}+\\
+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|Y_{t}\|_{2p}+\|(Y,Y^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{2p}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bar{Y}_{t}\|_{2p}+\|(\bar{Y},\bar{Y}^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{\bar{W}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{2p}}\leq M.

Denote by
(Z,Z^{\prime})=(fY,f^{\prime}Y+fY^{\prime})
and, similarly,
(\bar{Z},\bar{Z}^{\prime})=(\bar{f}\bar{Y},\bar{f}^{\prime}\bar{Y}+\bar{f}\bar{Y}^{\prime})
.
Then
(Z,Z^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}L_{p}(V)
,
(\bar{Z},\bar{Z}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{\bar{W}}^{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}L_{p}(V)
, and

\|Z,Z^{\prime};\bar{Z},\bar{Z}^{\prime}\|_{W,\bar{W};\lambda,\lambda^{\prime};p}\lesssim_{M}\llbracket f,f^{\prime};\bar{f},\bar{f}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};2p}+\llbracket f,f^{\prime};\bar{f},\bar{f}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{W,\bar{W};\lambda,\lambda^{\prime};2p}+\|Y,Y^{\prime};\bar{Y},\bar{Y}^{\prime}\|_{W,\bar{W};\lambda,\lambda^{\prime};p},

where
\lambda=\min\{\gamma,\delta\}
and
\lambda^{\prime}=\min\{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\delta,\delta^{\prime}\}
.

Proof.

1.
Straightforward generalization of
[
BCN24
, Proposition 3.3]
.

2.
We just prove the last assertion; the fact that both
(Z,Z^{\prime})
and
(\bar{Z},\bar{Z}^{\prime})
are
L_{p}
-integrable stochastic controlled rough path can be proved by using similar techniques as in
1
.
Let
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
.
For sake of compact notation we denote by
\tilde{Y}=Y-\bar{Y}
and
\tilde{f}=f-\bar{f}
.
One trivially has that

f_{t}Y_{t}-\bar{f}_{t}\bar{Y}_{t}=f_{t}\tilde{Y}_{t}+\tilde{f}_{t}\bar{Y}_{t}

and

f_{t}^{\prime}Y_{t}+f_{t}Y_{t}^{\prime}-\bar{f}_{t}^{\prime}\bar{Y}_{t}-\bar{f}_{t}\bar{Y}^{\prime}_{t}=f_{t}^{\prime}\tilde{Y}_{t}+\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{t}\bar{Y}_{t}+f_{t}\tilde{Y}^{\prime}_{t}+\tilde{f}_{t}\bar{Y}_{t}.

Moreover, we can write

\delta(fY-\bar{f}\bar{Y})_{s,t}=\delta(f\tilde{Y}+\tilde{f}\bar{Y})_{s,t}=f_{t}\delta\tilde{Y}_{s,t}+\delta f_{s,t}\tilde{Y}_{s}+\tilde{f}_{t}\delta\bar{Y}_{s,t}+\delta\tilde{f}_{s,t}\bar{Y}_{s}

and

\displaystyle\delta(f^{\prime}Y+fY^{\prime}-\bar{f}^{\prime}\bar{Y}-\bar{f}\bar{Y}^{\prime})_{s,t}

\displaystyle=\delta(f^{\prime}\tilde{Y}+\tilde{f}^{\prime}\bar{Y}+f\tilde{Y}^{\prime}+\tilde{f}\bar{Y}^{\prime})_{s,t}=

\displaystyle=f^{\prime}_{t}\delta\tilde{Y}_{s,t}+\delta f^{\prime}_{s,t}\tilde{Y}_{s}+\tilde{f}_{t}^{\prime}\delta\bar{Y}_{s,t}+\delta\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{s,t}\bar{Y}_{s}+f_{t}\delta\tilde{Y}^{\prime}_{s,t}+\delta f_{s,t}\tilde{Y}^{\prime}_{s}+\tilde{f}_{t}\delta\bar{Y}_{s,t}^{\prime}+\delta\tilde{f}_{s,t}\bar{Y}^{\prime}_{s}.

By linearity, it is possible to write

\displaystyle{R}_{s,t}^{{f}{Y}}-\bar{R}_{s,t}^{\bar{f}\bar{Y}}

\displaystyle=\delta(fY)_{s,t}-(f^{\prime}_{s}Y_{s}+f_{s}Y^{\prime}_{s})\delta{W}_{s,t}-\delta(\bar{f}\bar{Y})_{s,t}+(\bar{f}_{s}\bar{Y}_{s}+\bar{f}_{s}\bar{Y}^{\prime}_{s})\delta{\bar{W}}_{s,t}=

\displaystyle=f_{t}\delta\tilde{Y}_{s,t}+\delta f_{s,t}\tilde{Y}_{s}+\tilde{f}_{t}\delta\bar{Y}_{s,t}+\delta\tilde{f}_{s,t}\bar{Y}_{s}-f_{s}Y_{s}^{\prime}\delta W_{s,t}+\bar{f}_{s}\bar{Y}^{\prime}_{s}\delta\bar{W}_{s,t}-f^{\prime}_{s}Y_{s}\delta W_{s,t}+\bar{f}^{\prime}_{s}\bar{Y}_{s}\delta\bar{W}_{s,t}=

\displaystyle=\delta f_{s,t}\delta\tilde{Y}_{s,t}+f_{s}(R^{Y}_{s,t}-\bar{R}^{\bar{Y}}_{s,t})+(R^{f}_{s,t}-\bar{R}^{\bar{f}}_{s,t})\bar{Y}_{s}+R^{f}_{s,t}\tilde{Y}_{s}+\tilde{f}_{t}\delta\bar{Y}_{s,t}-\tilde{f}_{s}\bar{Y}^{\prime}_{s}\delta\bar{W}_{s,t}=

\displaystyle=\delta f_{s,t}\delta\tilde{Y}_{s,t}+f_{s}(R^{Y}_{s,t}-\bar{R}^{\bar{Y}}_{s,t})+(R^{f}_{s,t}-\bar{R}^{\bar{f}}_{s,t})\bar{Y}_{s}+R^{f}_{s,t}\tilde{Y}_{s}+\delta\tilde{f}_{s,t}\delta\bar{Y}_{s,t}+\tilde{f}_{s}\bar{R}^{\bar{Y}}_{s,t}.

The conclusion follows by applying Hölder’s inequality to all the previous identities. As an example, we notice that

\|\mathbb{E}_{s}(\delta f_{s,t}\delta\tilde{Y}_{s,t})\|_{p}\leq\|\delta f_{s,t}\|_{2p}\|\delta\tilde{Y}_{s,t}\|_{2p}\lesssim_{M}\|Y,Y^{\prime};\bar{Y},\bar{Y}^{\prime}\|_{W,\bar{W};\lambda,\lambda^{\prime};2p}|t-s|^{\lambda+\lambda^{\prime}}.

∎

Definition 2.9
.

(see
[
FH20
, Definition 2.1, Definition 2.4 and Section 2.2]
)
Let
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2}]
.
An
\alpha
-
rough path
on
[0,T]
with values in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a pair
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W})
such that

a)

W:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
|\delta W|_{\alpha}:=\sup_{s\neq t}\frac{|W_{t}-W_{s}|_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}}{|t-s|^{\alpha}}<+\infty
;

b)

\mathbb{W}:[0,T]^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
|\mathbb{W}|_{2\alpha}:=\sup_{s\neq t}\frac{|\mathbb{W}_{s,t}|_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{n}}}{|t-s|^{2\alpha}}<+\infty
;

c)

(Chen’s relation) for any
s,u,t\in[0,T]
, it holds

\mathbb{W}_{s,t}-\mathbb{W}_{s,u}-\mathbb{W}_{u,t}=\delta W_{s,u}\otimes\delta W_{u,t}

or, equivalently,
\mathbb{W}_{s,t}^{ij}-\mathbb{W}_{s,u}^{ij}-\mathbb{W}_{u,t}^{ij}=\delta W_{s,u}^{i}\delta W_{u,t}^{j}
for any
i,j=1,\dots,n
.

We write
\mathbf{W}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and we define
|\mathbf{W}|_{\alpha}:=|\delta W|_{\alpha}+|\mathbb{W}|_{2\alpha}
.
Given any pair of rough paths
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W}),\ \bar{\mathbf{W}}=(\bar{W},\bar{\mathbb{W}})\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we define their distance as

\displaystyle\rho_{\alpha}(\mathbf{W},\bar{\mathbf{W}})

\displaystyle:=|\delta(W-\bar{W})|_{\alpha}+|\mathbb{W}-\bar{\mathbb{W}}|_{2\alpha}.

An
\alpha
-rough path
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W})
is said to be
weakly geometric
if, for any
s,t\in[0,T]

Sym(\mathbb{W}_{s,t}):=\frac{\mathbb{W}_{s,t}+\mathbb{W}_{s,t}^{\top}}{2}=\frac{1}{2}\delta W_{s,t}\otimes\delta W_{s,t}.

or, equivalently,
\frac{1}{2}(\mathbb{W}^{ij}+\mathbb{W}^{ji})=\frac{1}{2}\delta W^{i}\delta W^{j}
for any
i,j=1,\dots,n
.
We write
\mathbf{W}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{g}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

.

)

.
Moreover,

\Big(\int_{0}^{\cdot}(X_{r},X_{r}^{\prime})d\mathbf{W}_{r},X\Big)\in\mathbf{D}^{\alpha,\min\{\alpha,\gamma\}}_{W}L_{p,q}(V).

Let
B=(B_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
be an

dX_{t}=dF_{t}+b_{t}(X_{t})\,dt+\sigma_{t}(X_{t})\,dB_{t}+(\beta_{t},\beta^{\prime}_{t})(X_{t})\,d\mathbf{W}_{t}

Definition 2.11
.

An
L_{p,q}
-integrable solution of (
8
) over
[0,T]
is a continuous and adapted
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued stochastic process
X
such that the following conditions are satisfied:

a)

\int_{0}^{T}|b_{r}(X_{r})|dr
and
\int_{0}^{T}|(\sigma\sigma^{\top})_{r}(X_{r})|dr

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.;

(\beta(X),D_{x}\beta(X)(\beta(X)+F^{\prime})+\beta^{\prime}(X))

,

\bar{\alpha},\bar{\alpha}^{\prime}\in(0,1]
such that
\alpha+\min\{\alpha,\bar{\alpha}\}>\frac{1}{2}
and
\alpha+\min\{\alpha,\bar{\alpha}\}+\bar{\alpha}^{\prime}>1

X
satisfies the following stochastic Davie-type expansion for every

\delta X_{s,t}=\delta F_{s,t}+\int_{s}^{t}b_{r}(X_{r})dr+\int_{s}^{t}\sigma_{r}(X_{r})dB_{r}+\\
+\beta_{s}(X_{s})\delta W_{s,t}+(D_{x}\beta_{s}(X_{s})(\beta_{s}(X_{s})+F^{\prime}_{s})+\beta^{\prime}_{s}(X_{s}))\mathbb{W}_{s,t}+X^{\natural}_{s,t},

where
\|\|X^{\natural}_{s,t}|\mathcal{F}_{s}\|_{p}\|_{q}=o(|t-s|^{\frac{1}{2}})
and
\|\mathbb{E}_{s}(X^{\natural}_{s,t})\|_{q}=o(|t-s|)
as
|t-s|\to 0
.

When the starting position
X_{0}=\xi
is specified, we say that
X
is a solution starting from
\xi
.
Equivalently, c) can be replaced by

c’)

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely and for any

,

X_{t}=\xi+F_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}b_{r}(X_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{r}(X_{r})dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(\beta_{r}(X_{r}),D_{x}\beta_{r}(X_{r})(\beta_{r}(X_{r})+F^{\prime}_{r})+\beta^{\prime}_{r}(X_{r}))d\mathbf{W}_{r}

and one has that
\|\|X^{\natural}_{s,t}|\mathcal{F}_{s}\|_{p}\|_{q}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\min\{\alpha,\bar{\alpha}\}}
and
\|\mathbb{E}_{s}(X^{\natural}_{s,t})\|_{q}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\min\{\alpha,\bar{\alpha}\}+\bar{\alpha}^{\prime}}
. We often denote by

.
Notice that, if a solution exists then necessarily
(X,\beta(X)+F^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\min\{\alpha,\bar{\alpha}\}}L_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
We are interested in two different situations:

1.

rough SDEs with bounded coefficients
(or simply

D_{x}\beta=\beta
, and b) can be directly deduced from c). In this case, the solution theory is possible for
p=q<\infty
.

By slightly rearranging the proof of

∎

We recall a classical interpolation result for Hölder spaces. This kind of statements can be applied to stochastic controlled rough paths and to stochastic controlled vector fields, possibly linear. For definiteness, we explicitly state the result in the former case in
2.17
; analogous statements can be deduced for the latter.

Proposition 2.16
.

(Interpolation)

Let
Z:[0,T]\to V
be an
\alpha
-Hölder continuous path, for some
\alpha\in(0,1]
, and let us denote by
[Z]_{\alpha}
its Hölder seminorm.
Then, for any
\gamma\in(0,\alpha)
,

[Z]_{\gamma}\leq[Z]_{\alpha}^{\frac{\gamma}{\alpha}}\left(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}|\delta Z_{s,t}|\right)^{1-\frac{\gamma}{\alpha}}.

In particular, if
(Z^{n})_{n}
is a sequence of
\alpha
-Hölder continuous paths such that
\sup_{n}[Z^{n}]_{\alpha}<+\infty
and
Z^{n}\to Z^{\infty}
uniformly on
[0,T]
, then, for any
\gamma\in(0,\alpha)
,
Z^{\infty}
is
\gamma
-Hölder continuous and
[Z^{n}-Z^{\infty}]_{\gamma}\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
.

Corollary 2.17
.

Let
(W^{n})_{n\geq 0}
be a sequence of
\alpha
-Hölder continuous paths, for some
\alpha\in(0,1]
. For any
n\geq 0
, let
(X^{n},(X^{n})^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W^{n}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,q}
and assume
\|X^{n},(X^{n})^{\prime};X^{0},(X^{0})^{\prime}\|_{W^{n},W^{0};\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p,q}\to 0
as
n\to+\infty,n\geq 1
.
Assume there exist
\lambda\in(\gamma,1]
and
\lambda^{\prime}\in(\gamma^{\prime},1]
such that

\sup_{n\geq 1}\|(X^{n},(X^{n})^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W^{n}}^{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}L_{p,q}}<+\infty.

Then
(X^{0},(X^{0})^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W^{0}}^{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}L_{p,q}
and
\|X^{n},(X^{n})^{\prime};X^{0},(X^{0})^{\prime}\|_{W^{n},W^{0};\eta,\eta^{\prime};p,q}\to 0
as
n\to+\infty,n\geq 1
, for any
\eta\in(\gamma,\lambda)
,
\eta^{\prime}\in(\gamma^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})
.

3
Parameter-dependent rough SDEs

Let
T>0
and let
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
be a complete filtered probability space and let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
.
In
[
Kry08
]
one can find the following notions of
\mathscr{L}
-
continuity
and
\mathscr{L}
-
differentiability
for a family of stochastic processes
\{X^{\zeta},\ \zeta\in U\}
taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and indexed by a parameter.

Definition 3.1
.

(cf.
[
Kry08
, Definition 1.7.1]
)
We denote by
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
the set of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued progressively measurable stochastic processes belonging to
L^{p}(\Omega\times[0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
. For definiteness,
X\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if it is progressively measurable and

\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|X_{r}|^{p}dr\right)<+\infty\qquad\text{for any $p\in[1,\infty)$}.

If clear from the context, we simply write
\mathscr{L}
instead of
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
With a little abuse of notation,
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
can also denote the set of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued random variables belonging to
L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
.

Definition 3.2
.

(cf.
[
Kry08
, Definition 1.7.2]
)
Let
(\mathcal{U},\rho)
be a metric space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be non-empty.
Let
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a family of stochastic processes in
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and let
\zeta^{0}\in U
be an accumulation point.
We say that
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
admits limit in
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
\zeta\to\zeta^{0}
if there is
Z\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that, for any
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
with
\rho(\zeta^{n},\zeta^{0})\to_{n\to+\infty}0
and for any
p\in[1,\infty)
, there exists

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|X^{\zeta^{n}}_{r}-Z_{r}|^{p}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}=0.

In such a case, we write
Z=\mathscr{L}\text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}X^{\zeta}
.
We say that
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}
-continuous at
\zeta^{0}\in U
if
\zeta^{0}
is an isolated point of
U
, or if it is an accumulation point and
X^{\zeta^{0}}=\mathscr{L}\text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}X^{\zeta}
.
We say that
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}
-continuous if it is
\mathscr{L}
-continuous at every
\zeta^{0}\in U
.

Definition 3.3
.

(cf.
[
Kry08
, Definition 1.7.2]
)
Let
(\mathcal{U},|\cdot|)
be a normed vector space, let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be a non-empty open subset and let
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}\subseteq\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
We say that
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable at
\zeta^{0}\in U
in the direction
l\in\mathcal{U},|l|=1
, if

\left\{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-X^{\zeta^{0}})\right\}_{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}}

admits limit in
\mathscr{L}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. We denote such a limit by
\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta^{0}}
.
We say that
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is (
\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable if it is
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable at any
\zeta^{0}\in U
in every direction
l\in\mathcal{U},|l|=1
(and, for any
l
,
\{\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}
-continuous).
Recursively on
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, we say that
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
(k+1)
-times (
\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable if, for any
l\in\mathcal{U},|l|=1
,
\{\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.

Remark 3.4
.

In practice, the limits in
3.2
and
3.3
can be required to hold for any
p\in[p_{0},\infty)
for some
1\leq p_{0}<\infty
, rather than all
p\in[1,\infty)
.
Since the time horizon
T
is finite, the previous definitions also apply to random variables (i.e. constant-in-time stochastic processes).

The previous framework accommodates where
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is the family of solutions to a parameter-dependent SDE of the form
dX^{\zeta}_{t}=b_{t}(\zeta,X^{\zeta}_{t})dt+\sigma_{t}(\zeta,X^{\zeta}_{t})dB_{t}
.
Indeed, every
X^{\zeta}
can be seen as an element of
L^{p}(\Omega\times[0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
See
[
Kry08
, Theorem 1.8.1]
and
[
Kry08
, Theorem 1.8.4]
for relevant results.
To convince the reader that the previous are good notions to understand continuity or the differentiability in mean for a family of stochastic processes, we immediately present the following result.

Proposition 3.5
.

1.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\rho)
be a metric space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be non-empty. Let
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-continuous family of random variables and let
\phi:U\to\mathbb{R}
be the map defined by
\phi(\zeta):=\mathbb{E}(X^{\zeta})
.
Then
\phi
is continuous.

2.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
and let
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
be a family of
k
-times
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-differentiable random variables.
Let us define
\phi(\zeta):=\mathbb{E}(X^{\zeta})
for
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Then, for any
i_{1},\dots,i_{k}\in\{1,\dots,d\}
, there exists

\frac{\partial^{k}\phi}{\partial x^{i_{k}}\dots\partial x^{i_{1}}}(\zeta)=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i_{k}}}\dots\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i_{1}}}X^{\zeta}\right).

Proof.

1.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
be an accumulation point, and let
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U
be an arbitrary sequence such that
\rho(\zeta^{n},\zeta^{0})\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
.
Then, for any
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle|\phi(\zeta^{n})-\phi(\zeta^{0})|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}(X^{\zeta^{n}})-\mathbb{E}(X^{\zeta^{0}})|\leq\mathbb{E}(|X^{\zeta^{n}}-X^{\zeta^{0}}|)

and the right-hand side tends to 0 as
n\to+\infty
, by assumption.

2.
We argue recursively on
k
, and we first consider the case
k=1
.
Let
i\in\{1,\dots,d\}
. For any
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and for any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
we can write

\frac{\phi(\zeta+\varepsilon e_{i})-\phi(\zeta)}{\varepsilon}=\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(X^{\zeta+\varepsilon e_{i}}-X^{\zeta})\right),

and it tends to
\mathbb{E}(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}X^{\zeta})
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, by assumption.

Assume now the conclusion holds for
k=k_{0}
and that the assumptions are satisfied for
k=k_{0}+1
. Let
i_{1},\dots,i_{k_{0}},i_{k_{0}+1}\in\{1,\dots,d\}
.
For any
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and for any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\partial^{k_{0}}\phi}{\partial x^{k_{0}}\dots\partial x^{1}}(\zeta+\varepsilon e_{i_{k_{0}+1}})-\frac{\partial^{k_{0}}\phi}{\partial x^{k_{0}}\dots\partial x^{1}}(\zeta)\right)=

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i_{k_{0}}}}\dots\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i_{1}}}X^{\zeta+\varepsilon e_{k_{0}+1}}-\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i_{k_{0}}}}\dots\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i_{1}}}X^{\zeta}\right)\right)

and it tends to
\mathbb{E}\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i_{k_{0}+1}}}\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i_{k_{0}}}}\dots\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i_{1}}}X^{\zeta}\right)\right)
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, by assumption.
∎

As explained in
[
FHL21
]
, a solution
X^{\zeta}
to a parameter-dependent rough SDE of the form
dX^{\zeta}_{t}=b_{t}(\zeta,X^{\zeta}_{t})dt+\sigma_{t}(\zeta,X^{\zeta}_{t})dB_{t}+\beta_{t}(\zeta,X^{\zeta}_{t})d\mathbf{W}_{t}
must be considered in a subset of
L^{p}(\Omega\times[0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
, namely the space of stochastic controlled rough paths introduced in
2.1
.
The norm in
3.1
is too weak to obtain meaningful stability estimates (see instead
2.13
).
Moreover, the solution space itself depends on the rough path driving the equation.
For this reason, continuity with respect to
\zeta
is formulated below allowing
\mathbf{W}
to vary with the parameter, while differentiability requires
\mathbf{W}
to be fixed so that linear combinations of solutions remain well defined.
In the following paragraphs we refine the notions of
\mathscr{L}
-continuity and
\mathscr{L}
-differentiability to extend Krylov’s theory to rough SDEs.

3.1
Continuity for rough SDEs

Let
(\mathcal{U},\rho)
be a metric space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be non-empty.

Definition 3.6
.

Let
W:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
be
\alpha
-Hölder continuous, with
\alpha\in(0,1]
, and let
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in[0,1]
.
We denote by
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
the set of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued stochastic controlled rough paths belonging to
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
(see
2.1
).
If clear from the context, we simply denote
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
by
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
.

Definition 3.7
.

Let
\alpha,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in[0,1]
, with
\alpha\neq 0
.
Let
\{W^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in\mathcal{U}}
be a family of
\alpha
-Hölder continuous paths depending on a parameter and, for any
\zeta\in U
, let
(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
be an accumulation point.
We say that
\{(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
admits limit in
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\}_{\zeta\in U}
as
\zeta\to\zeta^{0}
if there is
(Z,Z^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta^{0}}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
such that, for any
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
converging to
\zeta^{0}
in
\mathcal{U}
and for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\|X^{\zeta^{n}},(X^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime};Z,Z^{\prime}\|_{W^{\zeta^{n}},W^{\zeta^{0}};\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p}=0.

In such a case we write

(Z,Z^{\prime})=\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\}_{\zeta}\ \text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime}).

We say that
\{(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\}_{\zeta}
-continuous at
\zeta^{0}\in U
if
\zeta^{0}
is an isolated point of
U
, or if it is an accumulation point and
(X^{\zeta^{0}},(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})=\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\}_{\zeta}\ \text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})
.
We say that
\{(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\}_{\zeta}
-continuous if it is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\}_{\zeta}
-continuous at every
\zeta^{0}\in U
.
If
W^{\zeta}=W
for any
\zeta\in U
, we simply write
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
instead of
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\}_{\zeta}
.

Let
(V,|\cdot|_{V})
an auxiliary Banach space, typically
V=\mathbb{R}^{d\times d_{2}}
. If clear from the context,
|\cdot|_{V}
is simply denoted by
|\cdot|
.

Definition 3.8
.

For any
\zeta\in U
, let
g^{\zeta}
be a random bounded vector field from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
V
in the sense of
2.2
.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
be an accumulation point.
We say that
\{g^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
admits limit in
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
as
\zeta\to\zeta^{0}
if there is a random bounded vector field
g
from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
V
such that, for any
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
with
\rho(\zeta^{n},\zeta^{0})\to_{n\to+\infty}0
and for any
p\in[1,\infty)
, there exists

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\||g^{\zeta^{n}}_{t}(\cdot)-g_{t}(\cdot)|_{\infty}\|_{p}=0.

In such a case we write
g=\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)\text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}g^{\zeta}
.
We say that
\{g^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}
-continuous at
\zeta^{0}
if
\zeta^{0}
is an isolated point of
U
, or if it is an accumulation point and
g^{\zeta^{0}}=\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}\text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}g^{\zeta}
.
We say that
\{g^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}
-continuous if it is
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}
-continuous at every
\zeta^{0}\in U
.
If clear from the context we simply write
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}
instead of
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
.

Definition 3.9
.

Let
\alpha,\delta\in[0,1]
, with
\alpha\neq 0
, and let
\kappa>1
.

•

Given an
\alpha
-Hölder continuous path
W:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we denote by
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C^{\kappa}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
the set of stochastic controlled vector fields from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
V
belonging to
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p}C^{\kappa}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
(see
2.3
).
If clear from the context, we simply write
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C^{\kappa}_{b}
instead of
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C^{\kappa}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
.

•

Let
\{W^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in\mathcal{U}}\subseteq{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a family of
\alpha
-Hölder continuous paths depending on a parameter.
For any
\zeta\in U
, consider
(\beta^{\zeta},(\beta^{\zeta})^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C_{b}^{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
and let
\zeta^{0}\in U
be an accumulation point.
We say that
\{(\beta^{\zeta},(\beta^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
admits limit in
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C_{b}^{\kappa}\}_{\zeta}
as
\zeta\to\zeta^{0}
if there is
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta^{0}}}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C^{\kappa}_{b}
such that, for any
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
with
\rho(\zeta^{n},\zeta^{0})\to_{n\to+\infty}0
and for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\llbracket(\beta^{\zeta^{n}}-\beta,(\beta^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\kappa;p}+\llbracket\beta^{\zeta^{n}},(\beta^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime};\beta,\beta^{\prime}\rrbracket_{W^{\zeta^{n}},W^{\zeta^{0}};2\delta;p;\kappa}=0.

In such a case we write

(\beta,\beta^{\prime})=\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C_{b}^{\kappa}\}_{\zeta}\text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}(\beta^{\zeta},(\beta^{\zeta})^{\prime}).

We say that
\{(\beta^{\zeta},(\beta^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C_{b}^{\kappa}\}_{\zeta}
-continuous at
\zeta^{0}\in U
if
\zeta^{0}
is an isolated point of
U
, or if it is an accumulation point and
(\beta^{\zeta^{0}},(\beta^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})=\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C_{b}^{\kappa}\}_{\zeta}\text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}(\beta^{\zeta},(\beta^{\zeta})^{\prime})
.
We say that
\{(\beta^{\zeta},(\beta^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C_{b}^{\kappa}\}_{\zeta}
-continuous if it is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C_{b}^{\kappa}\}_{\zeta}
-continuous at any
\zeta^{0}\in U
.
If
W^{\zeta}=W
for any
\zeta\in U
, we simply write
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C_{b}^{\kappa}
instead of
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C_{b}^{\kappa}\}_{\zeta}
.

Definition 3.10
.

Let
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2}]
and let
\{\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}\subseteq\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a family of
\alpha
-rough paths depending on a parameter.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
be an accumulation point.
We say that
\{\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
admits limit in
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
if there is
\mathbf{W}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that, for any arbitrary sequence
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
converging to
\zeta^{0}
, there exists

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\rho_{\alpha}(\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{n}},\mathbf{W})=0.

In such a case we write
\mathbf{W}=\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}\text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}
.
We say that
\{\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-continuous at
\zeta^{0}\in U
if
\zeta^{0}
is an isolated point of
U
, or if it is an accumulation point and
\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{0}}=\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}\text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}
.
We say that
\{\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-continuous if it is
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-continuous at every
\zeta^{0}\in U
.

Theorem 3.11
.

Let
\{\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in{U}}
be a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-continuous family of
\alpha
-rough paths in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2}]
, and let
\kappa\in(2,3]
and
\delta\in[0,\alpha]
such that
\alpha+(\kappa-1)\delta>1
.
Let
\{\xi^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuous family of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
-measurable random variables.
Let
\{b^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
and
\{\sigma^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be two families of random bounded Lipschitz vector fields which are
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuous and
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times m})
-continuous, respectively.
Let
\{(\beta^{\zeta},(\beta^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C^{\kappa-1}_{b}\}_{\zeta}
-continuous family of stochastic controlled vector fields such that, for any
\zeta\in U
,

(\beta^{\zeta},(\beta^{\zeta})^{\prime})\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}C_{b}^{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}).

For any
\zeta\in U
, let
X^{\zeta}
be an
L_{p,\infty}
-integrable solution of the following rough SDE, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
:

X^{\zeta}_{t}=\xi^{\zeta}+\int_{0}^{t}b_{r}^{\zeta}(X^{\zeta}_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{r}(X^{\zeta}_{r})dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(\beta^{\zeta}_{r}(X^{\zeta}_{r}),D_{x}\beta^{\zeta}_{r}(X^{\zeta}_{r})\beta^{\zeta}_{r}(X^{\zeta}_{r})+(\beta^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{r}(X^{\zeta}_{r}))d\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}_{r}\quad t\in[0,T].

Then
\{X^{\zeta},\beta^{\zeta}(X^{\zeta})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\}_{\zeta}
-continuous.

Proof.

Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
be an accumulation point and let
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
be an arbitrary sequence such that
\rho(\zeta^{n},\zeta^{0})\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
.
Let
p\in[2,\infty)
and let
M>0
be any constant such that

\displaystyle|\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{0}}|_{\alpha}+\sup_{n\geq 1}|\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{n}}|_{\alpha}+\|\beta^{\zeta^{0}}\|_{Lip}+\|\sigma^{\zeta^{0}}\|_{Lip}+\llbracket(\beta^{\zeta^{0}},(\beta^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\kappa;\infty}+\llbracket(\beta^{\zeta^{0}},(\beta^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{W^{\zeta^{0}};2\delta;p,\infty;\kappa}\leq M.

From
2.13
we conclude that, for any
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\|X^{\zeta^{n}},\beta^{\zeta^{n}}(X^{\zeta^{n}});X^{\zeta^{0}},\beta^{\zeta^{0}}(X^{\zeta^{0}})\|_{W^{\zeta^{n}},W^{\zeta^{0}};\alpha,\delta;p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M,\alpha,\delta,\kappa,p,T}\|X^{\zeta^{n}}_{0}-X^{\zeta^{0}}_{0}\|_{p}+\rho_{\alpha}(\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{n}},\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{0}})+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\||b^{\zeta^{n}}_{t}-b^{\zeta^{0}}_{t}|_{\infty}\|_{p}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\||\sigma^{\zeta^{n}}_{t}-\sigma^{\zeta^{0}}_{t}|_{\infty}\|_{p}+

\displaystyle+\llbracket(\beta^{\zeta^{n}}-\beta^{\zeta^{0}},(\beta^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime}-(\beta^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\kappa-1;p}+\llbracket\beta^{\zeta^{n}},(\beta^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime};\beta^{\zeta^{0}},(\beta^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\rrbracket_{W^{\zeta^{n}},W^{\zeta^{0}};2\alpha;p;\kappa-1}.

By assumption, the right-hand side is going to zero as
n\to+\infty
, and the assertion is therefore proved.
∎

3.2
Continuity for linear rough SDEs

Let
(\mathcal{U},\rho)
be a metric space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be non-empty.

Definition 3.12
.

Let
\{Q^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a parameter-dependent family of stochastic linear vector fields from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
V
, in the sense of
2.6
.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
be an accumulation point.
We say that
\{Q^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
admits limit in
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
as
\zeta\to\zeta^{0}
if there is a stochastic linear vector field
Q
from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
V
such that, for any
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
with
\rho(\zeta^{n},\zeta^{0})\to_{n\to+\infty}0
and for any
p\in[1,\infty)
, there exists

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|Q^{\zeta^{n}}_{r}-Q_{r}|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)}^{p}dr\right)=0.

In such a case we write

Q=\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)\text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}Q^{\zeta}.

If clear from the context, we simply write
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
instead of
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
.
We say that
\{Q^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuous at
\zeta^{0}\in U
if
\zeta^{0}
is an isolated point of
U
, or if it is an accumulation point and
Q^{\zeta^{0}}=\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}Q^{\zeta}
.
We say that
\{Q^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuous if it is
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuous at every
\zeta^{0}\in U
.

Definition 3.13
.

Let
\alpha,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in[0,1]
, with
\alpha\neq 0
.

•

Given any
\alpha
-Hölder continuous path
W:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we denote by
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
the set of stochastic controlled linear vector fields belonging to
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}L_{p}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
.
If clear from the context, we simply write
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
instead of
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
.

•

Let
\{W^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in\mathcal{U}}\subseteq C^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
and assume that, for every
\zeta\in U
, there is
(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
be an accumulation point.
We say that
\{(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
admits limit in
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\}_{\zeta}
as
\zeta\to\zeta^{0}
if there is
(f,f^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta^{0}}}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
such that, for any
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
with
\rho(\zeta^{n},\zeta^{0})\to 0
and for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\llbracket(f^{\zeta^{n}}-f^{\zeta^{0}},(f^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime}-(f^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};p}+\llbracket f^{\zeta^{n}},(f^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime};f,f^{\prime}\rrbracket_{W^{\zeta^{n}},W^{\zeta^{0}};\delta,\delta^{\prime};p}=0\qquad\text{for any $p\in[2,\infty)$}.

In such a case we write

(f,f^{\prime})=\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\}_{\zeta}\text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime}).

We say that
\{(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\}_{\zeta}
-continuous at
\zeta^{0}\in U
if
\zeta^{0}
is an isolated point of
U
, or if it is an accumulation point and
(f^{\zeta^{0}},(f^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})=\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\}_{\zeta}\text{-}\lim_{\zeta\to\zeta^{0}}(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})
.
We say that
\{(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\}_{\zeta}
-continuous if it is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\}_{\zeta}
-continuous at every
\zeta^{0}\in U
.
If
W^{\zeta}=W
for every
\zeta\in U
, we simply write
\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
instead of
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\}_{\zeta}
.

Theorem 3.14
.

Let
\{\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-continuous family of rough paths, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2})
, and let
\delta\in[0,\alpha]
and
\delta^{\prime}\in[0,1]
with
\alpha+\delta>\frac{1}{2}
and
\alpha+\delta+\min\{\delta,\delta^{\prime}\}>1
.
Let
\{\xi^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be an
\mathscr{L}(V)
-continuous family of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
-measurable random variables.
Let
\{G^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(V;V)
-continuous family of stochastic linear vector fields and let
\{S^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(V;\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{m};V))
-continuous.
Let
\{f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(V;\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};V))\}_{\zeta}
-continuous and let
\{(F^{\zeta},(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
be
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(V)\}_{\zeta}
-continuous.
Moreover, assume that for any
R>0
and for any
p\in[2,\infty)
,

\sup_{|\zeta|\leq R}\left(\llbracket G^{\zeta}\rrbracket_{\infty}+\llbracket S^{\zeta}\rrbracket_{\infty}+\llbracket(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};\infty}+\llbracket(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{W^{\zeta};\delta,\delta^{\prime};p,\infty;\mathcal{L}}\right)<+\infty.

For any
\zeta\in U
, let
Y^{\zeta}
be a
L_{p}
-integrable solution of the following linear RSDE, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
:

Y_{t}^{\zeta}=\xi^{\zeta}+F_{t}^{\zeta}+\int_{0}^{t}G_{r}^{\zeta}Y_{r}^{\zeta}dr+\int_{0}^{t}S_{r}^{\zeta}Y_{r}^{\zeta}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(f^{\zeta}_{r},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{r})Y^{\zeta}_{r}d\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}_{r}\qquad t\in[0,T].

where
(f^{\zeta}_{t},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{t})Y^{\zeta}_{t}=(f_{t}^{\zeta}Y_{t}^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{t}Y_{t}^{\zeta}+f_{t}^{\zeta}(f_{t}^{\zeta}Y_{t}^{\zeta}+(F^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{t}))
.
Then
\{(Y^{\zeta},f^{\zeta}Y^{\zeta}+(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(V)\}_{\zeta}
-continuous.

Proof.

The assertion follows from the local Lipschitz estimates for linear rough SDEs in
2.15
, with a similar argument as in the proof of
3.11
.
∎

3.3
Differentiability for linear rough SDEs

Let
(\mathcal{U},|\cdot|)
be a normed vector space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be a non-empty open subset.
We denote by
\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U}):=\{l\in\mathcal{U}\mid|l|=1\}
the set of all directions (along which we are interested to take derivatives).
Let
W:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an
\alpha
-Hölder continuous path, with
\alpha\in(0,1]
, and let
(V,|\cdot|_{V})
be a finite-dimensional Banach space (think of
V=\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
or
V=\mathbb{R}^{d\times d_{2}}
for some
d_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
).

Definition 3.15
.

Let
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in[0,1]
.
Let
\{(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
be a family of stochastic controlled rough paths depending on a parameter.
We say that it is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable at
\zeta^{0}\in U
in the direction
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
if

\left\{\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-X^{\zeta^{0}}),\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big((X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}-(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\big)\right)\right\}_{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}}

admits limit in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, in the sense of
3.7
.
We denote such a limit as

\left(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta^{0}},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\right).

We say that
\{(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable if it is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable at any
\zeta^{0}\in U
in every direction
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
(and, for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
,
\{(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-continuous).
Recursively on
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, we say that
\{(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
(k+1)
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable if, for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
,
\{(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.

Definition 3.16
.

We say that the family of stochastic linear vector fields
\{Q^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable at
\zeta^{0}\in U
in the direction
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
, if
