\left\{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(Q^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-Q^{\zeta^{0}}\right)\right\}_{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}}

admits limit in
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, in the sense of
3.12
.
We denote such a limit as
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}Q^{\zeta^{0}}
. We say that
\{Q^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable if it is
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable at every
\zeta^{0}\in U
and in every direction
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
.
We say that it is
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuously
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable if, in addition,

\{\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}Q^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuous for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
.
Recursively on
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, we say that
\{Q^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
(k+1)
-times (
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable if, for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
,
\{\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}Q^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable.

Definition 3.17
.

Let
\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in[0,1]
.
Let
\{(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
be a family of stochastic controlled linear vector fields.
We say that
\{(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable at
\zeta^{0}\in U
in the direction
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
if

\left\{\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(f^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-f^{\zeta^{0}}),\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big((f^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}-(f^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\big)\right)\right\}_{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}}

admits limit in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, in the sense of
3.13
.
We denote such a limit as

\left(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}f^{\zeta^{0}},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(f^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\right).

We say that
\{(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable if it is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable at every
\zeta^{0}\in U
in every direction
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
.
We say that it is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuously
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable if, in addition,
\{(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}f^{\zeta},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is

\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuous, for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
.
Recursively on
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
,
\{(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is said to be
(k+1)
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable if, for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
,
\{(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\alpha^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}f^{\zeta},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable.

Theorem 3.18
.

Let
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W})\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
be an
\alpha
-rough path, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2})
, and let
\delta\in[0,\alpha]
,
\delta^{\prime}\in[0,1]
with
\alpha+\delta>\frac{1}{2}
and
\alpha+\delta+\min\{\delta,\delta^{\prime}\}>1
.
Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
.
Assume that
\{\xi^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is a family of
k
-times (
\mathscr{L}(V)
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}(V)
-differentiable
\mathcal{F}_{0}
-measurable random variables, and that
\{(F^{\zeta},(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(V)
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(V)
-differentiable.
Assume
\{G^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
to be
k
-times (
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(V;V)
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(V;V)
-differentiable
and
\{S^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
to be
k
-times (
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(V;\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{m};V))
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(V;\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{m};V))
-differentiable.
Assume that
\{(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(V;\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};V))
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(V;\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};V))
-differentiable.
For any
\zeta\in U
, let
Y^{\zeta}
be a
L_{p}
-integrable solution of the following linear rough SDE, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
:

Y_{t}^{\zeta}=\xi^{\zeta}+F_{t}^{\zeta}+\int_{0}^{t}G_{r}^{\zeta}Y_{r}^{\zeta}dr+\int_{0}^{t}S_{r}^{\zeta}Y_{r}^{\zeta}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(f_{r}^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta}_{r})^{\prime})Y^{\zeta}_{r}d\mathbf{W}_{r}\qquad t\in[0,T],

and assume that, for any
R>0
and for any
p\in[2,\infty)
,

\sup_{|\zeta|\leq R}\left(\|G^{\zeta}\|_{\infty}+\|S^{\zeta}\|_{\infty}+\llbracket(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};\infty}+\llbracket(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{W;\delta,\delta^{\prime};p,\infty;\mathcal{L}}\right)<+\infty.

Then
\{(Y^{\zeta},f^{\zeta}Y^{\zeta}+(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(V)
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(V)
-differentiable.
In particular, given any
\zeta\in U
and
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
- and denoting by
Z^{\zeta,l}=\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}Y^{\zeta}
- it holds that, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, the latter is an
L_{p}
-integrable solution of the following linear RSDE on
[0,T]
with coefficients defined as in (
10
):

Z_{t}^{\zeta,l}=\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\xi^{\zeta}+\tilde{F}_{t}^{\zeta}+\int_{0}^{t}G_{r}^{\zeta}Z_{r}^{\zeta,l}dr+\int_{0}^{t}S_{r}^{\zeta}Z_{r}^{\zeta,l}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(f_{r}^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{r})Z_{r}^{\zeta,l}d\mathbf{W}_{r}.

Proof.

We argue recursively on
k
. Let us first consider the case
k=1
.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
and
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
be arbitrary.
For any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
, we define the following
V
-valued stochastic process:

Z^{\varepsilon}:=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(Y^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-Y^{\zeta^{0}}\right).

The idea is to show that
(Z^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{t}
solves a linear rough SDE, and use the results on linear rough SDEs to prove its convergence as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Let us therefore show that all the requirements in
2.11
are satisfied.
For any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
and by considering the Davie-type expansions of both
Y^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}
and
Y^{\zeta^{0}}
as solutions to some linear rough SDEs, we have

\displaystyle\delta Z_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\delta Y_{s,t}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-Y_{s,t}^{\zeta^{0}})=

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\delta F^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{s,t}-\delta F_{s,t}^{\zeta^{0}})+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{s}^{t}G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}}dr+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{s}^{t}S_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-S_{r}^{\zeta^{0}}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}}dB_{r}+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}}\big)\delta W_{s,t}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}+(F^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}_{s})-f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}+(F^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}_{s}\big)\mathbb{W}_{s,t}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big((f^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}_{s}Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-(f^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}_{s}Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}\big)\mathbb{W}_{s,t}+Z^{\varepsilon,\natural}_{s,t}

where
Z^{\varepsilon,\natural}_{s,t}:=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(Y^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,\natural}_{s,t}-Y^{\zeta^{0},\natural}_{s,t})
is such that
\|Z^{\varepsilon,\natural}_{s,t}\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\delta}
and
\|\mathbb{E}_{s}(Z^{\varepsilon,\natural}_{s,t})\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\delta+\delta^{\prime}}
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
.
By the linearity of the coefficients, we can rewrite all the terms on the right-hand side in a more convenient way.
For instance,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}})+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}}-G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}})=

\displaystyle=G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Z_{r}^{\varepsilon}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}})\right)Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}}=:G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Z_{r}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{r}^{\varepsilon},

and it is possible to prove - by a simple application of Hölder’s inequality - that
\mu^{\varepsilon}\in\mathscr{L}(V)
.
By assumption we have that
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(V;V)\text{-}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0,\varepsilon\neq 0}G^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}=G^{\zeta^{0}}
and we also deduce that
\mathscr{L}(V)\text{-}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0,\varepsilon\neq 0}\mu^{\varepsilon}=(\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}G^{\zeta^{0}})Y^{\zeta^{0}}
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(S_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-S_{r}^{\zeta^{0}}Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}})

\displaystyle=S_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Z_{r}^{\varepsilon}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(S_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-S_{r}^{\zeta^{0}})\right)Y_{r}^{\zeta^{0}}=:S_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Z_{r}^{\varepsilon}+\nu_{r}^{\varepsilon},

and we have that
\nu^{\varepsilon}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{m};V))
and
S^{\zeta^{0}}=\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(V;\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{m};V))\text{-}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0,\varepsilon\neq 0}S^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}
.
Again, a simple application of Hölder’s inequality shows that
\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{m};V))\text{-}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0,\varepsilon\neq 0}\nu^{\varepsilon}=(\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}S^{\zeta^{0}})Y^{\zeta^{0}}
.
The remaining terms can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})

\displaystyle=f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Z_{s}^{\varepsilon}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})\right)Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}=:f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Z_{s}^{\varepsilon}+\phi_{s}^{\varepsilon},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}((f^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}_{s}Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-(f^{\zeta^{0}}Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}_{s})

\displaystyle=(f^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}_{s}Z_{s}^{\varepsilon}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}((f^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}_{s}-(f^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}_{s})\right)Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}=:(f^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}_{s}Z_{s}^{\varepsilon}+(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{2})^{\prime}_{s},

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}+(F^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}_{s})-f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}+(F^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}_{s})=

\displaystyle=f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Z_{s}^{\varepsilon}+f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})\right)Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})\right)f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}Y_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}+

\displaystyle\quad+f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}((F^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}_{s}-(F^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}_{s})+\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})\right)(F^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}_{s}

\displaystyle=:f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}\left(f_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Z_{s}^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}((F^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}_{s}-(F^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}_{s})\right)+(\phi_{1}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}_{s}.

From
[
BCN24
, Proposition 3.3]
we see that
(\phi^{\varepsilon},(\phi^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}:=(\phi_{1}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}+(\phi_{2}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};V))
, being the space of stochastic controlled linear vector fields a linear space.
Moreover, by assumption we have that

\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(V;\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};V))\text{-}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0,\varepsilon\neq 0}(f^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l},(f^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime})=(f^{\zeta^{0}},(f^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}),

and from
2.8
below it is straightforward to deduce that
\{\phi^{\varepsilon},(\phi^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}\}_{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}}
tends to

\left((\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}f^{\zeta^{0}})Y^{\zeta^{0}},(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(f^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})Y^{\zeta^{0}}+\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}f^{\zeta^{0}}\left((\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}f^{\zeta^{0}})Y^{\zeta^{0}}+(F^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\right)\right)

in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\lambda}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};V))
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
\lambda=\min\{\delta,\delta^{\prime}\}
.
Putting everything together, we have shown that
Z^{\varepsilon}
solves the following linear rough SDE:

Z_{t}^{\varepsilon}=Z^{\varepsilon}_{0}+\tilde{F}^{\varepsilon}_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}G_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Z_{r}^{\varepsilon}dr+\int_{0}^{t}S_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}Z_{r}^{\varepsilon}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(f_{r}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l},(f^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}_{r})Z_{r}^{\varepsilon}d\mathbf{W}_{r}\quad t\in[0,T]

with

\displaystyle(\tilde{F}^{\varepsilon},(\tilde{F}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(F^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-F^{\zeta^{0}})+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\mu_{r}^{\varepsilon}dr+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\nu_{r}^{\varepsilon}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\phi_{r}^{\varepsilon},(\phi^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r},\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big((F^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}-(F^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\big)+\phi^{\varepsilon}\Big).

Let us now consider
(Z^{\zeta})_{\zeta\in U}
to be family of
L_{p}
-integrable solutions to the following (well-posed) linear rough SDE, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
:

\displaystyle Z_{t}^{\zeta}=\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\xi^{\zeta}+\tilde{F}_{t}^{\zeta}+\int_{0}^{t}G_{r}^{\zeta}Z_{r}^{\zeta}dr+\int_{0}^{t}S_{r}^{\zeta}Z_{r}^{\zeta}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(f_{r}^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{r})Z_{r}^{\zeta}d\mathbf{W}_{r}\qquad t\in[0,T]

(9)

with

\displaystyle(\tilde{F}^{\zeta},(\tilde{F}^{\zeta})^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}F^{\zeta}+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\mu_{r}^{\zeta}dr+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\nu^{\zeta}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\phi^{\zeta},(\phi^{\zeta})^{\prime})d\mathbf{W}_{r},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(F^{\zeta})^{\prime}+\phi^{\zeta}\right)

(10)

\displaystyle\mu^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=(\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}G^{\zeta})Y^{\zeta}

\displaystyle\nu^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=(\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}S^{\zeta})Y^{\zeta}

\displaystyle\phi^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}f^{\zeta})Y^{\zeta}

\displaystyle(\phi^{\zeta})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})Y^{\zeta}+\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}f^{\zeta}\left((\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}f^{\zeta})Y^{\zeta}+(F^{\zeta})^{\prime}\right).

From
3.14
and
3.28
below, due to the previous considerations we have that

(Z^{\zeta^{0}},f^{\zeta^{0}}Z^{\zeta^{0}}+(\tilde{F}^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})=\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(V)\text{-}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0,\varepsilon\neq 0}(Z^{\varepsilon},f^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon^{\varepsilon}l}Z^{\varepsilon}+(\tilde{F}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}).

In other words,
\{(Z^{\zeta},f^{\zeta}+(\tilde{F}^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is the
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-derivative of
\{(Y^{\zeta},f^{\zeta}Y^{\zeta}+(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
in the direction
l
.
The
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-continuity of
Z^{\zeta}
easily follows from the assumptions, considering again
3.14
.

Let us now assume the assertion is proved for
k=k_{0}
and that the assumptions of the theorem are satisfied for
k=k_{0}+1
. It is sufficient to show that, for any arbitrary direction
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
, the family
\{(Z^{\zeta},f^{\zeta}Z^{\zeta}+(\tilde{F}^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
defined as in (
9
) is
k_{0}
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
Indeed,
Z^{\zeta}
itself solves a linear RSDE whose coefficients and initial condition are
k_{0}
-times differentiable.
Moreover, from inductive assumptions and
3.28
, it holds that
\{(\tilde{F}^{\zeta},(\tilde{F}^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k_{0}
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
Being the assertion valid for
k=k_{0}
, the proof is concluded.
∎

3.4
Differentiability for rough SDEs

Let
(\mathcal{U},|\cdot|)
be a Banach space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be a non-empty open subset.
Let
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W})\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a rough path taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2})

4
4

4

The case
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}
is not included because in the proof of
3.19
we are applying
3.18
which in turn is based on
2.15
and
3.28
.
We consider the following progressively measurable mappings:

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad(\omega,t,\zeta,x)\mapsto b_{t}(\zeta,x)

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times m},\quad(\omega,t,\zeta,x)\mapsto\sigma_{t}(\zeta,x)

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times n},\quad(\omega,t,\zeta,x)\mapsto\beta_{t}(\zeta,x)

\displaystyle\beta^{\prime}

\displaystyle:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}),\quad(\omega,t,\zeta,x)\mapsto\beta^{\prime}_{t}(\zeta,x).

We assume that, for any
\zeta\in U
, there is a
L_{p,\infty}
-integrable solution
X^{\zeta}
to the following rough SDE with
\mathcal{F}_{0}
-measurable initial condition
\xi^{\zeta}
, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
(cf.
2.11
):

X_{t}^{\zeta}=\xi^{\zeta}+\int_{0}^{t}b_{r}(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta})dr+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{r}(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta})dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(\beta_{r},\beta^{\prime}_{r})(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta})d\mathbf{W}_{r}\qquad t\in[0,T],

(11)

where
(\beta_{t},\beta^{\prime}_{t})(\zeta,X^{\zeta}_{t}):=(\beta_{t}(\zeta,X^{\zeta}_{t}),D_{x}\beta(\zeta,X^{\zeta}_{t})\beta_{t}(\zeta,X^{\zeta}_{t})+\beta^{\prime}_{t}(\zeta,X^{\zeta}_{t}))
.

Theorem 3.19
.

Let
\delta\in[0,\alpha]
such that
\alpha+\delta>\frac{1}{2}
and
\alpha+2\delta>1
, and let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
.
Assume that
\{\xi^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-differentiable. Assume that, for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega
and for any
t\in[0,T]
,

b_{t}(\cdot,\cdot)\in C^{k}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma_{t}(\cdot,\cdot)\in C^{k}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times m})

in Fréchet sense and the functions and all their (partial) derivatives up to order
k
are uniformly bounded in
(\omega,t,\zeta,x)
.
Moreover, assume

(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}C_{b}^{\kappa}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})

(12)

for some
\kappa\in[2+k,3+k]
, in the sense of
2.3
.
Then
\{(X^{\zeta},\beta(\zeta,X^{\zeta})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-differentiable.
In particular, given
\zeta\in U
and
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
and denoting by
Y^{\zeta,l}=\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta}
, it holds that, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, the latter is an
L_{p}
-integrable solution of the following linear rough SDE on
[0,T]
whose coefficients are defined as in (
15
) :

Y_{t}^{\zeta,l}=\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X_{0}^{\zeta}+F^{\zeta}_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}G_{r}^{\zeta}Y_{r}^{\zeta,l}dr+\int_{0}^{t}S_{r}^{\zeta}Y_{r}^{\zeta,l}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(f_{r}^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{r})Y_{r}^{\zeta,l}d\mathbf{W}_{r}.

Proof.

Notice that, for any
\zeta\in U
,
b(\zeta,\cdot)
is a random bounded Lipschitz vector fields with
\sup_{\zeta\in U}\|b(\zeta,\cdot)\|_{\infty}<+\infty
.
The same holds replacing
b
with
\sigma
.
From
2.4
, we deduce

(\beta(\zeta,\cdot),\beta^{\prime}(\zeta,\cdot))\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}C_{b}^{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})

with

\sup_{\zeta\in U}\left(\llbracket(\beta(\zeta,\cdot),\beta^{\prime}(\zeta,\cdot))\rrbracket_{\kappa;\infty}+\llbracket(\beta(\zeta,\cdot),\beta^{\prime}(\zeta,\cdot))\rrbracket_{W;2\delta;p,\infty;\kappa}\right)<+\infty,\quad\text{for any $p\in[2,\infty)$}.

From
2.12
it follows that

\sup_{\zeta\in U}\|(X^{\zeta},\beta(\zeta,X^{\zeta}))\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}L_{p,\infty}}<+\infty.

Moreover, the following facts hold true (recall that, by construction,
\kappa-1\geq 2
):

-

(D_{x}\beta,D_{x}\beta^{\prime})\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}C_{b}^{\kappa-1}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}))
;

-

(D_{\zeta}\beta,D_{\zeta}\beta^{\prime})\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}C_{b}^{\kappa-1}(U\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathcal{L}(U;\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}))
.

It is straight-forward to see that, due to the assumptions on
b
,
\{b(\zeta,\cdot)\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuous in the sense of
3.8
.
Similarly, we also have that
\{\sigma(\zeta,\cdot)\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times m})
-continuous.
One can also prove that
\{(\beta(\zeta,\cdot),\beta^{\prime}(\zeta,\cdot))\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\textbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C_{b}^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})
-continuous in the sense of
3.9
(notice that, even with
k=1
we have
\kappa\in[3,4]
). That is, for any
\zeta^{0}\in U
accumulation point and for any
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
such that
|\zeta^{n}-\zeta^{0}|\to_{n\to+\infty}0
,

\llbracket(\beta(\zeta^{n},\cdot)-\beta(\zeta^{0},\cdot),\beta^{\prime}(\zeta^{n},\cdot)-\beta^{\prime}(\zeta^{0},\cdot))\rrbracket_{3;p}+\llbracket\beta(\zeta^{n},\cdot),\beta^{\prime}(\zeta^{n},\cdot);\beta(\zeta^{0},\cdot),\beta^{\prime}(\zeta^{0},\cdot)\rrbracket_{W;2\delta;p;3}\to 0

(13)

as
n\to+\infty
. For example, one has that uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}_{s}(\beta_{t}(\zeta^{n},x)-\beta_{s}(\zeta^{n},x)-\beta^{\prime}_{s}(\zeta^{n},x)\delta W_{s,t}-\beta_{t}(\zeta^{0},x)-\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0},x)-\beta^{\prime}_{s}(\zeta^{0},x)\delta W_{s,t})|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}_{s}\big(D_{\zeta}\beta_{t}((1-\theta)\zeta^{0}+\theta\zeta^{n},x)-D_{\zeta}\beta_{s}((1-\theta)\zeta^{0}+\theta\zeta^{n},x)+

\displaystyle-D_{\zeta}\beta_{s}^{\prime}((1-\theta)\zeta^{0}+\theta\zeta^{n},x)\delta W_{s,t}\big)d\theta(\zeta^{n}-\zeta^{0})\Big|\leq\|\mathbb{E}_{s}(R_{s,t}^{D_{\zeta}\beta})\|_{\infty}|\zeta^{n}-\zeta^{0}|.

Similar computations apply for any other term in (
13
).
Being
\{\xi^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}

\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuous by assumption and applying
3.11
with
\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}\equiv\mathbf{W}
, we deduce that
\{(X^{\zeta},\beta(\zeta,X^{\zeta}))\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuous.

We can now prove the statement of the theorem, and we argue recursively on
k
.
First, let
k=1
. In this case
\kappa\in[3,4]
.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
and
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
be arbitrary.
For any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
, we define the following stochastic process

Y^{\varepsilon}:=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-X^{\zeta^{0}}\right).

It is sufficient to show that
\{(Y^{\varepsilon},\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\beta(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})-\beta(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}})))\}_{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}}
admits limit in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
(and the limit is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-continuous).
Let us first observe that
Y^{\varepsilon}
is the solution of a linear RSDE. Indeed, for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
we can write

\displaystyle\delta Y_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\delta X_{s,t}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-\delta X_{s,t}^{\zeta^{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{s}^{t}b_{r}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{r})-b_{r}(\zeta^{0},X_{r}^{\zeta^{0}})dr+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{s}^{t}\sigma_{r}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{r})-\sigma_{r}(\zeta^{0},X_{r}^{\zeta^{0}})dB_{r}+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{s})-\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0},X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})\right)\delta W_{s,t}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(D_{x}\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{s})\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{s})+

\displaystyle-D_{x}\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0},X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0},X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})+\beta_{s}^{\prime}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{s})-\beta_{s}^{\prime}(\zeta^{0},X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})\Big)\mathbb{W}_{s,t}+Y^{\varepsilon,\natural}_{s,t},

where
Y^{\varepsilon,\natural}_{s,t}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,\natural}_{s,t}-X^{\zeta^{0},\natural}_{s,t})
is such that
\|Y^{\varepsilon,\natural}_{s,t}\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\delta}
and
\|\mathbb{E}_{s}(Y^{\varepsilon,\natural}_{s,t})\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\delta+\delta^{\prime}}
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
.
Every term in the right-hand side of the previous identity can be re-written due to the differentiability assumptions on the coefficients, by applying the mean value theorem; by doing so the linear structure of the equation is made visible.
For simplicity, for
\theta\in[0,1]
we denote by
X^{\varepsilon}(\theta)=(1-\theta)X^{\zeta^{0}}+\theta X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}
. For instance,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{s}^{t}b_{r}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{r})-b_{r}(\zeta^{0},X_{r}^{\zeta^{0}})dr=

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}D_{\zeta}b_{r}(\zeta^{0}+\theta\varepsilon l,X^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\theta))ld\theta dr+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\int_{0}^{1}D_{x}b_{r}(\zeta^{0}+\theta\varepsilon l,X^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\theta))d\theta\right)Y^{n}_{r}dr=:\int_{s}^{t}\mu_{r}^{\varepsilon}dr+\int_{s}^{t}G_{r}^{\varepsilon}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}dr.

Applying
Lemma

3.22
to the
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}+d})
-continuous process
\{(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})\}_{\varepsilon\neq 0}
, we have that
G^{\varepsilon}
is a stochastic linear vector field, and it approaches
G:=D_{x}b(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}})
in
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
, as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Moreover, it trivially follows that there exists
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{-}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mu^{\varepsilon}=\mu
, where
\mu:=D_{\zeta}b(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}})l
. Similarly,

\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{s}^{t}\sigma_{r}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{r})-\sigma_{r}(\zeta^{0},X_{r}^{\zeta^{0}})dB_{r}=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{0}^{1}D_{\zeta}\sigma_{r}(\zeta^{0}+\theta\varepsilon l,X^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\theta))ld\theta dB_{r}+\\
+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\int_{0}^{1}D_{x}\sigma_{r}(\zeta^{0}+\theta\varepsilon l,X^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\theta))d\theta\right)Y^{\varepsilon}_{r}dB_{r}=:\int_{s}^{t}\nu_{r}^{\varepsilon}dB_{r}+\int_{s}^{t}S_{r}^{\varepsilon}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}dB_{r},

where
S^{\varepsilon}
is a random bounded linear vector field approaching
S:=D_{x}\sigma(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}})
in
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times m})
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, and
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d\times m})\text{-}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\nu^{\varepsilon}=\nu
, with
\nu:=D_{\zeta}\sigma(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}})l
. The remaining terms can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{s})-\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0},X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}D_{\zeta}\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\theta\varepsilon l,X^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\theta))ld\theta+\left(\int_{0}^{1}D_{x}\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\theta\varepsilon l,X^{\varepsilon}_{r}(\theta))d\theta\right)Y_{s}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=:\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}+f_{s}^{\varepsilon}Y_{s}^{\varepsilon},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\beta^{\prime}_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{s})-\beta^{\prime}_{s}(\zeta^{0},X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}D_{\zeta}\beta^{\prime}_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\theta\varepsilon l,X^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\theta))ld\theta+\left(\int_{0}^{1}D_{x}\beta^{\prime}_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\theta\varepsilon l,X^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\theta))d\theta\right)Y_{s}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=:(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{2})^{\prime}_{s}+(f^{\varepsilon}_{2})^{\prime}_{s}Y_{s}^{\varepsilon}

and, applying the mean value theorem to the function
(x,y,z)\mapsto D_{y}\beta(x,y)z
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(D_{x}\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{s})\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{s})-D_{x}\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0},X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0},X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})\right)=

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}D^{2}_{\zeta x}\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\theta\varepsilon l,X_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\theta)((1-\theta)\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0},X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})+\theta\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}),l)d\theta+

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{0}^{1}D^{2}_{xx}\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\theta\varepsilon l,X_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\theta)((1-\theta)\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0},X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}})+\theta\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}),\cdot)d\theta\right)Y_{s}^{\varepsilon}+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{1}D_{x}\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\theta\varepsilon l,X_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\theta))d\theta)(\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})-\beta_{s}(\zeta^{0},X_{s}^{\zeta^{0}}))d\theta

\displaystyle=:(\phi_{1}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}_{s}+(f_{1}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}_{s}Y_{s}^{\varepsilon}+f_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{s}+f_{s}^{\varepsilon}Y_{s}^{\varepsilon}).

Notice that, as a general fact, we can define
\bar{X}^{\zeta}:=(\zeta,X^{\zeta})\in U\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
and we get that
\delta\bar{X}^{\zeta}=(0,\delta X^{\zeta})
.
This implies that, from the point of view of stochastic sewing, we can identify
\bar{X}^{\zeta}
as a
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued stochastic controlled rough path with
(\bar{X}^{\zeta})^{\prime}=(0,\beta(\zeta,X^{\zeta}))
.
With a little abuse of notation, we keep writing
((\zeta,X^{\zeta}),\beta(\zeta,X^{\zeta}))
instead of
((\zeta,X^{\zeta}),(0,\beta(\zeta,X^{\zeta})))
.
Applying
Lemma

3.27
to the
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-continuous family of stochastic controlled rough path
\{(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}),\beta(\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l,X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})\}_{\varepsilon\neq 0}
, it follows that

(f^{\varepsilon},(f^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}:=(f^{\varepsilon}_{1})^{\prime}+(f^{\varepsilon}_{2})^{\prime})\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}),

for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and it approaches
(f,f^{\prime}):=(D_{x}\beta(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}}),D^{2}_{xx}\beta(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}})\beta(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}})+D_{x}\beta^{\prime}(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}}))
in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\lambda}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, for any
\lambda\in[0,\delta)
.
In particular, being
\alpha+2\delta>1
, we can choose such
\lambda
so that it satisfies
\alpha+\delta+\lambda>1
.
Moreover, it also follows that
(\phi^{\varepsilon},(\phi^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}:=(\phi_{1}^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}+(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{2})^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})
and it tends to
(\phi,\phi^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\lambda}\mathscr{L}
, where

(\phi,\phi^{\prime}):=\left(D_{\zeta}\beta(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}})l,D^{2}_{x\zeta}\beta(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}})(\beta(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}}),l)+D_{\zeta}\beta^{\prime}(\zeta^{0},X^{\zeta^{0}})l\right).

Therefore we have shown that
Y^{\varepsilon}
solves

Y^{\varepsilon}_{t}=Y^{\varepsilon}_{0}+F^{\varepsilon}_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}G_{r}^{\varepsilon}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}dr+\int_{0}^{t}S_{r}^{\varepsilon}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(f^{\varepsilon}_{r}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon},(f^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}_{r}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}+f_{r}^{\varepsilon}(f_{r}^{\varepsilon}Y_{r}^{\varepsilon}+(F^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}_{r}))d\mathbf{W}_{r}

with
Y^{\varepsilon}_{0}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(X_{0}^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-X_{0}^{\zeta^{0}})
and
(F^{\varepsilon},(F^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})=\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}\mu_{r}^{\varepsilon}dr+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\nu_{r}^{\varepsilon}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\phi^{\varepsilon}_{r},(\phi^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r},\phi^{\varepsilon}\right).

Let us now define, for any
\zeta\in U
,
Y^{\zeta}=(Y^{\zeta}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
to be the solution of the following (well-posed) linear SDE

Y_{t}^{\zeta}=\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X_{0}^{\zeta}+F^{\zeta}_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}G_{r}^{\zeta}Y_{r}^{\zeta}dr+\int_{0}^{t}S_{r}^{\zeta}Y_{r}^{\zeta}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(f_{r}^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{r})Y_{r}^{\zeta}d\mathbf{W}_{r},

(14)

with

\displaystyle G_{r}^{\zeta}

\displaystyle=D_{x}b_{r}(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta})\qquad\qquad S_{r}^{\zeta}=D_{x}\sigma_{r}(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta})

(15)

\displaystyle(f_{r}^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{r})

\displaystyle=(D_{x}\beta_{r}(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta}),D^{2}_{xx}\beta_{r}(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta})\beta_{r}(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta})+D_{x}\beta^{\prime}_{r}(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta}))

\displaystyle(F^{\zeta},(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}\mu^{\zeta}_{r}dr+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\nu^{\zeta}_{r}dB_{t}+\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\phi^{\zeta}_{r},(\phi^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r},\phi^{\zeta}\right)

\displaystyle\mu^{\zeta}_{r}

\displaystyle=D_{\zeta}b_{r}(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta})l\qquad\qquad\nu^{\zeta}_{r}=D_{\zeta}\sigma_{r}(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta})l

\displaystyle(\phi^{\zeta}_{r},(\phi^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{r})

\displaystyle=(D_{\zeta}\beta_{r}(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta})l,D^{2}_{x\zeta}\beta_{s}(\zeta,X_{r}^{\zeta})(\beta_{s}(\zeta,X_{s}^{\zeta}),l)+D_{\zeta}\beta^{\prime}_{s}(\zeta,X_{s}^{\zeta})l).

By construction, we have that

\sup_{\zeta\in U}\left(\|G^{\zeta}\|_{\infty}+\|S^{\zeta}\|_{\infty}+\llbracket(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};\infty}+\llbracket(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{W;\delta,\delta^{\prime};p,\infty;\mathcal{L}}\right)<+\infty.

The previous considerations and
3.14
allow us to conclude that

(Y^{\zeta^{0}},f^{\zeta^{0}}Y^{\zeta^{0}}+(F^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})=\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{-}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}(Y^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon}+(F^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}).

In other words, due to the arbitrariety of
\zeta^{0}
, we have that
\{(Y^{\zeta},f^{\zeta}Y^{\zeta}+(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is the (family of)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-derivatives of
\{(X^{\zeta},\beta(\zeta,X^{\zeta})\}_{\zeta\in U}
in the direction
l
.

Assume now that all the assumptions about the continuity of the derivatives hold. Being
\{(\zeta,X^{\zeta})\}_{\zeta\in U}

\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-continuous by contruction, we apply again
3.14
to have that
\{(Y^{\zeta},f^{\zeta}Y^{\zeta}+(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuous.
Indeed, from
3.20
it follows that
\{G^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U},\{S^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
are
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuous, and
\{\mu^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U},\{\nu^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
are
\mathscr{L}
-continuous.
Moreover, from
3.25
it follows that
\{(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\lambda}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuous, and
\{(\phi^{\zeta},(\phi^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\lambda}\mathscr{L}
-continuous.
This in particular implies - taking into account
3.28
- that
\{(F^{\zeta},(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-continuous.

Suppose now the theorem has been proved for
k=k_{0}
and that, in addition, all the assumptions are satisfied for
k=k_{0}+1
. In this case
\kappa-1\in[2+k_{0},3+k_{0}]
.
It is enough to show that, for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
, the family
\{(Y^{\zeta},f^{\zeta}Y^{\zeta}+(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
of stochastic controlled rough paths, defined according to (
14
), is
k_{0}
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
By inductive assumption,
\{\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X_{0}^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k_{0}
-times (
\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
Moreover,
\{((\zeta,X^{\zeta}),\beta(\zeta,X^{\zeta}))\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k_{0}
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-differentiable as well.
Due to the assumptions on
b,\sigma
, applying
3.20
we have that
\{G^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
and
\{S^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
are
k_{0}
-times (
\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
Thanks to the assumptions on
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})
, from
3.25
we can deduce that
\{(f^{\zeta},(f^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k_{0}
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\lambda}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\delta,\lambda}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable, for any
\lambda\in[0,\lambda)
.
A similar thing also holds for the
k_{0}
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuous)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiability of
\{(F^{\zeta},(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
, taking into account
3.28
.
The recursive assertion is therefore proved, due to
3.18
.
∎

3.5
Continuity and differentiability of composition and integration

In this section we collect a series of auxiliary result, which are extensively used in the previous sections.
Applications also appear in
Section

4
.
At the start of
Section

3
we introduced suitable notions of continuity and differentiability for a family
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued stochastic processes depending on a parameter.
These notions were later refined to accommodate the framework of rough stochastic analysis developed in
[
FHL21
]
.
Before applying these concepts to solutions of parameter-dependent rough SDEs, we verify their compatibility with key operations—namely composition and integration—by addressing the following questions:

-

If
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
suitably continuous (differentiable) with respect to
\zeta
, is
\{g(X^{\zeta})\}_{\zeta\in U}
still continuous (differentiable) for a given function
g
? Here
g
represents a (possibly random) vector field.
If
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta}\subseteq\mathscr{L}
in the sense of
3.1
, this is addressed in
3.20
and suitably generalized in
Lemma

3.22
and
3.23
.
If instead
X^{\zeta}
is a stochastic controlled rough path (cf.
3.6
) the relevant result is
3.25
, where
g
is assumed to be a stochastic controlled vector field.
Continuity and differentiability are also shown to be preserved under multiplication; see
3.24
.

-

If
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is suitably continuous (differentiable) with respect to
\zeta
, do the Lebesgue integral
\int_{0}^{\cdot}X^{\zeta}_{r}dr
, the Itô integral
\int_{0}^{\cdot}X^{\zeta}_{r}dB_{r}
and the rough stochastic integral
\int_{0}^{\cdot}X^{\zeta}_{r}d\mathbf{W}_{r}
inherit the same regularity? This is answered in
3.28

Let
V
be a finite-dimensional vector space (think of ,
V=\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
for some
d_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
).

Proposition 3.20
.

(see
[
Kry08
, Lemma 1.7.6]
and
[
Kry08
, Theorem 1.9.2 (b)]
)
Let
g:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to V
be a progressively measurable map such that, for any
t\in[0,T]
and for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega
,
\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto g_{t}(x):=g(\omega,t,x)\in V
is continuous.
Moreover, assume that there exist
K>0,m\geq 1
such that

\mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{\omega}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|g_{t}(x)|\leq K(1+|x|^{m})\quad\text{for any $x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$}.

1.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\rho)
be a metric space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be non-empty.
Let
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a family of
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuous stochastic processes. Then
\{g(X^{\zeta})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}(V)
-continuous

2.

Let
(\mathcal{U},|\cdot|)
be a normed vector space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be a non-empty open subset.
Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
and let
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a family of
k
-times (
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-differentiable stochastic processes.
Assume that, for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
\omega
and for any
t\in[0,T]
,
g(\omega,t,\cdot)\in C^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
, and all the derivatives up to order
k
do not exceed
K(1+|x|^{m})
.
Then
\{g(X^{\zeta})\}_{\zeta\in P}
is
k
-times (
\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
In particular, for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
,

\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}g(X^{\zeta})=D_{x}g(X^{\zeta})\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta}.

Remark 3.21
.

The previous proposition provides a criterion to construct
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuous (
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiable) stochastic linear vector fields, in the sense of
3.12
(
3.16
).
Indeed, if
g
is a random bounded vector field from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
V=\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}};\bar{V})
, then
3.20
ensures that
\{g(X^{\zeta})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}};\bar{V})
-continuous and/or
k
-times (
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}};\bar{V})
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}};\bar{V})
-differentiable.

Lemma 3.22
.

Let
g:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},V)
be a random bounded continuous vector field. Let
(\mathcal{U},|\cdot|)
be a normed vector space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be a non-empty open subset.
Let
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuous.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
and
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
be fixed.
For any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}
define

\displaystyle Q^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}g(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta))\,d\theta:=\int_{0}^{1}g((1-\theta)X^{\zeta^{0}}+\theta X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})\,d\theta

Then
\{Q^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}}
is a family of stochastic linear vector fields from
\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
to
V
, and it tends to
Q:=g(X^{\zeta^{0}})
in
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

Proof.

Notice that
Q^{\varepsilon}=\int_{0}^{1}g(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta))d\theta
is well-defined as a stochastic linear vector field from
\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
to
V
since
(g_{t}(X^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)))_{t\in[0,T]}
is progressively measurable, uniformly bounded and its dependence in
\theta
is continuous (cf.
3.21
).
The same holds for
Q
.
For any
p\in[1,\infty)
and for any
(\varepsilon^{n})_{n}\subseteq\mathbb{R}
converging to 0 as
n\to+\infty
,

\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|Q^{\varepsilon^{n}}_{r}-Q_{r}|^{p}\right)\leq\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|g(X^{\varepsilon^{n}}(\theta))-g(X^{\zeta^{0}})|^{p}\right)d\theta.

By continuous mapping theorem,
g(X^{\varepsilon^{n}}(\theta))
converges to
g(X^{\zeta^{0}})
in measure
\mathbb{P}\otimes\lambda
, as
n\to+\infty
, and being
g
uniformly bounded in
(\omega,t,x)
, a double application of Lebesgue’s dominated convergence shows that
Q=\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\text{-}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}Q^{\varepsilon}
.
∎

Proposition 3.23
.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\rho)
be a metric space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be non-empty.
Let
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuous family of stochastic processes and let
\{g^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
-continuous family of random bounded vector fields.
Then
\{g^{\zeta}(X^{\zeta})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}(V)
-continuous.

Proof.

By construction,
(g^{\zeta}_{t}(X^{\zeta}_{t}))_{t\in[0,T]}
is a progressively measurable stochastic process.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
be an accumulation point, and let
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
be any arbitrary sequence such that
\rho(\zeta^{n},\zeta^{0})\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
.
For any
n\geq 1
and for any
p\in[1,\infty)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|g^{\zeta^{n}}_{r}(X^{\zeta^{n}}_{r})-g^{\zeta^{0}}_{r}(X^{\zeta^{0}}_{r})|^{p}dr\right)\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|g^{\zeta^{n}}_{r}(\cdot)-g^{\zeta^{0}}_{r}(\cdot)|_{\infty}^{p}dr\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|g^{\zeta^{0}}_{r}(X^{\zeta^{n}}_{r})-g^{\zeta^{0}}_{r}(X^{\zeta^{0}}_{r})|^{p}dr\right).

The first term on the right-hand side tends to 0 as
n\to+\infty
by assumption. The second term tends to 0, by applying
3.20
.
∎

Proposition 3.24
.

Let
W\in C^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
, with
\alpha\in(0,1]
, and let
h:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to V
be three-times differentiable such that there exist
K>0
and
m\geq 1
with

|D_{x}h(x)|+|D^{2}_{xx}h(x)|+|D^{3}_{xxx}h(x)|\leq K(1+|x|^{m})\quad\text{for any $x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$}.

Let
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in[0,1]
.

1.

Let
(X,X^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, with
X_{0}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then
(h(X),D_{x}h(X)X^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(V)
.

2.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\rho)
be a metric space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be non-empty. Let
\{(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
be
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuous.
Then
\{(h(X^{\zeta}),D_{x}h(X^{\zeta})(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(V)
-continuous.

3.

Let
(\mathcal{U},|\cdot|)
be a normed vector space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be a non-empty open subset.
Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
and assume
h\in C^{2+k}_{p}(E,\bar{E})
.
Let
\{(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be
k
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\bar{\alpha}}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\bar{\alpha}}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable. Then
\{(h(X^{\zeta}),D_{x}h(X^{\zeta})(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\bar{\alpha}}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\bar{\alpha}}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable, for any
\bar{\alpha}\in(0,\alpha)
.

Proof.

1.
It follows from
[
BCN24
, Proposition 2.13]
.

2.
If
h\in C^{3}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
, the result would follow from
2.5
because, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, we would have that

\|h(X^{\zeta^{n}}),D_{x}h(X^{\zeta^{n}})(X^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime};h(X^{\zeta^{0}}),D_{x}h(X^{\zeta^{0}})(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\|_{W;\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p}\lesssim\|X^{\zeta^{n}},(X^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime};X^{\zeta^{0}},(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\|_{W;\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p}

(16)

for any
\zeta^{0}\in U
accumulation point and any
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
such that
\rho(\zeta^{n},\zeta^{0})\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
, where the implicit constant is uniform in
n
.
2.5
can be easily generalized to vector fields of polynomial growth. One need to consider the following useful elementary estimate : given any
g\in C^{2}(V,\bar{V})
such that
|D_{x}g(x)|+|D^{2}_{xx}g(x)|\leq K(1+|x|)^{m}
and given any arbitrary
a,b,c,d\in V
, an application of the mean value theorem shows that

|g(a)-g(b)-g(c)+g(d)|\leq K(1+|a|+|b|+|c|+|d|)^{m}\big((|a-c|+|b-d|)|c-d|+|a-b-c+d|\big).

(17)

For instance, for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
it follows that

\|h(X^{\zeta^{n}}_{t})-h(X^{\zeta^{n}}_{s})-h(X^{\zeta^{0}}_{t})+h(X^{\zeta^{0}}_{s})\|_{p}\lesssim_{K,m,p,M(4mp)}(1+\|\delta X^{\zeta^{0}}\|_{\gamma;2p})\|X^{\zeta^{n}},(X^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime};X^{\zeta^{0}},(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\|_{W;\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};4p}|t-s|^{\gamma}

where
M=M(q)>0
is any constant such that
\sup_{n\geq 0}\|(X^{\zeta^{n}},(X^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{q}}\leq M(q)
.
Estimates for the remaining quantities can be obtained by exploiting (
17
) while retracing the proof of
2.5
for the deterministic controlled vector field
(h,0)
; it is thus possible to conclude that (
16
) also holds when
h
is of polynomial growth, with an implicit constant depending on
K,T,p,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
and
M(4mp)
.

3.
We argue recursively on
k
. Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
,
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
and
p\in[2,\infty)
be arbitrary.
For any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
we can write

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(h(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})-h(X^{\zeta^{0}})),\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big(D_{x}h(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}-D_{x}h(X^{\zeta^{0}})(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\big)\right)=(f^{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon},(f^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}Y^{\varepsilon}+f^{\varepsilon}(Y^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}),

where, denoting by
(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta),X^{\varepsilon}(\theta)^{\prime})
as in (
20
),

\displaystyle(f^{\varepsilon},(f^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=\left(\int_{0}^{1}D_{x}h(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta))d\theta,\int_{0}^{1}D^{2}_{xx}h(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta))(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta)^{\prime},\cdot)d\theta\right)

\displaystyle(Y^{\varepsilon},(Y^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-X^{\zeta^{0}}),\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}-(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\big)\right).

From the estimates in
2.8
and using a very similar argument as in
Lemma

3.27
, it is straightforward to deduce that
\{(f^{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon},(f^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}Y^{\varepsilon}+f^{\varepsilon}(Y^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})\}_{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}}
converges to

\left(D_{x}h(X^{\zeta^{0}})\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta^{0}},D^{2}_{xx}h(X^{\zeta^{0}})\left(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta^{0}}\right)+D_{x}h(X^{\zeta^{0}})\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\right).

in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(V)
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
For any
\zeta\in U
, we define

\displaystyle Z^{\zeta}

\displaystyle:=D_{x}h(X^{\zeta})\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta}

\displaystyle(Z^{\zeta})^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=D^{2}_{xx}h(X^{\zeta})\left(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(X^{\zeta})^{\prime},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta}\right)+D_{x}h(X^{\zeta})\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(X^{\zeta})^{\prime}.

It is straightformward to show that
\{(Z^{\zeta},(Z^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-continuous, and this proves the claim in the case
k=1
.

Let us now assume that the assertion is proved for
k=k_{0}
, and that all the assumptions hold for
k=k_{0}+1
.
By recursive hypothesis, it can be easily shown that
\{(Z^{\zeta},(Z^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k_{0}
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
Indeed, we can always see
(Z^{\zeta},(Z^{\zeta})^{\prime})
as
(g(Y^{\zeta}),D_{x}g(Y^{\zeta})(Y^{\zeta})^{\prime})
where

g(y):=D_{x}h(y^{1})y^{2}\qquad y=(y^{1},y^{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}

is still a function of polynomial growth, and
Y^{\zeta}=(X^{\zeta},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta})
is the stochastic controlled rough path in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
, whose Gubinelli derivative is defined through the (linear) action

(Y^{\zeta})^{\prime}x:=\left((X^{\zeta})^{\prime}x,(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta})x\right)\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

∎

Proposition 3.25
.

Let by
\alpha,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\delta\in[0,1]
, with
\alpha\neq 0
. Denote by
\lambda=\min\{\gamma,\delta\}
and
\lambda^{\prime}=\min\{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\delta\}
.

1.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\rho)
be a metric space and let
o\in U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
.
Let
\{{W}^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}\subseteq C^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a collection of
\alpha
-Hölder continuous paths in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and let
\{(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a family of
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\}_{\zeta}
-continuous stochastic controlled rough paths such that

\sup_{\zeta\in U:\ \rho(\zeta,o)\leq R}\|(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}}<+\infty,\quad\text{for any $R>0$ and for any $p\in[2,\infty)$}.

Let
\kappa\in[2,3]
, and let
\{(g^{\zeta},(g^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{2\delta}\mathscr{L}C_{b}^{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)\}_{\zeta}
-continuous family of stochastic controlled vector fields such that

\sup_{\zeta\in U:\ \rho(\zeta,o)\leq R}\left(\llbracket(g^{\zeta},(g^{\zeta})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\kappa;\infty}+\llbracket(g^{\zeta},(g^{\zeta})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{W^{\zeta};2\delta;p,\infty;\kappa}\right)<+\infty,\quad\text{for any $R>0$ and for any $p\in[2,\infty)$}.

For any
\zeta\in U
, define

(Z^{\zeta},(Z^{\zeta})^{\prime}):=(g^{\zeta}(X^{\zeta}),D_{x}g^{\zeta}(X^{\zeta})(X^{\zeta})^{\prime}+(g^{\zeta})^{\prime}(X^{\zeta})).

(18)

Then
\{(Z^{\zeta},(Z^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\lambda,\eta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(V)\}_{\zeta}
-continuous for any
\eta^{\prime}\in[0,\lambda^{\prime})
;
while it is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(V)\}_{\zeta}
-continuous if
\kappa=3
.

2.

Let
(\mathcal{U},|\cdot|)
be a normed vector space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be a non-empty open subset. Let
W:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an
\alpha
-Hölder continuous path. Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
. Assume that
\{(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable with

\sup_{|\zeta|\leq R}\|(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}}<+\infty,\quad\text{for any $R>0$ and for any $p\in[2,\infty)$}.

Assume

(g,g^{\prime})\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}C^{\kappa}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V),

for some
\kappa\in[2+k,3+k]
.
Then the family
\{(Z^{\zeta},(Z^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
of stochastic controlled rough paths defined as in (
18
) with
(g^{\zeta},(g^{\zeta})^{\prime})=(g,g^{\prime})
is
(k-1)
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(V)
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,{\lambda}^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(V)
-differentiable. Moreover, all its
(k-1)
-th
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,{\lambda^{\prime}}}\mathscr{L}
-derivatives are (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\eta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(V)
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\eta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(V)
-differentiable for any
\eta^{\prime}\in[0,\lambda^{\prime})
; such derivatives are (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(V)
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(V)
-differentiable if
\kappa=3+k
.
In particular, for any
\zeta\in U
and for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\eta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(Z^{\zeta},(Z^{\zeta})^{\prime})=

\displaystyle=\left(D_{x}g(X^{\zeta})\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta},D^{2}_{xx}g(X^{\zeta})\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta}+D_{x}g^{\prime}(X^{\zeta})\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(X^{\zeta})^{\prime}\right).

Proof.

1.
The assertion follows from a straightforward application of
2.5
, together with an interpolation argument (see
2.17
).

2.
We argue by recursion on
k
.
Let us first assume
k=1
and let us denote by
\eta=\min\{(\kappa-3)\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\delta\}
.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
and
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
be arbitrary.
For any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
, we define

\displaystyle(Y^{\varepsilon},(Y^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-X^{\zeta^{0}}),\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big((X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}-(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\big)\right).

By construction,
\{(Y^{\varepsilon},(Y^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})\}_{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}}
tends to
(Y,Y^{\prime}):=(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta^{0}},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Denoting by
(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta),X^{\varepsilon}(\theta)^{\prime})=((1-\theta)X^{\zeta^{0}}+\theta X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l},(1-\theta)(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}+\theta(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}),
by means of the mean value theorem we can write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(Z^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-Z^{\zeta^{0}})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}D_{x}g(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta))d\theta\ Y^{\varepsilon}=:h^{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}((Z^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}-(Z^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{0}^{1}D^{2}_{xx}g(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta))(\cdot,X^{\varepsilon}(\theta)^{\prime})d\theta+\int_{0}^{1}D_{x}g^{\prime}(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta))d\theta\right)Y^{\varepsilon}+\int_{0}^{1}D_{x}g(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta))d\theta\ (Y^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:(h^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}Y^{\varepsilon}+h^{\varepsilon}(Y^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}

From
Lemma

3.27
and being
(D_{x}g,D_{x}g^{\prime})\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}C_{b}^{\kappa-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V))
by construction, it holds that
\{(h^{\varepsilon},(h^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})\}_{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}}\subseteq\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V)
, and it tends to

(h,h^{\prime}):=(D_{x}g(X^{\zeta^{0}}),D^{2}_{xx}g(X^{\zeta^{0}})(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}+D_{x}g^{\prime}(X^{\zeta^{0}}))

in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\eta}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Whenever

, by interpolation the previous convergence also holds by replacing
\eta
with any
\eta^{\prime}\in[0,\lambda^{\prime})
. From
[

, Proposition 3.3]
, we know that
\{(h^{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon},(h^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}Y^{\varepsilon}+h^{\varepsilon}(Y^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})\}_{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}}\subseteq\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}(V)
for any

and from

it is straightforward to conclude that

\displaystyle\|h^{\varepsilon^{n}}Y^{\varepsilon^{n}},(h^{\varepsilon^{n}})^{\prime}Y^{\varepsilon^{n}}+h^{\varepsilon^{n}}(Y^{\varepsilon^{n}})^{\prime};hY,h^{\prime}Y+hY^{\prime}\|_{W;\lambda,\eta;p}\to 0

as

. Such a convergence also holds by replacing

, by interpolation and whenever

.
In other words, denoting by
(Y^{\zeta},(Y^{\zeta})^{\prime}):=(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})
and by
(h^{\zeta},(h^{\zeta})^{\prime}):=(D_{x}g(X^{\zeta}),D^{2}_{xx}g(X^{\zeta})(X^{\zeta})^{\prime}+D_{x}g^{\prime}(X^{\zeta}))
we proved that, for any

,

\{(h^{\zeta}Y^{\zeta},(h^{\zeta})^{\prime}Y^{\zeta}+h^{\zeta}(Y^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}

(19)

is the

-derivative of

.
From

-continuous, provided that

-continuous.
Both of the previous differentiability and continuity results also hold for

, if

.

Let us now assume that the assertion is proved for

and that all the assumptions hold for

.

) is necessarily the

-derivative of

in the (arbitrary) direction

.
By assumption, we have that

and

is

-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-differentiable.
Moreover, being and
(D_{x}g,D_{x}g^{\prime})\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}C^{\kappa-1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};V))
, the recursive assumption leads to the fact that
\{D_{x}g(X^{\zeta}),D^{2}_{xx}g(X^{\zeta})(X^{\zeta})^{\prime}+D_{x}g^{\prime}(X^{\zeta})\}_{\zeta\in U}

-continuously)

;

By applying

Remark 3.26
.

The previous proposition directly yields continuity and differentiability results for a class of stochastic controlled linear vector fields.
Indeed, for any given finite dimensional Banach space
\bar{V}
, applying
3.25
in the case
V=\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}};\bar{V})
shows that
\{(g(X^{\zeta}),D_{x}g(X^{\zeta})(X^{\zeta})^{\prime}+g^{\prime}(X^{\zeta}))\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\eta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuous.
Similarly, we can deduce results about its
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\eta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-differentiability.

Lemma 3.27
.

Let
(\mathcal{U},|\cdot|)
be a normed vector space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be a non-empty open subset.
Let
W\in C^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
, with
\alpha\in(0,1]
, let
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\delta\in[0,1]
and denote by
\lambda=\min\{\gamma,\delta\}
,
\lambda^{\prime}=\min\{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\delta\}
.
Let

(g,g^{\prime})\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\delta}L_{p,\infty}C_{b}^{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}};V)),

for some
\kappa\in[2,3]
, and let
\{(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuous family of progressively measurable stochastic controlled rough paths such that

\sup_{|\zeta|\leq R}\|(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}}<+\infty\qquad\text{for any $R>0$ and for any $p\in[2,\infty)$}.

Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
and
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
be fixed. For any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}
, define

\displaystyle(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta),X^{\varepsilon}(\theta)^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=(1-\theta)(X^{\zeta^{0}},(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})+\theta(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l},(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime})\qquad\theta\in[0,1]

(20)

\displaystyle(f^{\varepsilon},(f^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=\left(\int_{0}^{1}g(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta))d\theta,\int_{0}^{1}D_{x}g(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta))X^{\varepsilon}(\theta)^{\prime}+g^{\prime}(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta))d\theta\right)

Then
\{(f^{\varepsilon},(f^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})\}_{\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}}\subseteq\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}};V)
, and

\sup_{|\varepsilon|\leq R}\left(\llbracket(f^{\varepsilon},(f^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};\infty}+\llbracket(f^{\varepsilon},(f^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{W;\lambda,\lambda^{\prime};p,\infty;\mathcal{L}}\right)<+\infty\quad\text{for any $R>0$ and for any $p\in[2,\infty)$}.

Moreover, it tends to
(f,f^{\prime}):=(g(X^{\zeta^{0}}),D_{x}g(X^{\zeta^{0}})(X^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}+g^{\prime}(X^{\zeta^{0}}))
in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\eta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}};V)
as
\varepsilon\to 0
for any
\eta^{\prime}\in[0,\lambda^{\prime})
, and the convergence in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}};V)
holds if
\kappa=3
.

Proof.

For simplicity, write
(f^{\varepsilon},(f^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})=(\int_{0}^{1}f^{\varepsilon}(\theta)d\theta,\int_{0}^{1}f^{\varepsilon}(\theta)^{\prime}d\theta)
.
By construction,
(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta),X^{\varepsilon}(\theta)^{\prime})\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}
,
\theta\in[0,1]
and, for any
R>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{|\varepsilon|\leq R}\sup_{\theta\in[0,1]}\|(X^{\varepsilon}(\theta),X^{\varepsilon}(\theta)^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{|\zeta|\leq|\zeta^{0}|+R}\|(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}}<+\infty.

Notice that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)\|_{\infty}\leq\llbracket(g,g^{\prime})\rrbracket_{0;\infty}<+\infty

Hence, by
2.5
and by considering
3.26
it holds that
(f^{\varepsilon}(\theta),f^{\varepsilon}(\theta)^{\prime})\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}}L_{p,\infty}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}};V)
.
Notice that, as a general fact, given any stochastic process
A(\theta)=(A_{s,t}(\theta))_{(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}}
such that the dependence on
\theta
is
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. continuous,

\|\int_{0}^{1}A_{s,t}(\theta)d\theta|\mathcal{F}_{s}\|_{p}\leq\left(\int_{0}^{1}\|A_{s,t}(\theta)d\theta|\mathcal{F}_{s}\|_{p}^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s}.

This allows us to conclude that, for any
R>0
and for any
p\in[2,\infty)
,

\sup_{|\varepsilon|\leq R}\left(\llbracket(f^{\varepsilon},(f^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};\infty}+\llbracket(f^{\varepsilon},(f^{\varepsilon})^{\prime})\rrbracket_{W;\lambda,\lambda^{\prime};p,\infty;\mathcal{L}}\right)\\
\leq\sup_{|\varepsilon|\leq R}\sup_{\theta\in[0,1]}\left(\llbracket(f^{\varepsilon}(\theta),f^{\varepsilon}(\theta)^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};\infty}+\llbracket(f^{\varepsilon}(\theta),f^{\varepsilon}(\theta)^{\prime})\rrbracket_{W;\lambda,{\lambda^{\prime}};p,\infty}\right)<+\infty.

From
3.26
and
2.5
and being
\{(X^{\zeta},(X^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}

\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-continuous, it follows that, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
and for any arbitrary sequence
(\varepsilon^{n})_{n}\subseteq\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
converging to zero,

\llbracket f^{\varepsilon^{n}},(f^{\varepsilon^{n}})^{\prime};f,f^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{L};p}+\llbracket f^{\varepsilon^{n}},(f^{\varepsilon^{n}})^{\prime};f,f^{\prime}\rrbracket_{W;\lambda,\eta;p}\to 0

as
n\to+\infty
, where
\eta=\min\{(\kappa-2)\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\delta\}
.
The conclusion follows by interpolation (see
2.17
), in a similar way to how it is done in the proof of
3.25
.
∎

Proposition 3.28
.

Let
\mathbf{W}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a rough path, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2})
, and let
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in[0,1]
be such that
\alpha+\gamma>\frac{1}{2}
and
\alpha+\min\{\alpha,\gamma\}+\gamma^{\prime}>1
.

1.

Let
\mu\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\nu\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d\times m})
be two stochastic processes. Let
(\phi,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})
with
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\phi_{t}\|_{p}<+\infty
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
.
For any
t\in[0,T]
define

\displaystyle F_{t}

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\mu_{r}dr+\int_{0}^{t}\nu_{r}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(\phi_{r},\phi^{\prime}_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}

\displaystyle F_{t}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\phi_{t}.

Then
(F,F^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\gamma}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and, for any
p>\frac{2}{1-2\alpha}
,

\|(F,F^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\gamma}L_{p}}\lesssim_{\alpha,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},p,T,|\mathbf{W}|_{\alpha}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\mu_{r}|^{p}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\nu_{r}|^{p}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\phi_{t}\|_{p}+\|(\phi,\phi^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p}}.

2.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\rho)
be a metric space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be non-empty, and let
\{\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}=(W^{\zeta},\mathbb{W}^{\zeta})\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-continuous family of rough paths.
Let
\{\mu^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
-continuous family of stochastic processes and let
\{\nu^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d\times m})
-continuous.
Let
\{(\phi^{\zeta},(\phi^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
be
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})\}_{\zeta}
-continuous and assume that, for any
\zeta\in U
and for any
p\in[2,\infty)
,
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\phi^{\zeta}_{t}\|_{p}<+\infty
.
For any
\zeta\in U
, define

(F^{\zeta},(F^{\zeta})^{\prime}):=\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}\mu^{\zeta}_{r}dr+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\nu^{\zeta}_{r}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\phi^{\zeta}_{r},(\phi^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{\zeta}_{r},\phi^{\zeta}\right).

Then
\{(F^{\zeta},(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta}}^{\alpha,\gamma}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\}_{\zeta}
-continuous.

3.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
and assume
(\mathcal{U},|\cdot|)
is a normed vector space.
Assume that
\{\mu^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
and
\{\nu^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
are both
k
-times (
\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
Assume that
\{(\phi^{\zeta},(\phi^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
Then
\{(F^{\zeta},(F^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\gamma}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\gamma}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
In particular, for any
\zeta\in U
and for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
,

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\gamma}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}F^{\zeta},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\gamma}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(F^{\zeta})^{\prime}\right)=

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\mu^{\zeta}_{r}dr+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\nu^{\zeta}_{r}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\left(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\phi^{\zeta}_{r},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(\phi^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{r}\right)d\mathbf{W}_{r},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\phi^{\zeta}\right).

Proof.

1.
From the estimates on rough stochastic integrals in
2.10
, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
it holds that that

\left\|\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\phi_{r},\phi^{\prime}_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r},\phi\right)\right\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\gamma}L_{p}}\lesssim_{\alpha,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},p,T,|\mathbf{W}|_{\alpha}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\phi_{t}\|_{p}+\|\phi,\phi^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{p}}.

Moreover, a simple application of Hölder’s and BDG inequalities shows that, for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{s}^{t}\mu_{r}dr\right\|_{p}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}\mu_{r}dr\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq|t-s|^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\mu_{r}|^{p}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

and

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{s}^{t}\nu_{r}dB_{r}\right\|_{p}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}\nu_{r}dB_{r}\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim_{p}\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\int_{s}^{t}|\nu_{r}|^{2}dr\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq|t-s|^{\frac{p-2}{2p}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\nu_{r}|^{p}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

Under the assumption
p>\frac{2}{1-2\alpha}
, we have
\frac{p-1}{p}>\alpha+\gamma
and
\frac{p-2}{2p}>\alpha
. Notice that this is possible only if
\alpha\neq\frac{1}{2}
.
The previous three estimates are enough to prove the assertion, if one observes that
\mathbb{E}_{s}(R^{F}_{s,t})=\mathbb{E}_{s}(\int_{s}^{t}\mu_{r}dr)+\mathbb{E}_{s}(R^{\phi}_{s,t})
.

2.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
be an accumulation point, and let
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
such that
\rho(\zeta^{n},\zeta^{0})\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
.
When taking the difference
F^{\zeta^{n}}-F^{\zeta^{0}}
, estimating the term
I^{n}:=\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\phi^{\zeta^{n}}_{r},(\phi^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{n}}_{r}-\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\phi^{\zeta^{0}}_{r},(\phi^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{0}}_{r}
requires some attention.
Recalling the construction of the rough stochastic integral (see
2.10
), for any
p\in[2,\infty)
and for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
it holds that

\|\delta I^{n}_{s,t}-\Xi^{n}_{s,t}\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\gamma}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\mathbb{E}_{s}(\delta I^{n}_{s,t}-\Xi^{n}_{s,t})\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\min\{\alpha,\gamma\}+\gamma^{\prime}},

where
\Xi^{n}_{s,t}:=\phi^{\zeta^{n}}_{s}\delta W^{\zeta^{n}}_{s,t}+(\phi^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime}_{s}\mathbb{W}^{\zeta^{n}}_{s,t}-\phi^{\zeta^{0}}_{s}\delta W^{\zeta^{0}}_{s,t}-(\phi^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}_{s}\mathbb{W}^{\zeta^{0}}_{s,t}
.
By the uniqueness statement in the stochastic sewing lemma (see e.g.
[
Lê20
]
), we deduce that
I^{n}
is the unique processes characterized by
\Xi^{n}
and we can use the estimates therein contained to have

\displaystyle\|\delta I^{n}_{s,t}\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\|\delta I^{n}_{s,t}-\Xi^{n}_{s,t}\|_{p}+\|\Xi^{n}_{s,t}\|_{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\alpha,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},p,T,M}\left(\|\phi^{\zeta^{n}},(\phi^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime};\phi^{\zeta^{0}},(\phi^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})\|_{W^{\zeta^{n}},W^{\zeta^{0}};\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p}+\rho_{\alpha}(\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{n}},\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{0}})\right)|t-s|^{\alpha+\gamma}

and

\|\mathbb{E}_{s}(\delta I^{n}_{s,t})\|_{p}\lesssim_{\alpha,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},p,T,M}\left(\|\phi^{\zeta^{n}},(\phi^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime};\phi^{\zeta^{0}},(\phi^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})\|_{W^{\zeta^{n}},W^{\zeta^{0}};\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p}+\rho_{\alpha}(\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{n}},\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{0}})\right)|t-s|^{\alpha+\min\{\alpha,\gamma\}+\gamma^{\prime}},

where
M
is any positive constant such that
\sup_{n\geq 0}(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\phi_{t}^{\zeta^{n}}\|_{p}+\|(\phi^{\zeta^{n}},(\phi^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W^{\zeta^{n}}}L_{p}}+|\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{n}}|_{\alpha})\leq M.

Let now
p\in(\frac{1}{1-2\alpha},\infty)
be arbitrary.
Notice that, by construction,
F_{0}^{\zeta}=0
for any
\zeta\in U
.
By linearity of the integrals and by the previous considerations, for any
n\geq 1
we can write that

\displaystyle\|F^{\zeta^{n}},(F^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime};F^{\zeta^{0}},(F^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\|_{W^{\zeta^{n}},W^{\zeta^{0}};\alpha,\gamma;p}=\left\|\int_{\cdot}^{\cdot}\mu^{\zeta^{n}}_{r}-\mu_{r}^{\zeta^{0}}dr\right\|_{\alpha+\gamma;p}+\left\|\int_{\cdot}^{\cdot}\nu^{\zeta^{n}}_{r}-\nu^{\zeta^{0}}_{r}dr\right\|_{\alpha;p}+

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\phi^{\zeta^{n}}_{r},(\phi^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{n}}_{r},\phi^{\zeta^{n}};\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\phi^{\zeta^{0}}_{r},(\phi^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{0}}_{r},\phi^{\zeta^{0}}\right\|_{W^{\zeta^{n}},W^{\zeta^{0}};\alpha,\gamma;p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\alpha,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},p,T,|\mathbf{W}|_{\alpha},M}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\mu^{\zeta^{n}}_{r}-\mu^{\zeta^{0}}_{r}|^{p}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\nu^{\zeta^{n}}_{r}-\nu^{\zeta^{0}}_{r}|^{p}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+

\displaystyle+\|\phi^{\zeta^{n}},(\phi^{\zeta^{n}})^{\prime};\phi^{\zeta^{0}},(\phi^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})\|_{W^{\zeta^{n}},W^{\zeta^{0}};\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p}+\rho_{\alpha}(\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{n}},\mathbf{W}^{\zeta^{0}}),

and the right-hand side tends to 0 as
n\to+\infty
, by assumption.

3.
We argue recursively on
k
. Let us first assume
k=1
. Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
and
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
be fixed.
For any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
we can write

\displaystyle\Xi^{\varepsilon}:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(F^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-F^{\zeta^{0}})=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\cdot}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\mu^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{r}-\mu^{\zeta^{0}}_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\nu^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{r}-\nu^{\zeta^{0}}_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left((\phi^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l},(\phi^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime})-(\phi^{\zeta^{0}},(\phi^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime})\right)d\mathbf{W}_{r}

\displaystyle(\Xi^{\varepsilon})^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big((F^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}-(F^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\big)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-\phi^{\zeta^{0}})

For any
\zeta\in U
, define
