(\Xi^{\zeta},(\Xi^{\zeta})^{\prime}):=\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\mu^{\zeta}_{r}dr+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\nu^{\zeta}_{r}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{\cdot}\left(\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\alpha^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\phi^{\zeta}_{r},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\alpha^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(\phi^{\zeta})^{\prime}_{r}\right)d\mathbf{W}_{r},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{2\alpha^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\phi^{\zeta}\right).

Let
(\varepsilon^{n})_{n}\subseteq\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
be any arbitrary sequence converging to 0.
Then by reasoning as before, it follows that, for any
p\in(\frac{1}{1-2\alpha},\infty)
and for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\|\Xi^{\varepsilon^{n}},(\Xi^{\varepsilon^{n}})^{\prime};\Xi^{\zeta^{0}},(\Xi^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}\|_{W;\alpha,\gamma;p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\alpha,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},p,T,|\mathbf{W}|_{\alpha}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\mu^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{r}-\mu^{\zeta^{0}}_{r})-\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\mu^{\zeta^{0}}_{r}\right|^{p}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\nu^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}_{r}-\nu^{\zeta^{0}}_{r})-\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\nu^{\zeta^{0}}_{r}\right|^{p}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+

\displaystyle+\left\|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\phi^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-\phi^{\zeta^{0}}),\frac{1}{\varepsilon}((\phi^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l})^{\prime}-(\phi^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime});\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}\phi^{\zeta^{0}}_{r},\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}(\phi^{\zeta^{0}})^{\prime}_{r}\right\|_{W;\gamma,\gamma^{\prime};p}.

The right-hand side is going to 0 as
n\to+\infty
, by assumption.
Moreover, from what we proved in 1.  it is easy to deduce that
\{(\Xi^{\zeta},(\Xi^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\gamma}\mathscr{L}
-continuous, provided that the continuity assumptions on the integrands processes are satisfied.

Let us now assume that the assertion is proved for
k=k_{0}
and that all the assumptions hold for
k=k_{0}+1
.
By the recursive assumption, it is immediate to conclude that, for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
,
\{(\Xi^{\zeta},(\Xi^{\zeta})^{\prime})\}_{\zeta\in U}
is (
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\gamma}\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\gamma}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
∎

4
Well-posedness of rough PDEs

Let
T>0
and let
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W})\in\mathscr{C}_{g}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a weakly geometric rough path, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2}]
.
Given
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, we aim at proving an existence-and-uniqueness result for the following terminal value problem:

\begin{cases}-du_{t}=L_{t}u_{t}\ dt+\Gamma_{t}u_{t}\ d\mathbf{W}_{t}\qquad t\in[0,T]\\
u_{T}=g\end{cases}.

(21)

The solution is a map
u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
.
The (respectively, second and first order) time-dependent differential operators
L_{t}
and
\Gamma_{t}=((\Gamma_{t})_{1},\dots,(\Gamma_{t})_{n})
are defined, given
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, by

\displaystyle L_{t}f(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\sum_{a=1}^{m}\sigma_{a}^{i}(t,x)\sigma_{a}^{j}(t,x)\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{i}}(x)+\sum_{i=1}^{d}{b^{i}}(t,x)\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{i}}(x)+c(t,x)f(x)

\displaystyle(\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}f(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\beta_{\mu}^{i}(t,x)\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{i}}(x)+\gamma_{\mu}(t,x)f(x)\qquad\mu=1,\dots,n

for some functions

\displaystyle b,\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{m},\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{n}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\qquad\text{and}\qquad c,\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n},:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}.

We denote by
b^{i}
the
i
-th component of
b
(similarly for all the other maps).
From now on we use Einstein’s repeated indices summation. Let

\beta^{\prime}_{11},\dots,\beta^{\prime}_{nn}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\gamma^{\prime}_{11},\dots,\gamma^{\prime}_{nn}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}.

The formal identities
\beta_{\mu}(t,x)-\beta_{\mu}(s,x)\approx\beta^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}(s,x)\delta W^{\nu}_{s,t}
and
\gamma_{\mu}(t,x)-\gamma_{\mu}(s,x)\approx\gamma^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}(s,x)\delta W^{\nu}_{s,t}

justify the definition of

(\Gamma_{t}^{\prime})_{\mu\nu}f(x)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\beta_{\mu\nu}^{\prime})^{i}(t,x)\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{i}}(x)+\gamma^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}(t,x)f(x)\qquad\mu,\nu=1,\dots,n.

Definition 4.1
.

A regular solution to (
21
) is a map
u\in C^{0,2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})

5
5

5

For
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, we denote by
C^{0,\kappa}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}:\mathbb{R})
(
C_{b}^{0,\kappa}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
) the space of functions
f=f(t,x)
such that
f(t,\cdot)\in C^{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
for any
t\in[0,T]
, and
f,D_{x}f,\dots,D^{\kappa}_{x\dots x}f\in C^{0}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})\ (C_{b}^{0}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))
such that

i)

u(T,x)=g(x)
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
;

ii)

there exist
\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime}\in[0,1]
with
\alpha+\min\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\}+\alpha^{\prime\prime}>1
and a constant
C>0
such that,
for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
and for any
\mu\in\{1,\dots,n\}

6
6

6

The reason for the presence of the minus sign in front of
\Gamma^{\prime}
is due to the backward-in-time nature of the equation. It is essentially due to the fact that in
4.4
we consider
\beta
and
\gamma
time-controlled by
\beta^{\prime}
and
\gamma^{\prime}
, respectively, in a forward sense. A clear understanding of this phenomenon comes by looking at the proof of
4.9
. For an overview on backward rough integration and backward rough PDEs we refer to
[
FH20
, Section 5.4]
and
[
FH20
, Remark 12.4]
.
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\big((\Gamma_{s})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{s})_{\nu}-(\Gamma_{s}^{\prime})_{\mu\nu}\big)u_{s}(x)-\big((\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{t})_{\nu}-(\Gamma_{t}^{\prime})_{\mu\nu}\big)u_{t}(x)|\leq C|t-s|^{\alpha^{\prime}}\qquad\nu=1,\dots,n

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|(\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}u_{t}(x)-(\Gamma_{s})_{\mu}u_{s}(x)+\big((\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{t})_{\nu}-(\Gamma^{\prime}_{t})_{\mu\nu}\big)u_{t}(x)\ \delta W_{s,t}^{\nu}|\leq C|t-s|^{\alpha^{\prime}+\alpha^{\prime\prime}};

iii)

for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
and for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

u_{s}(x)-u_{t}(x)=\int_{s}^{t}L_{r}u_{r}(x)dr+(\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}u_{t}(x)\delta W_{s,t}^{\mu}+\big((\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{t})_{\nu}-(\Gamma_{t}^{\prime})_{\mu\nu}\big)u_{t}(x)\mathbb{W}_{s,t}^{\mu\nu}+u^{\natural}_{s,t}(x)

(22)

with
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u^{\natural}_{s,t}(x)|=o(|t-s|)
as
|t-s|\to 0
.

It is convenient to explicitly write
(\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{t})_{\nu}f(x)=(\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}((\Gamma_{t})_{\nu}f(\cdot))(x)
in coordinates as follows:

\displaystyle(\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{t})_{\nu}f(x)

\displaystyle=\beta_{\mu}^{j}(t,x)\frac{\partial\beta_{\nu}^{i}}{\partial x^{j}}(t,x)\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{i}}(x)+\beta_{\mu}^{j}(t,x)\beta_{\nu}^{i}(t,x)\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{i}}(x)+\beta^{j}_{\mu}(t,x)\frac{\partial\gamma_{\nu}}{\partial x^{j}}(t,x)f(x)+

(23)

\displaystyle+\beta^{j}_{\mu}(t,x)\gamma_{\nu}(t,x)\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{j}}(x)+\gamma_{\mu}(t,x)\beta^{i}_{\nu}(t,x)\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{i}}(x)+\gamma_{\mu}(t,x)\gamma_{\nu}(t,x)f(x).

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be any function satisfying condition ii) of
4.1
. Then, for any
(t,x)\in[0,T]\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and along any sequence of partitions
\pi
of
[t,T]
whose mesh size tends to zero, there exists

\int_{t}^{T}(\Gamma_{r}u_{r}(x),(\Gamma_{r}\Gamma_{r}-\Gamma_{r}^{\prime})u_{r}(x))d\mathbf{W}_{r}:=\lim_{|\pi|\to 0}\sum_{[s,\tau]\in\pi}(\Gamma_{\tau})_{\mu}u_{\tau}(x)\delta W^{\mu}_{s,\tau}+((\Gamma_{\tau})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{\tau})_{\nu}-(\Gamma^{\prime}_{\tau})_{\mu\nu})u_{\tau}(x)\mathbb{W}^{\mu\nu}_{s,\tau}.

In particular condition iii) of the previous definition can be equivalently replaced by

iii’)

for any
t\in[0,T]
and for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

u_{t}(x)=g(x)+\int_{t}^{T}L_{r}u_{r}(x)dr+\int_{t}^{T}(\Gamma_{r}u_{r}(x),(\Gamma_{r}\Gamma_{r}-\Gamma^{\prime}_{r})u_{r}(x))d\mathbf{W}_{r}

(24)

and the quantity
u^{\natural}_{s,t}(x)
in fact satisfies
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u^{\natural}_{s,t}(x)|\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\min\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\}+\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
for any
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
.

Proof.

The proof is essentially a consequence of the sewing lemma (see
[
FH20
, Lemma 4.2]
). Let
t\in[0,T]
and, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and for any
s,\tau\in[0,t]
, define

A_{s,\tau}(x):=(\Gamma_{\tau})_{\mu}u_{\tau}(x)\delta W_{s,\tau}^{\mu}+((\Gamma_{\tau})_{\mu}\Gamma_{\nu}+(\Gamma^{\prime}_{\tau})_{\mu\nu})u_{\tau}(x)\mathbb{W}_{s,\tau}^{\mu,\nu}\in\mathbb{R}.

Recalling Chen’s relation for rough paths, for any
s,r,\tau\in[0,t]
we have

\displaystyle A_{s,\tau}(x)-A_{s,r}(x)-A_{r,\tau}(x)

\displaystyle=((\Gamma_{\tau})_{\mu}u_{\tau}(x)-(\Gamma_{r})_{\mu}u_{r}(x))\delta W^{\mu}_{r,\tau}+

\displaystyle\quad+((\Gamma_{\tau})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{\tau})_{\nu}u_{\tau}(x)-(\Gamma^{\prime}_{\tau})_{\nu\mu}u_{\tau}(x))(\mathbb{W}^{\mu\nu}_{s,r}+\delta W^{\mu}_{s,r}\delta W^{\nu}_{r,\tau})+

\displaystyle\quad-((\Gamma_{s})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{s})_{\nu}u_{s}(x)-(\Gamma^{\prime}_{s})_{\nu\mu}u_{s}(x))\mathbb{W}^{\mu\nu}_{s,r}.

From condition (ii) of we deduce that
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|A_{s,v}(x)-A_{s,u}(x)-A_{u,v}(x)|\leq C|\mathbf{W}|_{\alpha}|t-s|^{\alpha+\min\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\}+\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
.
∎

In what follows, we are making use of a sort of time-shift in the rough driver of the equation, as illustrated in the following

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W})
be an
\alpha
-Hölder (weakly geometric) rough path, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2}]
, and let
s\in[0,T]
be arbitrary. Let us define

W^{s}:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}^{n},\quad u\mapsto W^{s}_{u}:=\begin{cases}W_{s+u}&\text{if $u\leq T-s$}\\
W_{T}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

and

\mathbb{W}^{s}:[0,T]^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{n},\quad(u,v)\mapsto\mathbb{W}^{s}_{u,v}:=\begin{cases}\mathbb{W}_{s+u,s+v}&\text{if $u,v\leq T-s$}\\
\mathbb{W}_{s+u,T}&\text{if $u\leq T-s<v$}\\
\mathbb{W}_{T,s+v}&\text{if $v\leq T-s<u$}\\
\mathbb{W}_{T,T}=0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}.

Then
\mathbf{W}^{s}:=(W^{s},\mathbb{W}^{s})
is an
\alpha
-Hölder (weakly geometric) rough path with
|\mathbf{W}^{s}|_{\alpha}\leq|\mathbf{W}|_{\alpha}
. Moreover, for any
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha)
the family
\{\mathbf{W}^{s}\}_{s\in[0,T]}
is
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha^{\prime}}
-continuous in the sense of
3.10
.

Proof.

It is straightforward to see that, for any
s\in[0,T]
,
|\delta W^{s}|_{\alpha}\leq|W|_{\alpha}
,
|\mathbb{W}^{s}|_{2\alpha}\leq|\mathbb{W}|_{2\alpha}
and that the Chen’s relation
\mathbb{W}^{s}_{u,v}-\mathbb{W}^{s}_{u,r}-\mathbb{W}^{s}_{r,v}=\delta W^{s}_{u,r}\otimes\delta W^{s}_{r,v}
holds for any
u,r,v\in[0,T]
.
Moreover,
\mathbf{W}^{s}
is clearly weakly geometric if
\mathbf{W}
is weakly geometric.
Let us now consider any arbitrary sequence
(s^{n})_{n}
in
[0,T]
, converging to
s
as
n\to+\infty
.
It is clear that
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|\mathbf{W}^{s^{n}}|_{\alpha}\leq|\mathbf{W}|_{\alpha}
.
Therefore the last assertion of the proposition follows by interpolation (see
2.16
), if we are able to show that

\sup_{u,v\in[0,T]}\left(|\delta W^{s^{n}}_{u,v}-\delta W^{s}_{u,v}|+|\mathbb{W}^{s^{n}}_{u,v}-\mathbb{W}^{s}_{u,v}|\right)\to 0\quad\text{as $n\to+\infty$}.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Without loss of generality we can assume
T-s^{n}\leq T-s
, otherwise we exchange
s^{n}
with
s
in all the following computations. Let
u,v\in[0,T]
be arbitrary. We only consider the case
\max\{u,v\}\leq T-s^{n}
. The proof of the other cases follows in analogous manner. Uniformly in
u,v
it holds that

|\delta W^{s^{n}}_{u,v}-\delta W^{s}_{u,v}|=|\delta W_{s^{n}+u,s^{n}+v}-\delta W_{s+u,s+v}|=|\delta W_{s+v,s^{n}+v}-\delta W_{s+u,s^{n}+u}|\leq 2|\delta W|_{\alpha}|s^{n}-s|^{\alpha}

and, using Chen’s relation,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{W}^{s^{n}}_{u,v}-\mathbb{W}^{s}_{u,v}|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{W}_{s^{n}+u,s^{n}+v}-\mathbb{W}_{s+u,s+v}|\leq|\mathbb{W}_{s^{n}+u,s^{n}+v}-\mathbb{W}_{s^{n}+u,s+v}|+|\mathbb{W}_{s^{n}+u,s+v}-\mathbb{W}_{s+u,s+v}|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{W}_{s+v,s^{n}+v}+\delta W_{s^{n}+u,s+v}\otimes\delta W_{s+v,s^{n}+v}|+|\mathbb{W}_{s^{n}+u,s+u}+\delta W_{s^{n}+u,s+u}\otimes\delta W_{s+v,s^{n}+v}|

\displaystyle\leq 2|\mathbb{W}|_{2\alpha}|s^{n}-s|^{2\alpha}+8T^{\alpha}|\delta W|_{\alpha}|s^{n}-s|^{\alpha}.

∎

Assumptions 4.4
.

Let
\lambda\in(\frac{1}{\alpha},3]
, and consider
\delta,\eta\in[0,\alpha]
and
\kappa,\theta\in[4,5]
such that
7
7

7

At first reading, take
\lambda=3
,
\kappa=\theta=5
and
\delta=\eta=\alpha

\alpha+\min\{\delta,\eta\}>\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha+\min\{\delta,\eta\}+\min\{(\lambda-2)\alpha,(\min\{\kappa,\theta\}-4)\alpha,\delta,\eta\}>1.

Let the following hold:

•

g\in C^{\lambda}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
;

•

b\in C^{0}([0,T];C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}))\cap\mathcal{B}([0,T];C^{\lambda}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}))
;

•

the map
\sigma=(\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{m}):[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{m};\mathbb{R}^{d})\equiv\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
defined by
\sigma(t,x)y:=\sigma_{j}(t,x)y^{j},\ y\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
belongs to
C^{0}([0,T];C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{m};\mathbb{R}^{d})))\cap\mathcal{B}([0,T];C^{\lambda}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{m};\mathbb{R}^{d})))
;

•

c\in C^{0}([0,T];C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))\cap\mathcal{B}([0,T];C^{\lambda}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))
;

•

the maps
\beta=(\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{n}):[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{d})\equiv\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}
and
\beta^{\prime}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{d}))
defined via
\beta^{\prime}(t,x)yz:=\beta^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}(t,x)y^{\nu}z^{\mu},\ y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
are such that
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\in\mathscr{D}_{W}^{2\delta}C_{b}^{\kappa}
with
\beta\in C^{0}([0,T];C^{\kappa}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})
and
\beta^{\prime}\in C^{0}([0,T];C^{\kappa-1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}))
;

•

(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in\mathscr{D}_{W}^{2\eta}C_{b}^{\theta}
with
\gamma\in C^{0}([0,T];C^{\theta}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in C^{0}([0,T];C^{\theta-1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})).

Theorem 4.5
.

Under
4.4
. Let
B=(B_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
be a Brownian motion on a complete filtered probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
.
For any
(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
, denote by
X^{s,x}
the solution of the following rough SDE

X^{s,x}_{t}=x+\int_{0}^{t}b(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})dr+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(\beta,\beta^{\prime})(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r},

(25)

where
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r}):=(\beta(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x}),D_{x}\beta(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})\beta(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})+\beta^{\prime}(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r}))
and
\mathbf{W}^{s}
is defined as in
4.3
.
Define

u(s,x):=\mathbb{E}\left(g(X_{T-s}^{s,x})e^{\int_{0}^{T-s}c(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{T-s}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}}\right).

(26)

Then

u\in C^{0,2}_{b}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{B}([0,T];C^{\lambda}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))\cap C^{\alpha}([0,T];C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))

and it is the unique regular solution to
-du_{t}=L_{t}u_{t}+\Gamma_{t}u_{t}d\mathbf{W}_{t}
in the sense of
4.1
.

Proof.

The space-time regularity of
u
is established in
4.8
using the results results on parameter dependent rough SDEs from
Section

3
.
Existence is proved in
4.9
, with
4.11
and
4.18
as the main tools.
Uniqueness follows from and
4.12
.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

In our notation, the parameter
s
in
X^{s,x}
represents a time-shift in the coefficients and the rough driver, not the initial time. A more familiar formulation may be

u(s,x)=\mathbb{E}\left(g(\bar{X}^{s,x}_{T})e^{\int_{s}^{T}c_{r}(\bar{X}_{r}^{s,x})dr+\int_{s}^{T}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})(r,\bar{X}_{r}^{s,x})d\mathbf{W}_{r}}\right),

being
\bar{X}^{s,x}=(\bar{X}_{t}^{s,x})
the solution of

X_{t}=x+\int_{s}^{t}b(r,X_{r})dr+\int_{s}^{T}\sigma(r,X_{r})dB_{r}+\int_{s}^{T}(\beta,\beta^{\prime})(r,X_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}\qquad t\in[s,T].

We prefer to work with
X^{s,x}
defined as in (
25
), since it treats
s
as a parameter, allowing us to apply the parameter dependence theory we previously developed in this paper.
The two approaches are consistent if one considers
4.15
and performs a time-change in the integrals (as explained, for example, in the proof of
4.16
.2 below).
Indeed, with the notation introduce in Section
4.2
it is possible to show that
Y=(Y_{t}:=\bar{X}^{s,x}_{s+t})_{t\in[0,T-s]}
belongs to
RSDE(s,x,B^{s},\{\mathcal{F}^{s}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T-s]})
,
where
B^{s}_{t}:=B_{s+t}-B_{s}
and
\mathcal{F}^{s}_{t}:=\mathcal{F}_{s+t}
(cf.
4.16
).

Remark 4.7
.

As a consequence of
4.15
below, the rough PDE solution
u
given in (
26
) is uniquely determined by the functions
b,\sigma\sigma^{T},\beta,\beta^{\prime},c,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
and
g
.
As already pointed out in
[
Kry08
, Remark 1.9.11]
, the definition of
u(s,x)
does not change if we take another filtered probability space and/or if we replace the Brownian motion with a
\bar{m}
-dimensional Brownian motion (
\bar{m}\neq m
) and take another coefficient
\bar{\sigma}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times\bar{m}}
, provided that
\bar{\sigma}\bar{\sigma}^{T}\equiv\sigma\sigma^{T}
.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
u=u(s,x)
be defined as in (
26
).
Then under
4.4

u\in C_{b}^{0,2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{B}([0,T],C^{\lambda}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))\cap C^{\alpha}([0,T];C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})).

Proof.

Notice that, in general, equation (
25
) only makes sense on the time interval
[0,T-s]
.
Redefining the coefficients by
b(t,x)=b(T,x)
,
\sigma(t,x)=\sigma(T,x)
and
\beta(t,x)=\beta(T,x)
if
t\geq T
, we can always assume it to be defined for any
t\in[0,T]
.
We also extend
c(t,x)=c(T,x)
and
\gamma(t,x)=\gamma(T,x)
for
t\geq T
.
For any given
s\in[0,T]
, we define
b^{s}(t,x):=b(s+t,x)
and a similar definition holds for
\sigma^{s}
,
c^{s}
,
\beta^{s}
and
\gamma^{s}
.
We set
(\beta^{s})^{\prime}(t,x):=\beta^{\prime}(s+t,x)
and
(\gamma^{s})^{\prime}(t,x):=\gamma^{\prime}(s+t,x)
.
We start by studying the continuity and differentiability properties of the family
\{X^{s,x}\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
.
Under
4.4
it is straightforward to see that
\{b^{s}\}_{s\in[0,T]}
is a
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}
-continuous family of deterministic bounded Lipschitz vector fields with
\|b^{s}\|_{Lip}\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|D_{x}b(t,\cdot)|_{\infty}
uniformly in
s
. Moreover,
\{D_{x}b^{s}\}_{s\in[0,T]}
and
\{D^{2}_{xx}b^{s}\}_{s\in[0,T]}
are
\mathscr{L}C^{0}_{b}
-continuous.
The previous considerations apply if we replace
b^{s}
by
\sigma^{s}
and
c^{s}
.
On the other hand, it is also easy to see that, for any
s\in[0,T]
,
(\beta^{s},(\beta^{s})^{\prime})
is a (deterministic) controlled vector field in
\mathscr{D}_{W^{s}}^{2\delta}C_{b}^{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})
with

[(\beta^{s},(\beta^{s})^{\prime})]_{\kappa}+[(\beta^{s},(\beta^{s})^{\prime})]_{W^{s};2\delta;\kappa}\leq[(\beta,\beta^{\prime})]_{\kappa}+[(\beta,\beta^{\prime})]_{W;2\delta;\kappa}<+\infty.

Analogously, one shows that
(\gamma^{s},(\gamma^{s})^{\prime})\in\mathscr{D}_{W^{s}}^{2\eta}C_{b}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
Moreover, for any
(s^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq[0,T]
with
|s^{n}-s|\to_{n\to+\infty}0
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\beta^{s^{n}}(t,\cdot)-\beta^{s}(t,\cdot)|_{C^{\kappa}_{b}}+|(\beta^{s^{n}})^{\prime}(t,\cdot)-(\beta^{s})^{\prime}(t,\cdot)|_{C^{\kappa-1}_{b}}\to 0\qquad\text{as $n\to+\infty$}.

By interpolation (see
2.17
) this implies that
[\beta^{s^{n}}-\beta^{s}]_{{\delta^{\prime}};4}+[(\beta^{s^{n}})^{\prime}-(\beta^{s})^{\prime}]_{{\delta^{\prime}};3}+[R^{\beta^{s^{n}}}-R^{\beta^{s}}]_{{2\delta^{\prime}};3}\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
, for any
\delta^{\prime}\in[0,\delta)
, and consequently that
\{(\beta^{s},(\beta^{s})^{\prime})\}_{s\in[0,T]}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{2\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}C^{\kappa}_{b}\}_{s}
-continuous.
Arguing in a very similar way, we deduce that
\{(\gamma^{s},(\gamma^{s})^{\prime})\}_{s\in[0,T]}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{2\eta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}C^{\theta}_{b}\}_{s}
-continuous, for any
\eta^{\prime}\in[0,\eta)
.
From
4.3
we know that
\{\mathbf{W}^{s}\}_{s\in[0,T]}
is
\mathscr{C}^{\bar{\alpha}}
-continuous, for any
\bar{\alpha}\in(0,\alpha)
, and
\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|\mathbf{W}^{s}|_{\alpha}\leq|\mathbf{W}|_{\alpha}
.
Without loss of generality we can assume
\alpha\neq\frac{1}{2}
, let
\{\mathbf{W}^{s}\}_{s\in[0,T]}
be
\mathscr{C}^{{\alpha}}
-continuous, and choose
\delta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}
so that
\alpha+\min\{\delta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\}>\frac{1}{2}
and
\alpha+\min\{\delta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\}+\min\{(\lambda-2)\alpha,(\min\{\kappa,\theta\}-4)\alpha,\delta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}\}>1
.
Indeed, notice that a great majority of the results concerning continuity and differentiability for (possibly linear) rough SDEs in
Section

3
were stated for
\alpha
-rough paths with
\alpha\neq\frac{1}{2}
(for instance, cf.
3.19
). However, from the rest of the proof one can realize that such restrictions on the parameters do not cause any loss of generality in the statement of the proposition.

From
2.12
it follows that, for any
(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
X^{s,x}
is well-defined as the unique
L_{p,\infty}
-integrable solution to (
25
) for any
p\in[2,\infty)
.
From the
a priori estimate in
2.12
we have

\sup_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|(X^{s,x},\beta^{s}(X^{s,x}))\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\delta}L_{p,\infty}}<+\infty\qquad\text{for any $p\in[2,\infty)$}

(27)

and, from
2.13
,

\|X^{s,x},\beta^{s}(X^{s,x});X^{s,y},\beta^{s}(X^{s,y})\|_{W^{s};\alpha,\delta;p}\lesssim|x-y|,

(28)

for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, for any
s\in[0,T]
and for any
p\in[2,\infty)
,
where the implicit constant can be chosen as uniform in
s\in[0,T]
and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By
3.11
,
\{(X^{s,x},\beta^{s}(X^{s,x})\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}\}_{s}
-continuous and, for any
s\in[0,T]
,
\{(X^{s,x},\beta^{s}(X^{s,x})\}_{x}
is twice
\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-continuously
\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\delta}\mathscr{L}
-differentiable by
3.19
.

Moreover,
3.23
shows that
\{c^{s}(X^{s,x})\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-continuous, and by
3.20

\{c^{s}(X^{s,x})\}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is twice
\mathscr{L}
-continuously
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable for any fixed
s\in[0,T]
.
From
3.25
it is possible to deduce that

\{(\gamma^{s},(\gamma^{s})^{\prime})(X^{s,x}):=(\gamma^{s}(X^{s,x}),D_{x}\gamma^{s}(X^{s,x})\beta^{s}(X^{s,x})+(\gamma^{s})^{\prime}(X^{s,x}))\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}

is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\eta^{\prime},\min\{\delta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\}}\mathscr{L}\}_{s}
-continuous and that
\{(\gamma^{s}(X^{s,x}),(\gamma^{s},(\gamma^{s})^{\prime})(X^{s,x}))\}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is twice
\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\eta^{\prime},\bar{\eta}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})
-continuously
\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\eta^{\prime},\bar{\eta}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})
-differentiable, where
\bar{\eta}\in[0,\min\{\eta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}\})
can be chosen so that
\alpha+\eta^{\prime}+\bar{\eta}>1
.
We can therefore deduce that

(I^{s,x},(I^{s,x})^{\prime}):=\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}c^{s}(r,X^{s,x}_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\gamma^{s},(\gamma^{s})^{\prime})(r,X^{s,x}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r},\ \gamma^{s}(X^{s,x})\right)

(29)

is well defined as a stochastic controlled rough path in
\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\eta}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
, for any
(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
The a priori bound in statement
1.
of
3.28
and (
27
) show that

\displaystyle\sup_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|(I^{s,x},(I^{s,x})^{\prime})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\eta}L_{p,\infty}}<+\infty,

for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, while the proof of statement
2.
of
3.28
and (
28
) show instead that, for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\|I^{s,x},(I^{s,x})^{\prime};I^{s,y},(I^{s,y})^{\prime}\|_{W^{s};\alpha,\eta;p}\lesssim|x-y|

where the implicit constant is uniform in
s,x,y
.
From
3.28
we deduce that
\{I^{s,x},(I^{s,x})^{\prime}\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\eta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})\}_{s}
-continuous, and by applying
3.28
it is possible to show that
\{(I^{s,x},(I^{s,x})^{\prime})\}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is twice
\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\eta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-continuously
\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\eta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-differentiable, for any fixed
s\in[0,T]
.
Arguing as in the proof of
4.14
, one can use the two previous inequalities to show that, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
,

\sup_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|(e^{I^{s,x}},(I^{s,x})^{\prime}e^{I^{s,x}})\|_{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\eta}L_{p}}<+\infty

(30)

and

\|e^{I^{s,x}},(I^{s,x})^{\prime}e^{I^{s,x}};e^{I^{s,y}},(I^{s,y})^{\prime}e^{I^{s,y}}\|_{W^{s};\alpha,\eta;p}\lesssim|x-y|.

(31)

We now prove that
u\in C^{0,2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})\cap\mathcal{B}([0,T];C^{\lambda}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))
, which by definition is equivalent to show that the following four properties are satisfied:

a)

u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is continuous and (globally) bounded;

b)

for any
i\in\{1,\dots,d\}
, there exists
\frac{\partial u}{\partial x^{i}}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
and it is continuous and bounded;

c)

for any
i,j\in\{1,\dots,d\}
, there exists
\frac{\partial^{2}u}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{i}}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
and it is continuous and bounded;

d)

uniformly in
s\in[0,T]
,

\left[\frac{\partial^{2}u}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{i}}(s,\cdot)\right]_{\lambda-2}:=\sup_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},x\neq y}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{\lambda-2}}\left|\frac{\partial^{2}u}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{i}}(s,x)-\frac{\partial^{2}u}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{i}}(s,y)\right|<+\infty.

a) To prove that
u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is continuous, we show that the family of random variables
\{g(X^{s,x}_{T-s})e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-continuous and we apply
3.5
.
Let
(s^{0},x^{0})\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
and let
((s^{n},x^{n}))_{n\geq 1}\subseteq[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{(s^{0},x^{0})\}
be any arbitrary sequence with
|s^{n}-s^{0}|+|x^{n}-x^{0}|\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
.
For any
p\in[2,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\|X^{s^{n},x^{n}}_{T-s^{n}}-X^{s^{0},x^{0}}_{T-s^{0}}\|_{p}\leq\|X^{s^{n},x^{n}}_{T-s^{n}}-X^{s^{n},x^{n}}_{T-s^{0}}\|_{p}+\|X^{s^{n},x^{n}}_{T-s^{0}}-X^{s^{0},x^{0}}_{T-s^{0}}\|_{p}

(32)

\displaystyle\leq\|(X^{s^{n},x^{n}},\beta^{s^{n}}(X^{s^{n},x^{n}})\|_{W^{s^{n}};\alpha,\delta^{\prime};p}|s^{n}-s^{0}|^{\alpha}+\|X^{s^{n},x^{n}},\beta^{s^{n}}(X^{s^{n},x^{n}});X^{s^{0},x^{0}},\beta^{s^{n}}(X^{s^{0},x^{0}})\|_{W^{s^{n}},W^{s^{0}};\alpha,\delta^{\prime};p}

and the right-hand side tends to zero as
n\to+\infty
.
Similarly, one shows that
\{I^{s,x}_{T-s}\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is a
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-continuous family of random variables, which implies that
\{e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-continuous, by
4.13
.
Applying
3.20
to the function
(x,t)\mapsto g(x)y
and considering
3.5
, this proves that
u\in C^{0}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
.
By construction,
u
is also globally bounded. Indeed,

\displaystyle|u(s,x)|

\displaystyle\leq|g|_{\infty}\mathbb{E}(|e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}|)\leq|g|_{\infty}\|\delta e^{I^{s,x}}\|_{\alpha;1}T^{\alpha}

and the right-hand side is uniformly bounded in
(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
by (
30
).

b) Note that, for any fixed
s\in[0,T]
, the family
\{g(X^{s,x}_{T-s})e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}\}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is twice
\mathscr{L}
-continuously
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
Denote by
e_{1},\dots,e_{d}
the elements of the canonical basis of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, which in particular belong to
\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Given any
i\in\{1,\dots,d\}
and denoting by
Y^{s,x,i}=\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}X^{s,x}
, it holds that the latter is the solution of the following linear rough SDE:

\displaystyle Y^{s,x,i}_{t}

\displaystyle=e_{i}+\int_{0}^{t}D_{x}b^{s}(r,X_{r}^{s,x})Y^{s,x,i}_{r}dr+\int_{0}^{t}D_{x}\sigma^{s}(r,X_{r}^{s,x})Y^{s,x,i}_{r}dB_{r}+

(33)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}(D_{x}\beta^{s}(r,X^{s,x}_{r}),D^{2}_{xx}\beta^{s}(r,X^{s,x}_{r})\beta^{s}(r,X_{r}^{s,x})+D_{x}(\beta^{s})^{\prime}(r,X^{s,x}_{r}))Y^{s,x,i}_{r}d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}

\displaystyle=:e_{i}+\int_{0}^{t}G_{r}^{s,x}Y_{r}^{s,x,i}dr+\int_{0}^{t}S_{r}^{s,x}Y_{r}^{s,x,i}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(f^{s,x}_{r},(f^{s,x})^{\prime}_{r})Y_{r}^{s,x,i}d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}.

From
3.5

\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{i}}u(s,x)=\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\partial g}{\partial x^{k}}(X^{s,x}_{T-s})(Y^{s,x,i}_{T-s})^{k}e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}+g(X^{s,x}_{T-s})\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}_{T-s}\right)e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}\right),

where
(Y^{s,x,i}_{T-s})^{k}
denotes the
k
-th component of the
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued random vector
Y^{s,x,i}_{T-s}
and

\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}=\int_{0}^{T-s}D_{x}c^{s}(r,X^{s,x}_{r})Y^{s,x,i}_{r}dr+\\
+\int_{0}^{T-s}\big(D_{x}\gamma^{s}(r,X^{s,x}_{r}),D^{2}_{xx}\gamma^{s}(r,X^{s,x}_{r})\beta^{s}(r,X^{s,x}_{r})+D_{x}(\gamma^{s})^{\prime}(r,X^{s,x}_{r})\big)Y_{r}^{s,x,i}d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}.

Boundedness of
\frac{\partial u}{\partial x^{i}}
essentially follows from (
27
) and (
30
), as already pointed out for
u
. We already know that
\{g(X^{s,x}_{T-s})\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
,
\{D_{x}g(X^{s,x}_{T-s})\}_{(s,x)}
and
\{e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}\}_{(s,x)}
are
\mathscr{L}
-continuous, hence the continuity of
\frac{\partial u}{\partial x^{i}}
as a map
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
if we show that
\{Y^{s,x,i}_{T-s}\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
and
\{\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}_{T-s}\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
are
\mathscr{L}
-continuous. From
3.20
and
3.21
,
\{G^{s,x}\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}
-continuous, and the same holds for
\{S^{s,x}\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
.
Being
\{(D_{x}\beta^{s},D_{x}(\beta^{s})^{\prime})\}_{s\in[0,T]}

\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{2\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}C^{3}_{b}\}_{s}
-continuous by construction, by applying
3.25
.1 we can deduce that
\{(f^{s,x},(f^{s,x})^{\prime})\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{2\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}\}_{s}
-continuous.
Hence, by
3.14
we have that
\{(Y^{s,x,i},f^{s,x}Y^{s,x,i})\}_{(s,x)}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\}_{s}
-continuous.
Similarly, it is possible to show that
\{D_{x}c^{s}(X^{s,x})\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is
\mathscr{L}\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
-continuous and that
\{(D_{x}\gamma^{s},D_{x}(\gamma^{s})^{\prime})(X^{s,x})\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{2\eta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\}_{s}
-continuous. Hence
\{(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x},D_{x}\gamma^{s}(X^{s,x})Y^{s,x,i})\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\min\{\eta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}\}}\mathscr{L}\}_{s}
-continuous by
3.28
.

c) For any
i,j\in\{1,\dots,d\}
denote by
Z^{s,x,i,j}=\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{j}}Y^{s,x,i}
. The latter solves the following linear rough SDE:

\displaystyle Z^{s,x,i,j}_{t}

\displaystyle=F^{s,x,i,j}_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}G_{r}^{s,x}Z_{r}^{s,x,i,j}dr+\int_{0}^{t}S_{r}^{s,x}Z_{r}^{s,x,i,j}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(f^{s,x}_{r},(f^{s,x})^{\prime}_{r})Z_{r}^{s,x,i,j}d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r},

where

\displaystyle F^{s,x,i,j}_{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}D^{2}_{xx}b^{s}(r,X_{r}^{s,x})Y^{s,x,j}_{r}Y^{s,x,i}_{r}dr+\int_{0}^{t}D^{2}_{xx}\sigma^{s}(r,X_{r}^{s,x})Y^{s,x,j}_{r}Y^{s,x,i}_{r}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(\phi^{s,x,i,j}_{r},(\phi^{s,x,i,j})^{\prime}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}

\displaystyle\phi^{s,x,i,j}_{t}

\displaystyle=D^{2}_{xx}\beta^{s}(t,X^{s,x}_{t})Y^{s,x,j}_{t}Y^{s,x,i}_{t}

\displaystyle(\phi^{s,x,i,j})^{\prime}_{t}

\displaystyle=D^{3}_{xxx}\beta^{s}(t,X^{s,x}_{t})\beta^{s}(t,X_{t}^{s,x})Y^{s,x,j}_{t}Y^{s,x,i}_{t}+D^{2}_{xx}\beta^{s}(t,X^{s,x}_{t})D_{x}\beta^{s}(t,X^{s,x}_{t})Y^{s,x,j}_{t}Y^{s,x,i}_{t}+

\displaystyle+D^{2}_{xx}\beta^{s}(t,X^{s,x}_{t})Y^{s,x,j}_{t}D_{x}\beta^{s}(t,X^{s,x}_{t})Y^{s,x,i}_{t}+D^{2}_{xx}(\beta^{s})^{\prime}(t,X^{s,x}_{t})Y^{s,x,j}_{t}Y^{s,x,i}_{t}.

Analogously as before, one deduces that there exists

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}u}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{i}}(s,x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg(\frac{\partial^{2}g}{\partial x^{l}\partial x^{k}}(X^{s,x}_{T-s})(Y^{s,x,i}_{T-s})^{k}(Y^{s,x,i}_{T-s})^{l}e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}+\frac{\partial g}{\partial x^{k}}(X^{s,x}_{T-s})(Z^{s,x,i,j}_{T-s})^{k}e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}+

(34)

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial g}{\partial x^{k}}(X^{s,x}_{T-s})(Y^{s,x,i}_{T-s})^{k}\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{j}}I^{s,x}_{T-s}\right)e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}+\frac{\partial g}{\partial x^{k}}(X^{s,x}_{T-s})(Y^{s,x,j})^{k}\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}_{T-s}\right)e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}+

\displaystyle+g(X^{s,x}_{T-s})\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{j}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}_{T-s}\right)e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}+g(X^{s,x}_{T-s})\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}_{T-s}\right)\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{j}}I^{s,x}_{T-s}\right)e^{I^{s,x}_{T-s}}\bigg)

and that it is globally bounded in
(s,x)
.
To prove the continuity of
\frac{\partial^{2}u}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{i}}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, we are left to show that
\{Z^{s,x,i,j}_{T-s}\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
and
\{\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{j}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}_{T-s}\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
are
\mathscr{L}
-continuous.
Taking into account
3.14
, we just need to show that
\{F^{s,x,i,j}\}_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}
is
\{\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\delta^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}\}_{s}
-continuous, which is a trivial consequence of
3.28
, and to apply
3.28
for the second family of random variables.

d) We are left to prove that
[\frac{\partial^{2}u}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{i}}(s,\cdot)]_{\lambda-2}<+\infty
, uniformly in
s\in[0,T]
.
To do so, we just combine (
28
) and (
31
) with
2.15
and the local Lipschitz estimates on integrals deduced, for instance, in the proof of
3.28
.2.
Let
s\in[0,T]
be fixed and let
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
It holds that, for any arbitrary
p\in[2,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|G^{s,x}_{r}-G^{s,y}_{r}|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq|D_{x}b|_{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|X^{s,x}_{r}-X^{s,y}_{r}|^{p}\right)\leq|D_{x}b|_{\infty}T\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{t}^{x}-X_{t}^{y}\|_{p}\lesssim|x-y|.

A similar conclusion holds replacing
b
with
\sigma
.
In addition,
2.5
leads to

\|f^{s,x},(f^{s,x})^{\prime};f^{s,y},(f^{s,y})^{\prime}\|_{W^{s};2\delta^{\prime};p}\lesssim\|X^{s,x},\beta^{s}(X^{s,x});X^{s,y},\beta^{s}(X^{s,y})\|_{W^{s};2\delta^{\prime};p}\lesssim|x-y|.

We can therefore conclude (cf.
2.15
) that

\|Y^{s,x,i},f^{s,x}Y^{s,x,i};Y^{s,y,i},f^{s,y}Y^{s,y,i}\|_{W^{s};\alpha,\delta^{\prime};p}\lesssim|x-y|.

Notice that
\|\phi^{s,x,i,j}_{r},(\phi^{s,x,i,j})^{\prime}_{r};\phi^{s,y,i,j}_{r},(\phi^{s,y,i,j})^{\prime}_{r}\|_{W^{s};2\delta^{\prime};p}\lesssim|x-y|
and, due to our assumptions on
b
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|D_{xx}b^{s}(r,X_{r}^{s,x})-D^{2}_{xx}b^{s}(r,X^{s,x}_{r})|^{p}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}[D^{2}_{xx}b(t,\cdot)]_{\lambda-2}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|X_{r}^{s,x}-X^{s,x}_{r}|^{(\lambda-2)p}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}[D^{2}_{xx}b(t,\cdot)]_{\lambda-2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|X_{t}^{s,x}-X^{s,x}_{t}\|_{(\lambda-2)p}^{\lambda-2}\lesssim|x-y|^{\lambda-2}.

A similar conclusion holds if we replace
b
with
\sigma
.
An application of Hölder’s inequality is sufficient to show that

\|F^{s,x,i,j},\phi^{s,x,i,j};F^{s,y,i,j},\phi^{s,y,i,j}\|_{W^{s};\alpha,\delta^{\prime};p}\lesssim|x-y|^{\lambda-2}\qquad\text{for any $p\in[2,\infty)$}.

This implies that (cf.
2.15
)

\|Z^{s,x,i,j},(Z^{s,x,i,j})^{\prime};Z^{s,y,i,j},(Z^{s,y,i,j})^{\prime}\|_{W^{s};\alpha,\delta^{\prime};p}\lesssim|x-y|^{\lambda-2}.

Analogous estimates show that

\bigg\|\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x},\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}\right)^{\prime};\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,y},\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,y}\right)^{\prime}\bigg\|_{W^{s};\alpha,\min\{\eta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}\};p}\lesssim|x-y|^{\lambda-2},

ans the same holds by replacing
\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}
with
\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{j}}\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}
.
Considering (
34
) and the fact that
D^{2}_{xx}g(\cdot)
is
(\lambda-2)
-Hölder continuous on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by assumption, the last statement of the assertion is proved.

To conclude the proof, we show that
u\in C^{\alpha}([0,T];C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))
using the results of
Section

4.2
.
Notice that
u_{t}(\cdot)\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
for any
t\in[0,T]
.
Let
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
be arbitrary.
Let
Y
be defined as in (
46
) and let
J=(J_{\tau})_{\tau\in[0,T-(t-s)]}
be defined by

J_{\tau}:=\int_{0}^{\tau}c^{t}(r,Y_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{\tau}(\gamma^{t}(r,Y_{r}),D_{x}\gamma^{t}(r,Y_{r})\beta^{t}(r,Y_{r}))d\mathbf{W}^{t}_{r}.

By means of
4.18
it holds that

\displaystyle|u_{t}(x)-u_{s}(x)|=\left|\mathbb{E}\left(g(X_{T-t}^{t,x})e^{I^{t,x}_{T-t}}-e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}g(Y_{T-t})e^{J_{T-t}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\left|g(X_{T-t}^{t,x})e^{I^{t,x}_{T-t}}\left(1-e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}\right)\right|\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\left|e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}g(X_{T-t}^{t,x})\left(e^{I^{t,x}_{T-t}}-e^{J_{T-t}}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left(\left|e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}\left(g(X_{T-t}^{t,x})-g(Y_{T-t})\right)e^{J_{T-t}}\right|\right)\lesssim_{M}\|e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}-1\|_{2}+\|e^{I^{t,x}_{T-t}}-e^{J_{t-t}}\|_{2}+\|X^{t,x}_{T-t}-Y_{T-t}\|_{2}

where
M
is any positive constant (independent on
x
) such that
|g|_{C^{1}_{b}}+\|\delta e^{I^{t,x}_{\cdot}}\|_{\alpha;4}+\|\delta e^{I^{s,x}_{\cdot}}\|_{\alpha;4}+\|\delta e^{J_{\cdot}}\|_{\alpha;4}\leq M
.
Notice that
\|e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}-1\|_{2}\leq\|\delta e^{I^{s,x}_{\cdot}}\|_{\alpha;4}|t-s|^{\alpha}
.
Moreover, from
2.13
and uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\sup_{r\in[0,T]}\|X^{t,x}_{r}-Y_{r}\|_{2}\lesssim\|X_{t-s}^{s,x}-x\|_{2}\leq\|\delta X^{s,x}\|_{\alpha;2,\infty}|t-s|^{\alpha}.

Similarly,

\sup_{r\in[0,T]}\|e^{I^{t,x}_{r}}-e^{J_{r}}\|_{2}\lesssim\|X^{t,x},\beta^{t}(X^{t,x});Y,\beta^{t}(Y)\|_{W^{t};\alpha,\delta^{\prime};2}\lesssim\|x-X^{s,x}_{t-s}\|_{2}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha}.

Indeed, recall that
Y
solves the same rough SDE as
X^{t,x}
, but with
X^{s,x}_{t-s}
as initial condition.
We have therefore showed that

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u_{t}(x)-u_{s}(x)|\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha}.

Arguing in a very similar way, one can prove the same for the functions
\frac{\partial u}{\partial x^{i}}
and
\frac{\partial^{2}u}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{i}}
.
∎

Theorem 4.9

(Existence)
.

Let
u=u(s,x)
be defined as in (
26
).
Then, under
4.4
,
u
is a regular solution of
-du_{t}=L_{t}u_{t}\,dt+\Gamma_{t}u_{t}\,d\mathbf{W}_{t}
in the sense of
4.1
.

Proof.

We prove that
u
satisfies all the requirements of
4.1
.
By
4.8
,
u\in C^{0,2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and, by construction,
u_{T}(x)=g(x)
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Let
(s,t)\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be arbitrary.
Recall that
u\in C^{\alpha}([0,T];C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))
. Hence, under our assumptions on
\beta,\beta^{\prime}
and
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
and looking at (
23
), it is easy to deduce that, for any
\mu,\nu=1,\dots,n
and uniformly in
x
,

|((\Gamma_{s})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{s})_{\nu}-(\Gamma^{\prime}_{s})_{\mu\nu})u_{s}(x)-((\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{t})_{\nu}-(\Gamma_{t}^{\prime})_{\mu\nu})u_{t}(x)|\lesssim|t-s|^{\min\{\alpha,\delta,\eta\}}.

Moreover, for any
\mu=1,\dots,n
we can write

\displaystyle(\Gamma_{s})_{\mu}u_{s}(x)-(\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}u_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=(\beta_{\mu}^{i}(s,x)-\beta^{i}_{\mu}(t,x))\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)+\beta^{i}_{\mu}(t,x)\left(\frac{\partial u_{s}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)-\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)\right)+

\displaystyle-(\gamma_{\mu}(t,x)-\gamma_{\mu}(s,x))u_{t}(x)+\gamma_{\mu}(t,x)(u_{s}(x)-u_{t}(x))+P^{1}_{s,t}(x)=

\displaystyle=-(\beta_{\mu\nu}^{\prime})^{i}(t,x)\delta W_{s,t}^{\nu}\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)+\beta^{i}_{\mu}(t,x)\left(\frac{\partial u_{s}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)-\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)\right)+

\displaystyle-\gamma^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}(t,x)\delta W^{\nu}_{s,t}u_{t}(x)+\gamma_{\mu}(t,x)(u_{s}(x)-u_{t}(x))+P^{2}_{s,t}(x)

with
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|P^{i}_{s,t}(x)|\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\min\{\delta,\eta\}}
(
i=1,2
).
From
4.18

u_{s}(x)=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}\ u_{t}(X_{t-s}^{s,x})\right)
, which leads to

\displaystyle u_{s}(x)-u_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}\ u_{t}(X_{t-s}^{s,x})-u_{t}(x)\right)=

(35)

\displaystyle=\left(\gamma_{\nu}(s,x)u_{t}(x)+\beta_{\nu}^{j}(s,x)\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{j}}(x)\right)\delta W_{s,t}^{\nu}+\mathbb{E}\left(R^{e^{I^{s,x}}u(X^{s,x})}_{0,t-s}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\gamma_{\nu}(t,x)u_{t}(x)+\beta_{\nu}^{j}(t,x)\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{j}}(x)\right)\delta W_{s,t}^{\nu}+Q_{s,t}(x)

with
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|Q_{s,t}(x)|\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\min\{\delta,\eta\}}
.
The previous identities hold because
u_{t}(\cdot)\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
,
e^{I^{s,x}_{0}}=1
and, by
2.5
and
4.14
,

(e^{I^{s,x}}u_{t}(X^{s,x}),\gamma^{s}(X^{s,x})e^{I^{s,x}}u_{t}(X^{s,x})+e^{I^{s,x}}D_{x}u_{t}(X^{s,x})\beta^{s}(X^{s,x}))\in\mathbf{D}_{W^{s}}^{\alpha,\min\{\delta,\eta\}}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}).

Recall that by construction
|\mathbb{E}(R^{e^{I^{s,x}}u(X^{s,x})}_{0,t-s})|\leq\mathbb{E}(|\mathbb{E}_{0}(R^{e^{I^{s,x}}u(X^{s,x})}_{0,t-s})|)\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\min\{\delta,\eta\}}
.
From
3.5
we get that, for any
i=1,\dots,d
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{i}}u_{s}(x)=\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{i}}\mathbb{E}\left(e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}\ u_{t}(X_{t-s}^{s,x})\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}\ D_{x}u_{t}(X_{t-s}^{s,x})Y_{t-s}^{s,x,i}+\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}_{t-s}\ e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}\ u_{t}(X_{t-s}^{s,x})\right),

where
e_{i}
denotes the
i
-th element of the canonical basis of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
Y^{s,x,i}
is defined as in (
33
) and

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\cdot}D_{x}c(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})Y^{s,x,i}_{r}dr+

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\cdot}(D_{x}\gamma(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x}),D^{2}_{xx}\gamma(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})\beta(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})+D_{x}\gamma^{\prime}(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r}))Y^{s,x,i}_{r}d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}.

Similarly as before one can show that, for any
i=1,\dots,d
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)-\frac{\partial u_{s}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}\ D_{x}u_{t}(X_{t-s}^{s,x})Y_{t-s}^{s,x,i}+\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}_{t-s}\ e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}\ u_{t}(X_{t-s}^{s,x})-\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{i}}u_{t}(x)\right)=

\displaystyle=\left(\gamma_{\nu}(s,x)\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}u_{t}}{\partial x^{k}\partial x^{i}}(x)\beta^{k}_{\nu}(s,x)+\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{j}}(x)\frac{\partial\beta^{j}_{\nu}}{\partial x^{i}}(s,x)+\frac{\partial\gamma_{\nu}}{\partial x^{i}}(s,x)u_{t}(x)\right)\delta W_{s,t}^{\nu}+S^{1}_{s,t}(x)

\displaystyle=\left(\gamma_{\nu}(t,x)\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}u_{t}}{\partial x^{k}\partial x^{i}}(x)\beta^{k}_{\nu}(t,x)+\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{j}}(x)\frac{\partial\beta^{j}_{\nu}}{\partial x^{i}}(t,x)+\frac{\partial\gamma_{\nu}}{\partial x^{i}}(t,x)u_{t}(x)\right)\delta W_{s,t}^{\nu}+S^{2}_{s,t}(x)

with
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|S^{i}_{s,t}(x)|\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\min\{(\lambda-2)\alpha,\delta,\eta\}}\ (i=1,2)
.
The previous identity hold because
D_{x}u_{t}(\cdot)\in C^{\lambda-1}_{b}
with
\lambda-1\in(1,2]
,
Y^{s,x,i}_{0}=e_{i}
and since
e^{I^{s,x}}\ D_{x}u_{t}(X^{s,x})Y^{s,x,i}+\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial e_{i}}I^{s,x}\ e^{I^{s,x}}\ u_{t}(X^{s,x})
can be proven to be stochastically controlled.
We therefore deduce that, for any arbitrary
\mu=1,\dots,n
and uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

|(\Gamma_{t})_{\kappa}u_{t}(x)-(\Gamma_{s})_{\kappa}u_{s}(x)-((\Gamma_{t})_{\kappa}(\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}-(\Gamma_{t}^{\prime})_{\mu\nu})u_{t}(x)\delta W^{\mu}_{s,t}|\lesssim|t-s|^{\min\{(\lambda-2)\alpha,\delta,\eta\}}.

Notice that, from
4.4
,
\alpha+\min\{\delta,\eta\}+\min\{(\lambda-2)\alpha,\delta,\eta\}>1
. That is, condition ii) of
4.1
is satisfied with
\alpha^{\prime}=\min\{\delta,\eta\}
and
\alpha^{\prime\prime}=\min\{(\lambda-2)\alpha,2\delta,2\eta\}
.

The final step of this proof consists in showing that
u
satisfies the Davie-type expansion stated in
4.1
.iii.
It is useful to recall that, for any
(t_{1},t_{2})\in\Delta_{[0,T]}
and for any
i=1,\dots,d
, we can write

\displaystyle(X^{s,x}_{t_{2}})^{i}

\displaystyle=(X^{s,x}_{t_{1}})^{i}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}b^{i}(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})dr+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\sigma_{\nu}^{i}(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})dB_{r}^{\nu}+\beta^{i}_{\mu}(s+t_{1},X_{t_{1}}^{s,x})(\delta W^{s}_{t_{1},t_{2}})^{\mu}+

(36)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\partial\beta_{\nu}^{i}}{\partial x^{j}}(s+t_{1},X_{t_{1}}^{s,x})\beta_{\mu}^{j}(s+t_{1},X_{t_{1}}^{s,x})+(\beta_{\nu\mu}^{\prime})^{i}(s+t_{1},X^{s,x}_{t_{1}})\right)(\mathbb{W}^{s}_{t_{1},t_{2}})^{\mu\nu}+(X^{s,x,\natural}_{t_{1},t_{2}})^{i},

with
\|X^{s,x,\natural}_{t_{1},t_{2}}\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\delta}
and
\|\mathbb{E}_{t_{1}}(X^{s,x,\natural}_{t_{1},t_{2}})\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+2\delta}
, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
.
While from
4.14
it also follows that

\displaystyle e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{2}}}-e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{1}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}c(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})e^{I_{r}^{s,x}}dr+\gamma_{\mu}(s+t_{1},X_{t_{1}}^{s,x})e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{1}}}\delta W^{\mu}_{t_{1},t_{2}}+\bigg(\frac{\partial\gamma_{\nu}}{\partial x^{j}}(s+t_{1},X_{t_{1}}^{s,x})\beta_{\mu}^{j}(s+t_{1},X_{t_{1}}^{s,x})+

(37)

\displaystyle+\gamma_{\nu\mu}^{\prime}(s+t_{1},X^{s,x}_{t_{1}})+\gamma_{\mu}(s+t_{1},X_{t_{1}}^{s,x})\gamma_{\nu}(s+t_{1},X_{t_{1}}^{s,x})\bigg)e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{1}}}\mathbb{W}^{\mu\nu}_{t_{1},t_{2}}+e^{s,x,\natural}_{t_{1},t_{2}}.

Here
\|e^{s,x,\natural}_{t_{1},t_{2}}\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\eta}
and
\|\mathbb{E}_{t_{1}}(e^{s,x,\natural}_{t_{1},t_{2}})\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\eta+\min\{\delta,\eta\}}
, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
.
Recalling that
u_{t}(\cdot)\in C^{\lambda}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and by applying
4.11
to the product process
e^{I^{s,x}_{\cdot}}u_{t}(X^{s,x}_{\cdot})
we have

\displaystyle e^{I^{s,x}_{t-s}}u_{t}(X^{s,x}_{t-s})-u_{t}(x)=

(38)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t-s}\underbrace{c(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})e^{I^{s,x}_{r}}u_{t}(X_{s}^{s,x})+e^{I^{s,x}_{r}}\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(X_{r}^{s,x})b^{i}(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})+e^{I^{s,x}_{r}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^{2}u_{t}}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{i}}(\sigma^{i}_{a}\sigma^{j}_{a})(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})}_{=e^{I^{s,x}_{r}}L_{s+r}u_{t}(X_{r}^{s,x})}\ dr+

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t-s}e^{I^{s,x}_{r}}\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(X_{r}^{s,x})\sigma^{i}_{a}(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})dB^{a}_{r}+\bigg(\gamma_{\mu}(s,x)u_{t}(x)+\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)\beta^{i}_{\mu}(s,x)\bigg)\delta(W^{s}_{0,t-s})^{\mu}+

\displaystyle+\bigg(\frac{\partial\gamma_{\nu}}{\partial x^{i}}(s,x)\beta^{i}_{\mu}(s,x)u_{t}(x)+\gamma^{\prime}_{\nu\mu}(s,x)u_{t}(x)+\gamma_{\mu}(s,x)\gamma_{\nu}(s,x)u_{t}(x)+\gamma_{\mu}(s,x)\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)\beta^{i}_{\nu}(s,x)+

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)\beta^{i}_{\mu}(s,x)\gamma_{\nu}(s,x)+\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)(\beta^{\prime}_{\nu\mu})^{i}(s,x)+\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)\frac{\partial\beta^{i}_{\nu}}{\partial x^{j}}(s,x)\beta^{j}_{\mu}(s,x)+

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial^{2}u_{t}}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{i}}(x)\beta^{i}_{\mu}(s,x)\beta^{j}_{\mu}(s,x)\bigg)(\mathbb{W}^{s}_{0,t-s})^{\mu\nu}+P^{s,x\natural}_{0,t-s},

where
\|P^{s,x,\natural}_{0,t-s}\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}
and
\|\mathbb{E}_{0}(P^{s,x,\natural}_{0,t-s})\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}+\min\{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime}\}}
, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
.
The conclusion follows by taking the expectation of the previous identity, up to slightly rearrange some terms.
Similarly as before,
|\mathbb{E}(P^{s,x,\natural}_{0,t-s})|\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}+\min\{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime}\}}
, and due to the martingale property of the Itô integral
\mathbb{E}\big(\int_{0}^{t-s}e^{I_{r}^{s,x}}\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(X_{r}^{s,x})\sigma_{a}^{i}(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})dB^{a}_{r}\big)=0
(notice that
e^{I^{s,x}_{\cdot}}\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(X_{\cdot}^{s,x})\sigma(s+\cdot,X^{s,x}_{\cdot})\in L^{2}(\Omega\times[0,T];\mathbb{R}^{m})
).
Moreover, we can write
\int_{0}^{t-s}e^{I^{s,x}_{r}}L_{s+r}u_{t}(X_{r}^{s,x})dr
as
\int_{s}^{t}e^{I^{s,x}_{r-s}}L_{r}u_{t}(X_{r-s}^{s,x})dr
and deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{s}^{t}e^{I^{s,x}_{r-s}}L_{r}u_{t}(X_{r-s}^{s,x})dr\right)-\int_{s}^{t}L_{r}u_{r}(x)dr\right|

(39)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}(e^{I^{s,x}_{r-s}}-1)L_{r}u_{t}(X_{r-s}^{s,x})dr\right|\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}L_{r}u_{t}(X_{r-s}^{s,x})-L_{r}u_{t}(x)dr\right|\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}L_{r}u_{t}(x)-L_{r}u_{r}(x)dr\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq|u_{t}(\cdot)|_{C^{2}_{b}}\left(|b|_{C^{1}_{b}}+|D_{x}\sigma|^{2}_{C^{2}_{b}}\right)\left(\|\delta e^{I^{s,x}}\|_{\alpha;1}+\|\delta X^{s,0,x}\|_{\alpha;1,\infty}+[u]_{\alpha}\right)|t-s|^{1+\alpha},

The proof is concluded since, from the assumptions on
\beta,\beta^{\prime}
and
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
,

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|(\Gamma_{s})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{s})_{\nu}u_{t}(x)\mathbb{W}_{s,t}^{\mu\nu}-(\Gamma_{t})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{t})_{\nu}u_{t}(x)\mathbb{W}_{s,t}^{\mu\nu}|\lesssim|t-s|^{2\alpha+\min\{\delta,\eta\}}.

and, being
\mathbf{W}
is weakly geometric,

\displaystyle\left(\gamma_{\mu}(s,x)u_{t}(x)+\beta^{i}_{\mu}(s,x)\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)\right)\delta W_{s,t}^{\mu}-\left(\gamma_{\mu}(t,x)u_{t}(x)+\beta^{i}_{\mu}(t,x)\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)\right)\delta W_{s,t}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle=-\left(\gamma^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}(t,x)\delta W^{\nu}_{s,t}u_{t}(x)+(\beta^{\prime}_{\mu\nu})^{i}(t,x)\delta W^{\nu}_{s,t}\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)\right)\delta W_{s,t}^{\mu}+T^{1}_{s,t}(x)=

\displaystyle=-\left(\gamma^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}(t,x)u_{t}(x)+(\beta^{\prime}_{\mu\nu})^{i}(t,x)\frac{\partial u_{t}}{\partial x^{i}}(x)\right)(\mathbb{W}_{s,t}^{\nu\mu}+\mathbb{W}^{\mu\nu}_{s,t})+T^{2}_{s,t}(x)

with
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|T^{i}_{s,t}(x)|\lesssim|t-s|^{\alpha+\min\{2\delta,2\eta\}},\ (i=1,2)
.
∎

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
g,b,\sigma,\beta,c
and
\gamma
be fixed as in
4.4
.
For any weakly geometric
\alpha
-rough path
\mathbf{W}
, denote by
u^{\mathbf{W}}=u^{\mathbf{W}}(s,x)
the solution to
-du_{t}=L_{t}u_{t}dt+\Gamma_{t}u_{t}d\mathbf{W}_{t}
defined as in (
26
).
Then the map

\mathscr{C}_{g}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})\ni\mathbf{W}\longmapsto u^{\mathbf{W}}\in C^{0,2}_{b}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})

is locally Lipschitz continuous.

Proof.

Let
R>0
and let
\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Z}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{g}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a pair of weakly geometric rough paths such that
|\mathbf{W}^{n}|_{\alpha},|\mathbf{Z}^{n}|_{\alpha}\leq R
.
Recall that

|u^{\mathbf{W}}-u^{\mathbf{Z}}|_{C^{0,2}_{b}}:=|u^{\mathbf{W}}-u^{\mathbf{Z}}|_{\infty}+|D_{x}u^{\mathbf{W}}-D_{x}u^{\mathbf{Z}}|_{\infty}+|D^{2}_{xx}u^{\mathbf{W}}-D^{2}_{xx}u^{\mathbf{Z}}|_{\infty}.

(40)

Let
(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
be fixed.
With an obvious notation, let us denote by
X^{s,x;\mathbf{W}}
the solution to equation (
25
) driven by
\mathbf{W}^{s}
and by
X^{s,x;\mathbf{Z}}
the solution to the same equation but driven by
\mathbf{Z}^{s}
.
Similarly, we define
I^{s,x;\mathbf{W}}
and
I^{s,x;\mathbf{Z}}
(see (
29
)).
It holds

\displaystyle|u^{\mathbf{W}}(s,x)-u^{\mathbf{Z}}(s,x)|

\displaystyle\leq|D_{x}g|_{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(|X^{s,x;\mathbf{W}}_{T-s}-X^{s,x;\mathbf{Z}}_{T-s}|e^{I^{s,x;\mathbf{W}}_{T-s}}\right)+|g|_{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\big|e^{I^{s,x;\mathbf{W}}_{T-s}}-e^{I^{s,x;\mathbf{Z}}_{T-s}}\big|\right).

From
2.13
we have that
\|X^{s,x;\mathbf{W}}_{T-s}-X^{s,x;\mathbf{Z}}_{T-s}\|_{p}\lesssim_{R}\rho_{\alpha}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Z})
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
and uniformly in
(s,x)
.
From the estimate in
3.28
.1 we have that
\sup_{n}\|I^{s,x;\mathbf{W}}\|_{p}<+\infty
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, and arguing as in the proof of
3.28
.2 we obtain
\|I^{s,x;\mathbf{W}}-I^{s,x;\mathbf{Z}}\|_{p}\lesssim\rho_{\alpha}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Z})
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
and for an implicit constant that depends on
R
but not on
s
nor
x
.
Arguing as in the proof of
4.13
.1, we can show that
\|e^{I^{s,x;\mathbf{W}}_{T-s}}-e^{I^{s,x;\mathbf{Z}}_{T-s}}\|_{p}\lesssim_{R}\rho_{\alpha}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Z})
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
and uniformly in
s,x
.
Thus, we conclude that

|u^{\mathbf{W}}-u^{\mathbf{Z}}|_{\infty}:=\sup_{(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u^{\mathbf{W}}(s,x)-u^{\mathbf{Z}}(s,x)|\lesssim_{R}\rho_{\alpha}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{Z}).

In the proof of
4.8
we deduce explicit expressions for higher order space-derivatives of a solution
u
. Hence we can argue as before - also using local Lipschitz estimates for linear rough SDEs contained in
2.15
- to conclude the proof of the assertion.
∎

The following version of the rough Itô formula is needed in the proof of the previous existence result and to prove uniqueness (cf.
4.12
below).
It easily follows from the rough Itô formula for functions of polynomial growth developed in
[
BCN24
, Proposition 2.16]
.
With respect to the original formulation in
[
FHL21
, Theorem 4.13]
, here the
p
-integrability of the rough Itô process
X
is not a priori fixed, and the use functions of polynomial growth (i.e. not globally bounded) allows to derive a rough product formula.

Proposition 4.11

(Rough Itô’s formula)
.

Let
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W})
be a weakly geometric
\alpha
-rough path, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2}]
, and let
B
be an
m
-dimensional Brownian motion on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
complete filtered probability space.
Let
\kappa\in(\frac{1}{\alpha},3]
and let
F\in C^{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}};\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
such that

|D_{x}F(x)|+\dots+|D_{x\dots x}^{\lfloor\kappa\rfloor}F(x)|\leq K(1+|x|^{k}),

for some
K>0
,
k\geq 0
and for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
.
Let
(\phi,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}))
be a stochastic controlled rough path for some
\gamma\in[0,\alpha],\gamma^{\prime}\in[0,1]
with
\alpha+\gamma>\frac{1}{2}
and
\alpha+\gamma+\min\{(\kappa-2)\alpha,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\}>1
, and such that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\phi_{t}\|_{p}<+\infty
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
. Let
\mu\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}})
,
\nu\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times m})
.
Let
X=(X_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
be an continuous and adapted
\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
-valued stochastic process such that

X_{t}=X_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\mu_{r}dr+\int_{0}^{t}\nu_{r}dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(\phi_{r},\phi_{r}^{\prime})d\mathbf{W}_{r}\qquad t\in[0,T].

(41)

Then

(D_{x}F(X)\phi,D_{x}F(X)\phi^{\prime}+D^{2}_{xx}F(X)(\phi,\phi))\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\min\{(\kappa-2)\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\}}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}))

(42)

and,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. and for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle F(X_{t})

\displaystyle=F(X_{0})+\int_{0}^{t}D_{x}F(X_{r})\mu_{r}+\frac{1}{2}\text{tr}\left(D^{2}_{xx}F(X_{r})\nu_{r}\nu_{r}^{\top}\right)dr+\int_{0}^{t}D_{x}F(X_{r})\nu_{r}dB_{r}+

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}(D_{x}F(X_{r})\phi,D_{x}F(X_{r})\phi^{\prime}_{r}+D^{2}_{xx}F(X_{r})(\phi_{r},\phi_{r}))d\mathbf{W}_{r}.

Equivalently, for any
s\leq t
, for any
i=1,\dots,d_{2}
and
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, it holds that

\displaystyle F^{i}(X_{t})

\displaystyle=F^{i}(X_{s})+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\frac{\partial F^{i}}{\partial x^{j}}(X_{r})\mu_{r}^{j}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{a=1}^{m}\frac{\partial^{2}F^{i}}{\partial x^{k}\partial x^{j}}(X_{r})(\nu_{r})_{a}^{j}(\nu_{r})_{a}^{k}\right)dr+\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\partial F^{i}}{\partial x^{j}}(X_{r})(\nu_{r})^{j}_{a}dB^{a}_{r}+

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial F^{i}}{\partial x^{j}}(X_{s})(\phi_{r})^{j}_{a}\delta W_{s,t}^{a}+\left(\frac{\partial F^{i}}{\partial x^{j}}(X_{s})(\phi_{r}^{\prime})^{j}_{ab}+\frac{\partial^{2}F^{i}}{\partial x^{k}\partial x^{j}}(X_{s})(\phi_{r})^{j}_{a}(\phi_{r})^{k}_{b}\right)\mathbb{W}_{s,t}^{ab}+F^{\natural,i}_{s,t},

where
\|F^{\natural,i}_{s,t}\|_{p}\lesssim_{K}|t-s|^{\alpha+\gamma}
and
\|\mathbb{E}(F^{\natural,i}_{s,t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s})\|_{p}\lesssim_{K}|t-s|^{\alpha+\gamma+\min\{(\kappa-2)\alpha,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\}}
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
.

Theorem 4.12
.

Let
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W})\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{g}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2}]
.
Let
\kappa,\lambda\in(\frac{1}{\alpha},3]
,
\theta\in(1,2]
and
\delta,\eta\in[0,\alpha]
be such that

\alpha+\min\{\delta,\eta\}>\frac{1}{2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\alpha+\min\{\delta,\eta\}+\min\{(\min\{\kappa,\lambda\}-2)\alpha,(\theta-1)\alpha,\delta,\eta\}>1.

Let
b,\sigma
be two deterministic bounded Lipschitz vector fields from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
, respectively, and let
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\in\mathscr{D}_{W}^{2\delta}C_{b}^{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times n})
be a deterministic controlled vector field.
Let
c:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be a bounded Lipschitz vector field and let
(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in\mathscr{D}_{W}^{2\eta}C^{\theta}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
.
Let
v
be a solution to
-dv_{t}=L_{t}v_{t}\,dt+\Gamma_{t}v_{t}\,d\mathbf{W}_{t}
in the sense of
4.1
, such that
v\in\mathcal{B}([0,T];C^{\lambda}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))\cap C^{\alpha^{\prime}}([0,T];C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))
for some
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,1]
.
Then, for any
(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

v(s,x)=\mathbb{E}\left(g(X_{T-s}^{s,x})e^{\int_{0}^{T-s}c(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{T-s}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}}\right),

where
(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r}):=(\gamma(s+r,X^{s,x}),D_{x}\gamma(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})\beta(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})+\gamma^{\prime}(s+r,X^{s,x}))
,
\mathbf{W}^{s}
is defined as in
4.3
and
X^{s,x}
denotes the solution of

X^{s,x}_{t}=x+\int_{0}^{t}b(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})dr+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})dB_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(\beta,\beta^{\prime})(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}

(43)

on any given complete filtered probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t},\mathbb{P})
endowed with an
m
-dimensional Brownian motion
B
.

Proof.

Notice that, under the assumptions of the theorem, equation (
43
) is well posed and it is possible to define
I^{s,x}
as in (
29
).
Let
s\in[0,T]
be fixed and, for any
(t,x)\in[s,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let us define the following auxiliary function

w_{s}(t,x):=\mathbb{E}\left(v_{t}(X_{t-s}^{s,x})e^{\int_{0}^{t-s}c(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{t-s}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}}\right).

We show that, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the mapping
[s,T]\ni t\mapsto w_{s}(t,x)
is constant.
This would prove the assertion, since

\displaystyle v_{s}(x)

\displaystyle=w_{s}(s,x)=w_{s}(T,x)=\mathbb{E}\left(g(X_{T-s}^{s,x})e^{\int_{0}^{T-s}c(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{T-s}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}}\right).

Let
(t_{1},t_{2})\in\Delta_{[s,T]}
. We can write

\displaystyle w_{s}(t_{2},x)-w_{s}(t_{1},x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(v_{t_{2}}(X_{{t_{2}}-s}^{s,x})e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{2}-s}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(v_{t_{1}}(X_{{t_{1}}-s}^{s,x})e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s}}\right)=

(44)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(v_{t_{2}}(X^{s,x}_{t_{2}-s})e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{2}-s}}-v_{t_{2}}(X^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s})e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s}}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\left(v_{t_{2}}(X^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s})-v_{t_{1}}(X^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s})\right)e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s}}\right).

We can apply
4.11
to with
F=v_{t_{2}}
in a very similar way to what we did in (
38
) (with the only difference that we work on a generic interval
[t_{1},t_{2}]
and we do not have
e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{1}}}=1
anymore) to obtain that

\displaystyle v_{t_{2}}(X^{s,x}_{t_{2}-s})e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{2}-s}}-v_{t_{2}}(X^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s})e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s}}=

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}L_{r}v_{t_{2}}(X_{r-s}^{s,x})e^{I^{s,x}_{r-s}}dr+\int_{t_{1}-s}^{t_{2}-s}\frac{\partial v_{t_{2}}}{\partial x^{j}}(X_{r}^{s,x})\sigma_{a}^{j}(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})e^{I^{s,x}_{r}}dB_{r}^{a}+(\Gamma_{t_{2}})_{\mu}v_{t_{2}}(X_{t_{1}-s}^{s,x})e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s}}\delta W^{\mu}_{t_{1},t_{2}}+

\displaystyle+((\Gamma_{t_{2}})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{t_{2}})_{\nu}-(\Gamma^{\prime}_{t_{2}})_{\mu\nu})v_{t_{2}}(X_{t_{1}-s}^{s,x})e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s}}\mathbb{W}_{t_{1},t_{2}}^{\mu\nu}+Q^{\natural}_{t_{1}-s,t_{2}-s},

where by construction
|\mathbb{E}(Q^{\natural}_{t_{1}-s,t_{2}-s})|\lesssim|t_{2}-t_{1}|^{\alpha+\min\{\delta,\eta\}+\min\{(\kappa-2)\alpha,(\lambda-2)\alpha,(\theta-1)\alpha,\delta,\eta\}}
.
Moreover, being
v
a solution to
-dv_{t}=L_{t}v_{t}\,dt+\Gamma_{t}v_{t}\,d\mathbf{W}_{t}
it follows that

\displaystyle v_{t_{2}}(X^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s})-v_{t_{1}}(X^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s})

\displaystyle=-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}L_{r}v_{r}(X_{t_{1}-s}^{s,x})dr-(\Gamma_{t_{2}})_{\mu}v_{t_{2}}(X_{t_{1}-s}^{s,x})\delta W^{\mu}_{t_{1},t_{2}}+

\displaystyle-((\Gamma_{t_{2}})_{\mu}(\Gamma_{t_{2}})_{\nu}-(\Gamma_{t}^{\prime})_{\mu\nu})v_{t_{2}}(X_{t_{1}-s}^{s,x})\mathbb{W}_{t_{1},t_{2}}^{\mu\nu}+v_{t_{1},t_{2}}^{\natural}(X^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s})\qquad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.},

and
|\mathbb{E}(v_{t_{1},t_{2}}^{\natural}(X^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s}))|\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|v^{\natural}_{t_{1},t_{2}}(x)|\lesssim|t_{s}-t_{1}|^{\alpha+\min\{\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}\}+\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
for some
\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime}
as in
4.1
.
Hence, from (
44
) and due to the martingale property of the Itô integral, it follows that

\displaystyle w_{s}(t_{2},x)-w_{s}(t_{1},x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}L_{r}v_{t_{2}}(X_{r-s}^{s,x})e^{I^{s,x}_{r-s}}-L_{r}v_{r}(X_{t_{1}-s}^{s,x})e^{I^{s,x}_{t_{1}-s}}dr\right)+w^{\natural}_{t_{1},t_{2}}

where
|w^{\natural}_{t_{1},t_{2}}|\lesssim|t_{2}-t_{1}|^{1+\varepsilon}
for some
\varepsilon>0
.
Arguing very similarly to what we did in (
39
), we can conclude that, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and for any
s\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq T
,
|v_{s}(t_{2},x)-v_{s}(t_{1},x)|\lesssim|t_{2}-t_{1}|^{1+\varepsilon}

for some
\varepsilon>0
, which implies that
v_{s}(\cdot,x)
is constant.
∎

4.1
The exponential term

The presence of the stochastic exponential term

e^{\int_{0}^{T-s}c(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{T-s}(\gamma(s+r,X^{s,x_{r}}),D_{x}\gamma(s+r,X_{r}^{s,x})\beta(s+r,X^{s,x}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}}

in the representation of the solution
u
given in (
26
) requires particular attention.
The two main tools that allow us to treat such a random variable and to prove meaningful results are the John-Nirenberg inequality proved in
[
FHL21
, Proposition 2.7]
and the mean value theorem, according to which

|e^{y}-x^{x}|\leq e^{|x|+|y|}\ |y-x|\qquad\text{for any $x,y\in\mathbb{R}$}.

Proposition 4.13
.

1.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\rho)
be a metric space and let
o\in U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be a subset.
Let
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
be a
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-continuous family of random variables such that
\sup_{\rho(\zeta,o)\leq R}\|e^{|X^{\zeta}|}\|_{p}<+\infty
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
and for any
R>0
. Then
\{e^{X^{\zeta}}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-continuous.

2.

Let
(\mathcal{U},|\cdot|)
be a normed vector space and let
U\subseteq\mathcal{U}
be a non-empty open subset.
Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
and let
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
be a family of
k
-times (
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-differentiable random variables such that
\sup_{|\zeta|\leq R}\|e^{|X^{\zeta}|}\|_{p}<+\infty
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
and for any
R>0
.
Then
\{e^{X^{\zeta}}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k
-times (
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
-differentiable.
In particular, for any
\zeta\in U
and for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
,

\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}e^{X^{\zeta}}=\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta}\right)e^{X^{\zeta}}.

Proof.

1.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
be an accumulation point, and let
(\zeta^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq U\setminus\{\zeta^{0}\}
be any sequence such that
\rho(\zeta^{n},\zeta^{0})\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
.
For any
p\in[1,\infty)
and for any
n\geq 1
, we can apply the mean value theorem to get

\displaystyle\big\|e^{X^{\zeta^{n}}}-e^{X^{\zeta^{0}}}\big\|_{p}\leq\left(\sup_{n\geq 0}\|e^{|X^{\zeta^{n}}|}\|^{2}_{4p}\right)\|X^{\zeta^{n}}-X^{\zeta^{0}}\|_{2p}.

The right-hand side goes to zero as
n\to+\infty
assumption, and the assertion is therefore proved.

2.
We argue recursively on
k
. First, assume
k=1
.
Let
\zeta^{0}\in U
and let
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
be fixed. For any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
and for any
p\in[1,\infty)
, we can apply the mean-value theorem to get that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(e^{X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}}-e^{X^{\zeta^{0}}}\right)-\left(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta^{0}}\right)e^{X^{\zeta^{0}}}\right\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left({X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}}-{X^{\zeta^{0}}}-\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta^{0}}\right)e^{X^{\zeta^{0}}}\right\|_{p}+

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{0}^{1}e^{(1-\theta)X^{\zeta^{0}}+\theta X^{\zeta^{0}}}d\theta\ \frac{1}{\varepsilon}(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-X^{\zeta^{0}})(X^{\zeta^{0}+\varepsilon l}-X^{\zeta^{0}})\right\|_{p}.

As
\varepsilon\to 0
, the first term on the right-hand side goes to 0 by the
\mathscr{L}
-differentiability assumption on
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta}
and due to the locally uniform boundedness of
e^{|X^{\zeta}|}
in
L^{p}
-norm.
The last term on the right-hand side goes to 0 as well, taking also into account the
\mathscr{L}
-continuity of
\{e^{X^{\zeta}}\}_{\zeta}
showed in
1
.
The
\mathscr{L}
-continuity of
\{(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta})e^{X^{\zeta}}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is an easy consequence of the
\mathscr{L}
-continuity of
\{X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
.

Let us now assume that the assertion is proved for
k=k_{0}
and that all the assumptions hold for
k=k_{0}+1
.
Let us show that, for any
l\in\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{U})
,
\{(\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta})e^{X^{\zeta}}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k_{0}
-times (
\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
By assumption,
\{\mathscr{L}\text{-}\frac{\partial}{\partial l}X^{\zeta}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k_{0}
-times (
\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable, while by recursion we also have that
\{e^{X^{\zeta}}\}_{\zeta\in U}
is
k_{0}
-times (
\mathscr{L}
-continuously)
\mathscr{L}
-differentiable.
Hence, to conclude it is sufficient to apply
3.20
with
g(x,y)=xy
.
∎

Proposition 4.14
.

Let
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W})\in\mathscr{C}_{g}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a geometric rough path, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2}]
.
Let
\mu\in\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
and let
(\phi,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}L_{1,\infty}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}))
, for some
\gamma\in[0,\alpha],\ \gamma^{\prime}\in[0,1]
such that
\alpha+\gamma>\frac{1}{2}
and
\alpha+\gamma+\gamma^{\prime}>1
.
Define

(I,I^{\prime}):=\left(\int_{0}^{\cdot}\mu_{r}dr+\int_{0}^{\cdot}(\phi_{r},\phi_{r}^{\prime})d\mathbf{W}_{r},\phi\right).

1.

For any
p\in[1,\infty)
,

\mathbb{E}\left(e^{p\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|I_{t}|}\right)<+\infty

In particular,
e^{I_{t}}\in L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R})
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
and uniformly in
t\in[0,T]
.

2.

(e^{I},\phi\ e^{I})
is a rough Itô process, namely
(\phi e^{I},(\phi\phi+\phi^{\prime})e^{I})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\gamma}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
and, for any
s\leq t
,

\displaystyle e^{I_{t}}-e^{I_{s}}

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\mu_{r}e^{I_{r}}dr+(\phi_{s})_{\kappa}e^{I_{s}}\delta W^{\kappa}_{s,t}+((\phi_{s})_{\kappa}(\phi_{s})_{\lambda}+(\phi^{\prime}_{s})_{\kappa\lambda})e^{I_{s}}\mathbb{W}^{\kappa,\lambda}_{s,t}+e^{\natural}_{s,t},

where
\|e^{\natural}_{s,t}\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}
and
\|\mathbb{E}_{s}(e^{\natural}_{s,t})\|_{p}\lesssim|t-s|^{1+\varepsilon}
, for some
\varepsilon>0
and for any
p\in[2,\infty)
.

Proof.

1.
Notice that, by construction,
\|\delta I\|_{\alpha;1,\infty}<+\infty
and
I
is
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. continuous, with
I_{0}=0
.
Hence, from
[
FHL21
, Proposition 2.7]
,

\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\lambda\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\delta I_{0,t}|}\right)\leq 2^{1+(22\lambda\|\delta I\|_{\alpha;1,\infty})^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}T}\qquad\text{for any $\lambda>0$}.

2.
The proof of this fact is analogous to the proof of the rough Itô formula in
4.11
.
Indeed, in that context the polynomial growth assumptions on
F
and its derivatives can be replaced by the more general assumption
F(X_{t}),D_{x}F(X_{t}),\dots,D^{\lfloor\kappa\rfloor}_{x\dots x}F(X_{t})\in L^{p}
for any
p\in[2,\infty)
and for any
t\in[0,T]
.
For sake of completeness, we highlight the key points of the argument, which essentially relies on the mean value theorem.
Indeed, for any
s\leq t
we can write

|e^{I_{t}}-e^{I_{s}}|\leq e^{|I_{t}|+|I_{s}|}(I_{t}-I_{s}).

From the fact that

\displaystyle\phi_{t}e^{I_{t}}-\phi_{s}e^{I_{s}}-(\phi_{s}\phi_{s}+\phi^{\prime}_{s})e^{I_{s}}\delta W_{s,t}

\displaystyle=\phi_{s}(e^{I_{t}}-e^{I_{s}}-\delta\phi_{s,t}e^{\phi_{s}})+\phi_{s}R^{\phi}_{s,t}e^{I_{s}}+\delta\phi_{s,t}(e^{I_{t}}-e^{I_{s}})+R^{\phi}_{s,t}e^{I_{s}}

one can easily deduce that
(\phi e^{I},(\phi\phi+\phi^{\prime})e^{I})\in\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\gamma}\mathscr{L}(\mathbb{R})
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle e^{I_{t}}-e^{I_{s}}

\displaystyle=e^{I_{s}}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\mu_{r}dr+\int_{s}^{t}(\phi_{r},\phi_{r}^{\prime})d\mathbf{W}_{r}\right)+\frac{1}{2}e^{I_{s}}\left(\int_{s}^{t}(\phi_{r},\phi_{r}^{\prime})d\mathbf{W}_{r}\right)^{2}+e^{\natural}_{s,t}

and the conclusion follows arguing as in the proof of
4.11
.
∎

4.2
Functionals of solutions to rough SDEs

According to
4.5
, the solution
u
to the linear bacward rough PDE (
21
) admits a functional integral representation in terms of the solution to a suitable rough SDE. In
4.15
we point out how such a representation does not depend on the underline Brownian motion.
In
4.18
we prove a useful identity that connects
u(s,\cdot)
with
u(t,\cdot)
.
In doing so we also state and prove some flow properties and some measurability results about solutions to RSDEs (cf.
4.16
and
Lemma

4.17
, respectively).

Let
\mathbf{W}=(W,\mathbb{W})\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a rough path, with
\alpha\in(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{2}]
, let
b:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\sigma:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
be two deterministic bounded Lipschitz vector fields, and let
(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\in\mathscr{D}_{W}^{2\delta}C_{b}^{\kappa}
be a deterministic controlled vector field, for some
\kappa\in(\frac{1}{\alpha},3]
and
\delta\in[0,\alpha]
with
\alpha+2\delta>1
.
Let
t_{0}\in[0,T]
.
Recall that a solution to the RSDE on
[t_{0},T]
starting from
\xi
, with coefficients
b,\sigma,(\beta,\beta^{\prime})
and driven by
\mathbf{W}
is a quadruple

(X,\xi,B,\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[t_{0},T]}),

where
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[t_{0},T]}
is a complete filtration on some complete probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
,
\xi
is a
\mathcal{F}_{t_{0}}
-measurable
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued random variable,
B=(B_{t})_{t\in[t_{0},T]}
is an
m
-dimensional Brownian motion on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[t_{0},T]},\mathbb{P})
and
X
is a continuous
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t}
-adapted process solving

X_{t}=\xi+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}b_{r}(X_{r})dr+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\sigma_{r}(X_{r})dB_{r}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(\beta_{r},\beta^{\prime}_{r})(X_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}

in the sense of
2.11
, where
(\beta_{r},\beta^{\prime}_{r})(X_{r}):=(\beta_{r}(X_{r}),D_{x}\beta_{r}(X_{r})\beta_{r}(X_{r})+\beta^{\prime}_{r}(X_{r}))
.
In such a case we write
X\in RSDE(\xi,B,\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[t_{0},T]})
.

Proposition 4.15
.

Let
c:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be bounded and continuous, and let
(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in\mathscr{D}_{W}^{2\eta}C_{b}^{\lambda}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, for some
\eta\in[0,\alpha]
and
\lambda\in(1,2]
such that
\alpha+\eta>\frac{1}{2}
and
\alpha+\eta+\min\{(\lambda-2)\alpha,\eta,\delta\}>1
.
Let
(X,\xi,B,\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[t_{0},T]})
and
(\bar{X},\bar{\xi},\bar{B},\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\}_{t\in[t_{0},T]})
be two solutions on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
and
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}})
, respectively, and assume
Law(\xi)=Law(\bar{\xi})
.
Then, for any non-negative measurable function
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}

8
8

8

By considering positive and negative parts, the result can be easily extended to any measurable function
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\mathbb{E}\left(\Big|g(X_{T})e^{\int_{t_{0}}^{T}c_{r}(X_{r})dr+\int_{t_{0}}^{T}(\gamma_{r},\gamma^{\prime}_{r})(X_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}}\Big|\right),\ \bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\Big|g(\bar{X}_{T})e^{\int_{t_{0}}^{T}c_{r}(\bar{X}_{r})dr+\int_{t_{0}}^{T}(\gamma_{r},\gamma^{\prime}_{r})(\bar{X}_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}}\Big|\right)<+\infty.

,

\mathbb{E}\left(g(X_{T})e^{\int_{t_{0}}^{T}c_{r}(X_{r})dr+\int_{t_{0}}^{T}(\gamma_{r},\gamma^{\prime}_{r})(X_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}}\right)=\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(g(\bar{X}_{T})e^{\int_{t_{0}}^{T}c_{r}(\bar{X}_{r})dr+\int_{t_{0}}^{T}(\gamma_{r},\gamma^{\prime}_{r})(\bar{X}_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}}\right),

where
\mathbb{E}
and
\bar{\mathbb{E}}
denote the expectation on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
and
(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}})
, respectively.

Proof.

In case
(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})=0
, the conclusion is a trivial application of the uniqueness-in-law result
[
FHL21
, Theorem 4.21]
.
The presence of the rough stochastic exponential
e^{\int_{t_{0}}^{T}(\gamma_{r},\gamma^{\prime}_{r})(X_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}}
requires a bit of analysis, since the pathwise continuity of
X
is not sufficient to define it as a random variable. Indeed, we need to observe that
(\gamma(X),D_{x}\gamma(X)\beta(X))
is a stochastic controlled rough path in
\mathbf{D}_{W}^{\alpha,\min\{\eta,\delta\}}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbb{R}))
and to take into account
[
FHL21
, Proposition 2.7]
to get exponential integrability.
For any
M\in\mathbb{N}
, define

\psi_{M}(x):=e^{x}\mathbf{1}_{x\leq M}+e^{M}\mathbf{1}_{x>M},\ x\in\mathbb{R}.

The family
\{\psi_{M}\}_{M}
is by construction an increasing sequence of non-negative continuous functions, converging to
\psi(x):=e^{x}
as
M\to+\infty
.
By monotone convergence and denoting by
\varphi:C^{0}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
the map
\varphi(y_{\cdot}):=g(y(T))e^{\int_{t_{0}}^{T}c_{r}(y(r))dr}
, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\varphi(X_{\cdot})e^{\int_{t_{0}}^{T}(\gamma_{r},\gamma_{r}^{\prime})(X_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}}\right)

\displaystyle=\lim_{M\to+\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\varphi(X_{\cdot})\ \psi_{M}\left(\int_{t_{0}}^{T}(\gamma_{r},\gamma_{r}^{\prime})(X_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}\right)\right)

Let
(\pi^{n})_{n}
be a sequence of partitions of
[t_{0},T]
with
|\pi^{n}|\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
.
Up to passing to a subsequence, we have that

\int_{t_{0}}^{T}(\gamma_{r},\gamma_{r}^{\prime})(X_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi^{n}}\gamma_{u}(X_{u})\delta W_{u,v}+(D_{x}\gamma_{u}(X_{u})\beta_{u}(X_{u})+\gamma^{\prime}_{u}(X_{u}))\mathbb{W}_{u,v}=:\lim_{n\to+\infty}f^{n}(X_{\cdot})

\mathbb{P}
-a.s., where
f^{n}:C^{0}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
is measurable and bounded.
By dominated convergence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\varphi(X_{\cdot})\ (\psi_{M}\circ f^{n})\left(X_{\cdot}\right)\right)\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}\left(\varphi(X_{\cdot})\ \psi_{M}\left(\int_{t_{0}}^{T}(\gamma_{r},\gamma_{r}^{\prime})(X_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}\right)\right)\quad\text{ as $n\to+\infty$, for any $M$.}

By
[
FHL21
, Theorem 4.21]
, we deduce that
\mathbb{E}\left(\varphi(X_{\cdot})\ (\psi_{M}\circ f^{n})\left(X_{\cdot}\right)\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(\varphi(\bar{X}_{\cdot})\ (\psi_{M}\circ f^{n})\left(\bar{X}_{\cdot}\right)\right)
for any
n,M
.
The same argument applies to
\bar{X}_{\cdot}
, and we obtain that
\mathbb{E}\left(\varphi(\bar{X}_{\cdot})\ (\psi_{M}\circ f^{n})\left(\bar{X}_{\cdot}\right)\right)
converges, as
n\to+\infty
, to
\mathbb{E}(\varphi(\bar{X}_{\cdot})\ \psi_{M}(\int_{t_{0}}^{T}(\gamma_{r},\gamma_{r}^{\prime})(\bar{X}_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}))
.
By passing the latter to the limit as
M\to+\infty
(using monotone convergence), the assertion is proved.
∎

Let
s\in[0,T]
be a shift parameter.
In a very similar way to what is done at the beginning of the proof of
4.9
, we can extend the definition of
b_{t}(\cdot),\sigma_{t}(\cdot),\beta_{t}(\cdot)
and
\beta^{\prime}_{t}(\cdot)
for
t\in[0,+\infty)
.
A solution
X
to the ”shifted” RSDE on the time interval
[t_{0},T]
and starting from
\xi
is given by a (complete) filtered probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[t_{0},T_{0}]},\mathbb{P})
endowed with a Brownian motion
B=(B_{t})_{t\in[t_{0},T]}
, a
\mathcal{F}_{t_{0}}
-measurable random variable
\xi
and by a continuous
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t}
-adapted stochastic process
X
which solves

X_{t}=\xi+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}b_{s+r}(X_{r})dr+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\sigma_{s+r}(X_{r})dB_{r}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(\beta_{s+r},\beta^{\prime}_{s+r})(X_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}\qquad t\in[t_{0},T]

in the sense of
2.11
.
Here
\mathbf{W}^{s}
is the shifted rough path defined in
4.3
.
In such a case we write
X\in RSDE(s,\xi,B,\{\mathcal{F}\}_{t\in[t_{0},T]})
.
Due to strong uniqueness of rough SDEs under our assumptions on
b,\sigma,\beta
, it is possible to prove the following two flow properties.

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
s\in[0,T]
and let
t_{0}\in[0,T]
with be fixed. Let
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[t_{0},T]},\mathbb{P})
be a complete filtered probability space, and let
B=(B_{t})_{t\in[t_{0},T]}
be an
m
-dimensional Brownian motion on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[t_{0},T]},\mathbb{P})
.
Let
\xi\in L^{0}(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{t_{0}};\mathbb{R}^{d})
and let
