X\in RSDE(s,\xi,B,\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[t_{0},T]})
.

1.

Let
t_{1}\in[t_{0},T]
and, for any
t\in[t_{1},T_{0}]
, define
Y_{t}:=X_{t}
. Then

Y\in RSDE(s,X_{t_{1}},\bar{B},\{\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\}_{t\in[t_{1},T]})
, where
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{t}:=\mathcal{F}_{t}
and
\bar{B}_{t}:=B_{t}-B_{t_{1}}
for
t\in[t_{1},T_{0}]
.

2.

For any
t\in[0,T-t_{0}]
, define
Z_{t}:=X_{t_{0}+t}
. Then

Z\in RSDE(s+t_{0},\xi,\tilde{B},\{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T-t_{0}]})
, where
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}:=\mathcal{F}_{t_{0}+t}
and
\bar{B}_{t}:=B_{t+t_{0}}-B_{t_{0}}
for
t\in[0,T_{0}-t_{0}]
.

Proof.

1.
Let
t_{1}\in[t_{0},T]
. For any
t\in[t_{1},T]
,
X_{t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable and it holds that

\displaystyle X_{t}

\displaystyle=X_{t_{1}}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t}b_{s+r}(X_{r})+\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\sigma_{s+r}(X_{r})dB^{t_{0}}_{r}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t}(\beta_{s+r},\beta^{\prime}_{s+r})(X_{r}))d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}.

The conclusion follows on noting that
\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\sigma_{s+r}(X_{r})dB^{t_{0}}_{r}=\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\sigma_{s+r}(X_{r})dB^{t_{1}}_{r}
for any
t\in[t_{1},T]
, by the properties of Itô integration.

2.
Let
t\in[0,T-t_{0}]
be arbitrary. By construction,
Z_{t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t_{0}+t}
-measurable.
Given a partition
\pi
of
[t_{0},t_{0}+t]
, we denote by
\pi^{t_{0}}
the partition of
[0,t]
obtained by applying to every point the translation map
s\mapsto s-t_{0}
. It holds that

\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}\sigma_{s+u}(X_{u})(B_{v}-B_{u})=\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi^{t_{0}}}\sigma_{s+u+t_{0}}(X_{u+t_{0}})(B_{v+t_{0}}-B_{t_{0}}-B_{u+t_{0}}+B_{t_{0}})

and

\displaystyle\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}\beta_{s+u}(X_{u})\delta W^{s}_{u,v}+(D_{x}\beta_{s+u}(X_{u})\beta_{s+u}(X_{u})+\beta^{\prime}_{s+u}(X_{u}))\mathbb{W}_{u,v}^{s}=

\displaystyle=\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi^{t_{0}}}\beta_{s+u+t_{0}}(X_{u+t_{0}})\delta W^{s}_{u+t_{0},v+t_{0}}+(D_{x}\beta_{s+u+t_{0}}(X_{u+t_{0}})\beta_{s+u+t_{0}}(X_{u+t_{0}})+\beta^{\prime}_{s+u+t_{0}}(X_{u+t_{0}}))\mathbb{W}_{u+t_{0},v+t_{0}}^{s}

Passing to the limit in probability the two previous identities, it holds that

\displaystyle Y_{t}

\displaystyle=X_{t_{0}+t}=\xi+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{0}+t}b_{s+r}(X_{r})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{0}+t}\sigma_{s+r}(X_{r})dB^{t_{0}}_{r}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{0}+t}(\beta_{s+r},\beta^{\prime}_{s+r})(X_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}=

\displaystyle=\xi+\int_{0}^{t}b_{s+t_{0}+r}(Y_{r})+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{s+t_{0}+r}(Y_{r})d\tilde{B}_{r}+\int_{0}^{t}(\beta_{s+t_{0}+r},\beta^{\prime}_{s+t_{0}+r})(Y_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{s+t_{0}}_{r}.

∎

Lemma 4.17
.

Let
s,t_{0}\in[0,T]
be fixed.
For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let
X^{x}\in RSDE(s,x,B,\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[t_{0},T]})
be a solution to the rough SDE on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
, starting from
x
.
For any
t\in[t_{0},T]
, let us define

\mathcal{F}_{t}^{t_{0}}:=\sigma(B_{r}-B_{t_{0}},r\in[t_{0},t]).

(45)

1.

For any
t\in[t_{0},T]
, define
f_{t}:\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
to be the map

f_{t}(\omega,x):=X_{t}^{x}(\omega).

Then
f_{t}
is
(\mathcal{F}_{t}^{t_{0}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
-measurable, for any
t\in[t_{0},T]
.

2.

Let
(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in\mathscr{D}_{W}^{2\eta}C_{b}^{\lambda}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
be any deterministic controlled vector field, for some
\eta\in[0,\alpha]
and
\lambda\in(1,2]
such that
\alpha+\eta>\frac{1}{2}
and
\alpha+\eta+\min\{(\lambda-2)\alpha,\eta,\delta\}>1
.
For any
t\in[t_{0},T]
, let
I^{t}:\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be the map defined as

I^{t}(\omega,x):=\left(\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(\gamma_{s+r}(X_{r}^{x}),D_{x}\gamma_{s+r}(X_{r}^{x})\beta_{s+r}(X^{x}_{r})+\gamma^{\prime}_{s+r}(X_{r}^{x}))d\mathbf{W}_{r}^{s}\right)(\omega).

Then
I^{t}
is
(\mathcal{F}_{t}^{t_{0}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}))
-measurable, for any
t\in[t_{0},T]
.

Proof.

1.
Let
s,t_{0}\in[0,T]
be fixed. Recall that
X^{x}
can always be seen as the limit of
suitable Picard-type approximations (cf.
[
FHL21
, Proof of Theorem 4.6]
).
We denote by
X^{N,x}
the
N
-th
Picard’s iteration and, for any
t\in[t_{0},T]
, we define
f^{0}_{t}(\omega,x):=X^{0,x}=x
and

\displaystyle f_{t}^{N+1}(\omega,x)

\displaystyle:=X^{N+1,x}=x+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}b_{s+r}(f^{N}_{r}(\omega,x))dr+\left(\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\sigma_{s+r}(f^{N}_{r}(\cdot,x))dB_{r}\right)(\omega)+

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(\beta_{s+r}(f^{N}(\cdot,x)),D_{x}\beta_{s+r}(f^{N}(\cdot,x))\beta_{s+r}(f^{N}(\cdot,x))+\beta^{\prime}_{s+r}(f^{N}(\cdot,x)))d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}\right)(\omega)\qquad N\geq 1

By induction on
N
, we prove that
f_{t}^{N}
is
(\mathcal{F}_{t}^{t_{0}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
-measurable, for any
t\in[t_{0},T]
.
This allows us prove the assertion, being by
construction

f_{t}(\omega,x)=\lim_{N\to+\infty}f^{N}_{t}(\omega,x).

Measurability is trivial in the case
N=0
.
Assume that, for a certain
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and for any
t\in[t_{0},T]
,
f^{N_{0}}_{t}
is
(\mathcal{F}_{t}^{t_{0}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
-measurable.
Notice that we can write

\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t}(\beta_{s+r}(f_{r}^{N_{0}}(\cdot,x)),D_{x}\beta_{s+r}(f_{r}^{N_{0}}(\cdot,x))\beta_{s+r}(f_{r}^{N_{0}}(\cdot,x))+\beta_{s+r}^{\prime}(f_{r}^{N_{0}}(\cdot,x)))d\mathbf{W}^{s}_{r}=

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{|\pi|\to 0}\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}\beta_{s+u}(f_{u}^{N_{0}}(\cdot,x))\delta W^{s}_{u,v}+(D_{x}\beta_{s+u}(f_{u}^{N_{0}}(\cdot,x))\beta_{s+u}(f_{u}^{N_{0}}(\cdot,x))+\beta_{s+u}^{\prime}(f_{u}^{N_{0}}(\cdot,x)))\mathbb{W}^{s}_{u,v},

where the limit is in probability. Similarly

\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\sigma_{s+r}(f^{N_{0}}_{r}(\cdot,x))dB_{r}

\displaystyle=\lim_{|\pi|\to 0}\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}\sigma_{s+u}(f_{u}^{N_{0}}(\cdot,x))(B_{v}-B_{u})

\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t}b_{s+r}(f^{N_{0}}_{r}(\cdot,x))dr

\displaystyle=\lim_{|\pi|\to 0}\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}b_{s+u}(f_{u}^{N_{0}}(\cdot,x))(v-u)

Hence, we conclude that that
f_{t}^{N_{0}+1}
is
(\mathcal{F}_{t}^{t_{0}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
-measurable.

2.
This fact trivially comes from the previous one.
Indeed, we
have that, for any
t\in[t_{0},T]
,
I^{t}(\omega,x)
is
approximated (in probability) by a sequence of Riemann-type sums of the
form

\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}\gamma_{s+u}(f_{u}(\omega,x))\delta W^{s}_{u,v}+(D_{x}\gamma_{s+u}\left({f_{u}(\omega,x)}\right)\beta_{s+u}(f_{u}(\omega,x))+\gamma^{\prime}_{s+u}(f_{u}(\omega,x)))\mathbb{W}^{s}_{u,v}.

∎

Proposition 4.18
.

Let
c
and
(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})
as in
4.15
.
Let
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be a bounded Borel-measurable function.
For any
(s,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
, define

u(s,x):=\mathbb{E}\left(g(X^{s,x}_{T-s})e^{\int_{0}^{T-s}c_{s+r}(X_{r}^{s,x})dr+\int_{0}^{T-s}(\gamma_{s+r},\gamma^{\prime}_{s+r})(X_{r}^{s,x})d\mathbf{W}_{r}^{s}}\right),

where
X^{s,x}\in RSDE(s,x,B,\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})
, for any given complete filtered probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]},\mathbb{P})
endowed with an
m
-dimensional Brownian motion
B=(B_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
.
Then, for any
s\leq t
,

u(s,x)=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\int_{0}^{t-s}c_{s+r}(X_{r}^{s,x})dr+\int_{0}^{t-s}(\gamma_{s+r},\gamma^{\prime}_{s+r})(X_{r}^{s,x})d\mathbf{W}_{r}^{s}}\ u(t,X^{s,x}_{t-s})\right).

Proof.

Let
s\leq t
and let
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be fixed.
For
t_{0}\in[0,T]
and
\tau\in[t_{0},T]
, define
\mathcal{F}^{t_{0}}_{\tau}:=\sigma(B_{r}-B_{t_{0}},\ r\in[t_{0},\tau])
.
Properties of the exponential function and a simple time-change in the integrals show that

\displaystyle e^{\int_{0}^{T-s}c_{s+r}(X_{r}^{s,x})dr+\int_{0}^{T-s}(\gamma_{s+r},\gamma^{\prime}_{s+r})(X_{r}^{s,x})d\mathbf{W}_{r}^{s}}=

\displaystyle=e^{\int_{0}^{t-s}c_{s+r}(X_{r}^{s,x})dr+\int_{0}^{t-s}(\gamma_{s+r},\gamma^{\prime}_{s+r})(X_{r}^{s,x})d\mathbf{W}_{r}^{s}}\cdot e^{\int_{0}^{T-t}c_{t+r}(X_{(t-s)+r}^{s,x})dr+\int_{0}^{T-t}(\gamma_{t+r},\gamma^{\prime}_{t+r})(X_{(t-s)+r}^{s,x})d\mathbf{W}_{r}^{s}}.

By
Lemma

4.17
we get that
e^{\int_{0}^{t-s}c_{s+r}(X_{r}^{s,x})dr+\int_{0}^{t-s}(\gamma_{s+r},\gamma^{\prime}_{s+r})(X_{r}^{s,x})d\mathbf{W}_{r}^{s}}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t-s}^{0}
-measurable.
By the tower property of conditional expectations, we have

\displaystyle u(s,x)=

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\int_{0}^{t-s}c_{s+r}(X_{r}^{s,x})dr+\int_{0}^{t-s}(\gamma_{s+r},\gamma^{\prime}_{s+r})(X_{r}^{s,x})d\mathbf{W}_{r}^{s}}\mathbb{E}\left(g(Y_{T-t})e^{\int_{0}^{T-t}c_{t+r}(Y_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{T-t}(\gamma_{t+r},\gamma^{\prime}_{t+r})(Y_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}^{s}}\mid\mathcal{F}^{0}_{t-s}\right)\right),

where
Y_{\tau}:=X^{s,x}_{(t-s)+\tau}
.
By combining the two results of
4.16
, we observe that

Y\in RSDE(t,X^{s,x}_{t-s},B^{\star},\{\mathcal{F}^{\star}_{\tau}\}_{\tau\in[0,T-(t-s)]}),

(46)

where
B^{\star}_{\tau}:=B_{(t-s)+\tau}-B_{(t-s)}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\star}_{\tau}:=\mathcal{F}_{(t-s)+\tau}
, for any
\tau\in[0,T-(t-s)]
.
By applying
Lemma

4.17
, it is possible to deduce that there exists a
(\mathcal{F}^{t-s}_{T-s}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
-measurable map
\varphi:\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

g(Y_{T-t})e^{\int_{0}^{T-t}c_{t+r}(Y_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{T-t}(\gamma_{t+r},\gamma^{\prime}_{t+r})(Y_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}^{s}}=\varphi(\omega,X^{s,x}_{t-s}(\omega)).

Such a map is precisely given by

\varphi(\omega,y):=g(Z^{y}_{T-t}(\omega))e^{\int_{0}^{T-t}c_{t+r}(Z_{r}^{y}(\omega))dr+(\int_{0}^{T-t}(\gamma_{t+r},\gamma^{\prime}_{t-r})(Z^{y}_{r})d\mathbf{W}^{t}_{r})(\omega)},

where
Z^{y}\in RSDE(t,y,{B}^{\star},\{\mathcal{F}^{\star}_{\tau}\}_{\tau\in[0,T-(t-s)]})
.
Recalling that
\mathcal{F}^{t-s}_{T-s}
is independent from
\mathcal{F}^{0}_{t-s}
, by the properties of conditional expectation we conclude that

\mathbb{E}\left(g(Y_{T-t})e^{\int_{0}^{T-t}c_{t+r}(Y_{r})dr+\int_{0}^{T-t}(\gamma_{t+r},\gamma^{\prime}_{t+r})(Y_{r})d\mathbf{W}_{r}^{s}}\mid\mathcal{F}^{0}_{t-s}\right)=\mathbb{E}(\varphi(\cdot,y))|_{y=X_{t-s}^{s,x}},

(47)

and the quantity on the right-hand side is
u(t,X_{t-s}^{s,x})
by definition.
∎
